## Constraints that shape everything

Darsi's users are teachers on low-cost Android phones, often shared, with expensive and patchy mobile data, limited battery and little free storage. Most technical choices below follow from four rules:

1. **Offline is normal.** The app must open and teach with no signal. The network is an optimisation, never a requirement.
2. **Light on data and battery.** There are no live Firestore listeners and no polling, with one exception: AuthProvider listens to the signed-in person's own profile, so a role or organization change reaches the phone (see Roles). Staff screens (`admin/data.ts`) read once when they open, again after their own changes, and on Refresh. The app does one read per sign-in and writes only what changed. Heavy code is lazy-loaded, sizes are shown before downloads, and nothing autoplays.
3. **Every language is a first language.** Arabic (RTL) and English are both first-class. Layout uses logical CSS properties only, and numbers follow the locale.
4. **One codebase, three targets.** The PWA is the app. Capacitor wraps the same build for Android and iOS. Only `src/platform/` knows whether it runs in a browser or a native shell.

## Stack

| Area | Choice |
|---|---|
| App | React 19.3, TypeScript 6.0, react-router 7.18, Vite 8.3 |
| PWA | vite-plugin-pwa 2.0 (Workbox `generateSW`, prompt-to-update) |
| Backend | Firebase 12.19: Auth, Cloud Firestore (persistent offline cache), Cloud Functions 2nd gen (Node 22, firebase-functions 7.4, firebase-admin 14.5), Cloud Storage, Hosting |
| Design system | `@darsi/design-system`: tokens, fonts, icons, 45 RTL-aware React components, built with tsup |
| Native | Capacitor 8.5 (`app.darsi.teachers`) |
| Admin tooling | exceljs 4.4 (lazy-loaded) for Excel/CSV import and credential sheets |
| Kits | pptxgenjs 4.0 and headless Chromium, at build time only |
| Tests | Vitest 5 (jsdom), Playwright 1.64 (desktop and Pixel 7 profiles) |

## Repository layout

```
darsi/
├─ content/                      the starting courses, not shipped with the app: units/*.json (en + ar), the
│                                generated kits/ (PDF + PPTX, committed) and media/ (videos and light lessons,
│                                gitignored); `npm run seed` publishes them to Storage. retired/ holds courses taken
│                                off the site (ai-1, still the test course)
├─ scripts/seed-content.mjs      publishes content/ like the course editor (kits and media uploaded, then publishDraft)
├─ scripts/import-packs.mjs      turns the course packs and their Lesson Studio videos into content/units, kits and media
├─ apps/web/                     the PWA
│  ├─ public/content/            placement.json (the onboarding check) only: the app ships no courses
│  ├─ public/boot.js             the page's first script: the redirect to darsiai.org, the service worker's registration,
│  │                             the "update your browser" card (index.html has no inline script of its own)
│  ├─ public/push-sw.js          the service worker's push and notification-click handlers (imported into sw.js)
│  ├─ public/offline-sw.js       keeps what a phone saved for offline across updates (imported into sw.js)
│  ├─ public/robots.txt          search engines may index the public pages, not the staff screens, /__/ or /api/
│  ├─ scripts/build-kits.mjs     generates the kits from content/units into content/kits
│  ├─ src/
│  │  ├─ App.tsx                 providers, routes, role guards, lazy staff chunks
│  │  ├─ app/                    AppBoundary (the error boundaries, lazyRetry) and its strings
│  │  ├─ screens/more.ts         the one lazy chunk of learner pages not needed at once (account screens, Me, Kit, Labs…)
│  │  ├─ legal/                  the published privacy notice and terms: reader, pages, the terms prompt
│  │  ├─ staff/                  what staff screens share: refusal words (errors.ts), InlineConfirm
│  │  ├─ firebase-config.ts      Firebase settings from env (no SDK; safe on the startup path)
│  │  ├─ fb.ts                   the only way startup code reaches Firebase: loadFirebase(), call(), storedSession()
│  │  ├─ firebase.ts             the SDK (Auth, Firestore; Functions and Storage on first use); loaded via fb.ts
│  │  ├─ auth/                   AuthProvider (user + custom claims), sign-in screens, callable API
│  │  ├─ state/                  local store (IndexedDB) and cloud sync
│  │  ├─ screens/                learner screens: Home, Lesson, Kit, Downloads, Labs, Me, Onboarding
│  │  ├─ labs/                   Labs: catalogue, loader, kit (shared lab parts) and apps/<id>/ (one folder per lab)
│  │  ├─ lesson/                 lesson engine, step views, project submission, read-aloud
│  │  ├─ admin/                  org dashboard, people, import, person page, teacher and platform admin
│  │  ├─ account/                "Your data" in Me: download my data, delete my account
│  │  ├─ results/                /results (course results for university teachers) and /super/access (the grants)
│  │  ├─ cms/                    course editor (lazy "Cms" chunk): unit list, editor, step forms, uploads, its own strings
│  │  ├─ notify/                 push notifications: push.ts (token, permission, register) and the panel in Me
│  │  ├─ org/                    join or register an organization
│  │  ├─ landing/                public home page
│  │  ├─ content/                content types, loader (built-in + published units), validator, competency frameworks
│  │  ├─ i18n/                   UI strings (en.json, ar.json) and the provider
│  │  ├─ styles/                 one stylesheet per screen area (see Design system and CSS)
│  │  └─ platform/               the only web/native-aware code: storage, file save, share
│  ├─ e2e/, tests/               Playwright end-to-end, Vitest unit tests
│  └─ capacitor.config.ts
├─ packages/design-system/       @darsi/design-system (tokens.json → CSS/TS, components, logos, fonts)
├─ functions/                    Cloud Functions (own package and lockfile)
├─ firebase/                     firestore.rules, storage.rules, firestore.indexes.json (composite indexes, switched-off
│                                single-field indexes and the TTL policies), login-site/ (the one page of login.darsiai.org)
├─ scripts/                      set-superadmin, check-css, seed-content, import-media, isolate-code, local, resummarize,
│                                migrate-invites, migrate-org-requests, demo-org, check-claims, purge-agent-sessions (.mjs);
│                                verify, check-bundle (+ bundle-budget.json), deploy, e2e-signed; local/ (stand-ins for the
│                                payment providers, DNS and a school's sign-in provider)
├─ .github/workflows/ci.yml      CI: verify, the startup budget, the rules tests, npm audit, the browser suite, and the
│                                signed-in suite (allowed to fail for now)
├─ docs/                         FIREBASE (setup, deploy), OPERATIONS (releases, switches, watching, backups), CONTENT,
│                                NATIVE, SUPER, CURRICULUM, design-system PDF
└─ firebase.json, .firebaserc    hosting (two sites: the app, and school sign-in's callback), headers, rewrites,
                                 emulators, deploy targets
```

## Commands

Run from the repository root. Node 22 is recommended and npm 11 is expected (`npx -y npm@11.13.0 install` if npm 10 fails).

| Command | What it does |
|---|---|
| `npm install` | Installs the workspaces and builds the design system |
| `npm run dev` | Dev server at http://localhost:5173, talking to the Emulator Suite (`.env.development`) |
| `npm run build` | Builds the design system and the PWA into `apps/web/dist` (uses `.env.local`, the real project) |
| `npm run preview` | Serves the production build at http://localhost:4173 with the service worker on |
| `npm test` | Unit tests (design system + app) |
| `npm run e2e` | Playwright on desktop Chrome, Pixel 7 and (tests tagged `@smoke`) an iPhone on WebKit. It type-checks, builds the app in mode `e2e` into `apps/web/dist-e2e` (the committed `.env.e2e`: demo values, nothing of the live project) and serves that on its own port, 4180 (`E2E_PORT` for another); it never reuses a server already there, so a busy port fails the run. Set `CHROMIUM_PATH` if Playwright's own Chromium isn't installed |
| `npm run e2e:signed -w @darsi/web` | The signed-in browser suite: emulators on private ports, two seeded organizations and a stand-in school provider (see [Testing](#testing)). Needs JDK 21 |
| `npm run test:rules -w @darsi/web` | The Firestore and Storage rules tests against their own emulators. Needs JDK 21 |
| `npm run verify` | Every check in one command, stopping at the first failure: type-checks (also of the tests, the e2e suite and the tool configs), the functions' build, all unit tests, the CSS rules, contrast (`--rules`, `--e2e`, `--signed` and `--bundle <dir>` add the rules tests, the browser suite, the signed-in suite and the startup budget; docs/OPERATIONS.md §1) |
| `npm run deploy -- <targets> [--yes]` | A release tied to a commit: checks, builds, prints the commands, and with `--yes` deploys in a safe order and tags the commit (docs/OPERATIONS.md §2) |
| `node scripts/check-claims.mjs` | Read-only: lists staff accounts whose claims and profile disagree, platform admins with no profile, and staff profiles with no sign-in account (`--emulator`, `--project=<id>`, `--json`). Exits 0 when all agree, 1 when it listed something, 2 when it could not run. Run it before deploying rules |
| `npm run typecheck` | TypeScript across the workspace |
| `npm run lint:css` | Checks every app stylesheet against the RTL, token and old-WebView rules |
| `npm run kits` | Regenerates the classroom kits in `content/kits` from `content/units` |
| `node scripts/import-packs.mjs --packs <dir> --studio ../lessonstudio` | Imports the unzipped course packs as units (English), with their videos and light lessons from Lesson Studio in `content/media`. An existing unit file is left alone unless `--force`; `--media-only` refreshes only the video fields and files |
| `npm run seed -- --emulator` | Publishes the course sources (`content/units`) to the emulators; without `--emulator`, to the real project (Application Default Credentials, as for `superadmin`). Name the units (`npm run seed -- <id>…`); with no ids it takes every file there. An existing draft is left alone unless `--replace`. **On the live project, never `--replace` without ids:** it would republish all 96 sources, including the courses deliberately kept off the site. `--unpublish <id>…` takes units off the site |
| `npm run superadmin -- <email>` | Makes an account a platform admin (`--revoke`, `--emulator`) |
| `npm --prefix functions run build` / `test` | Builds or tests the Cloud Functions |
| `npm run cap:android` / `cap:ios` | Creates and opens the native projects |

## App architecture

### Provider tree

```
AuthProvider            Firebase user + custom claims {role, orgId}; claims are read before the user is published
└─ StoreProvider        local state, keyed by uid (or "guest"); switching accounts remounts everything below
   └─ I18nProvider      language from the store
      └─ SettingsProvider   tracks and languages platform admins set up (built-ins first, then content/settings.json);
         │                 fetches the picked language's strings (App waits for them)
         └─ BrowserRouter
            └─ Routed   routes, guards, useCloudSync(uid)
```

- **Firebase loads only when needed.** At startup, `storedSession()` (`fb.ts`) reads Firebase Auth's own persisted session from IndexedDB (or localStorage) without the SDK:
  - **A session is stored:** AuthProvider loads the SDK at once and subscribes to auth state.
  - **None is stored (a guest or new visitor):** nothing loads.
  - **The account screens** call `ensureFirebase()` when they open: the form shows immediately and the SDK arrives while people type. The Google button waits for it, because browsers only allow the sign-in popup straight from a tap. Where the popup can't open (blocked, `operation-not-supported-in-this-environment`) it goes to Google's page with `signInWithRedirect` and comes back to the same address, `?code=` and `?org=1` included; the auth domain is the site's own, so that stays first-party. In-app browsers (Facebook, Instagram, WhatsApp, Telegram, Android WebViews; `inAppBrowser` in `auth/AuthScreens.tsx`), which Google refuses, get "open this page in your phone's browser" with the link to copy instead.
  - **After a sign-in the account screens never navigate.** AuthProvider publishes the account (claims first), the store and router remount at the same address, and `SignedIn` (App.tsx) sends each role on. A `nav()` from the screen ran before that and landed admins in the learner's onboarding (fixed 2026-11-06; `tests/app-routes.test.tsx`).
  - **The SDK can't load** (offline, and its chunk isn't cached yet): AuthProvider sets `offlineUid` from the stored session. That person keeps learning in their own local store and reconnects on the next `online` event.
  - **The rule for new code:** never import `firebase/*` or `./firebase` statically on the guest path. Go through `loadFirebase()`, or keep the module in a lazy chunk. The e2e test "guests never download the Firebase SDK" guards this.
- **One local store per person on the device.** The keys are `darsi:state:<uid>` for accounts and `darsi:state` for guests.
  - An account used on this device for the first time starts from the guest's settings.
  - An account takes over the guest's learning only at the sign-in that created it on this device (sign-up or Google: creation and first sign-in within seconds, less than 10 minutes ago), so "your progress stays on this device until you create an account" holds true. Accounts an organization made never do (they carry an `orgId` claim from the start, and their first sign-in comes later): on a shared phone or lab PC the previous guest's lessons, notes and name must not land in a pupil's school record (`adoptsGuest` in `state/store.tsx`).
  - **Signing out** (`AuthProvider.signOut`, every Sign out button) leaves nothing of the person for the next one: after `pushSignOut` it waits up to 5 s for unsent writes (`waitForPendingWrites`), signs out, terminates Firestore and deletes its cache (`clearIndexedDbPersistence`: an admin's member lists and invite codes, notes), forgets cached org files, and reloads the page as a guest. When everything was sent, the account's local copy keeps its settings, onboarding answers and certificates and forgets progress, notes, weeks, name and organization (`forgetAccount`); sync, the profile and `useOrgBrand` bring them back at the next sign-in. With unsent work it keeps them, and says so after the reload: offline or on a slow connection, with the unsent mark set (below), or with a project draft that has words in it and was never sent. Sync sends the lessons and notes at the next sign-in here; the draft waits there for the person to send it. A cache delete that didn't finish (`darsi:purgeFs`) is finished when an account screen next opens on a signed-out device, before anything reads Firestore.
- **Guests:** anyone who chose "Continue without an account", or who used Darsi before accounts existed. Guests get the whole learning experience; only cloud features need an account.

### Routes and roles

| Path | Who | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `/` | everyone | Landing page for new visitors; otherwise redirects to the role's home (`/welcome` redirects here) |
| `/signin`, `/signup`, `/forgot` | signed out | A "not configured" notice when the build has no Firebase config. Forgot sends Firebase's reset email in the page's language with a continue link to this address's `/signin` (refused for an address Auth doesn't list: then the plain link), always answers the same (no hint whether the account exists), and says to look in Spam and Promotions, to use Google for a Google account, and can send again |
| `/learn`, `/lesson/:unit/:lesson`, `/downloads`, `/labs`, `/labs/:id`, `/kit`, `/tracks`, `/me` | signed in or guest | Behind the onboarding gate (language, "Who are you?" (teacher or student), where a teacher teaches or a student studies, tracks, weekly goal, placement). `/tracks` is My tracks. `/lab` (the old AI lab, linked from sent notifications) redirects to `/labs` |
| `/org` | signed in | Join with an invite code, or request a new organization (`?request=1`: the request form comes first). A guest gets "Sign in first" from App itself, never the organization chunk (it reads through the SDK); an invite code goes on to `/signin?code=` |
| `/me#projects` | signed in | Me's "My projects" (`screens/MyProjects.tsx`, shown only with an account): each project's status and the reviewer's feedback; review notifications open it |
| `/tutor` | signed in or guest | The tutor's panel as a page (`?unit=…&lesson=…`); people without Darsi Super see what it is (see [Darsi Super](#darsi-super)) |
| `/upgrade` | signed in or guest | The Darsi Super screen: plans, packs and seats, read with a plain `fetch` (a lazy chunk, no Firebase) |
| `/results` | signed in | Course results: a university teacher's read-only view of the courses a platform admin gave them on `/super/access` (`courseAccess/{uid}`); the `courseResults` callable reads them, limited to the grant's courses, organizations and end date (a grant that has ended says so), and without a grant the page tells a university teacher how they get one, and sends anyone else (a school's teacher, admin or student: `mayHaveResults` in `results/who.ts`) back to Me. Guests are sent to sign in without loading it (it reads through the SDK) |
| `/licences` | everyone | Open-source licences: every third-party component that reaches phones, with its copyright and licence, and the licence texts (`landing/licences.json`, written by `apps/web/scripts/licences.mjs`; a lazy chunk, not precached). Linked from Me and the home page's footer |
| `/privacy`, `/terms` | everyone | The privacy notice and the terms of use a platform admin published (`legal/Legal.tsx`, a lazy chunk, not precached; see [Retention and legal pages](#retention-and-legal-pages)). Until something is published there is no link to them anywhere |
| `/signin?school=<slug>`, `/sso-test` | signed out | A school's own sign-in, and the page a student's test of it returns to (see [School sign-in](#school-sign-in)) |
| `/admin`, `/admin/people/:uid`, `/admin/add`, `/admin/import` | `orgAdmin` | Organization dashboard, person page, add people, Excel/CSV import |
| `/admin/analytics` | `orgAdmin` | The organization's learning report: students and teachers against UNESCO, DigComp, ISTE and CSTA (see [Organization analytics](#organization-analytics)) |
| `/admin/courses` | `orgAdmin` | Which courses the organization's teachers, students and classes see ("Courses" in the nav and on the dashboard; see [Course visibility in organizations](#course-visibility-in-organizations)). Platform admins have the same panel on `/super/orgs/:orgId` |
| `/teach`, `/teach/:uid`, `/teach/analytics` | `teacher`, `orgAdmin` | The teacher's assigned students, and the learning report for them |
| `/super` | `superadmin` | Send announcements; approve or reject organization requests; the audit log and the payments ledger (`admin/Ledger.tsx`: 50 at a time, read only when a list is opened) |
| `/super/access` | `superadmin` | Course access for university teachers: which courses each one may see on `/results` (`courseAccess/{uid}`, `results/CourseAccess.tsx`) |
| `/super/plans` | `superadmin` | Darsi Super's switch, plans and economics, and the capstone AI-review limits (`admin/CapstoneSettings.tsx`); in the SuperAdmin chunk |
| `/super/projects`, `/super/certificates` | `superadmin` | Every project submitted, to review, and every certificate earned (see [Platform administration](#platform-administration)) |
| `/super/orgs/:orgId/report` | `superadmin` | One organization's learning report (see [Organization analytics](#organization-analytics)) |
| `/super/content`, `/super/content/:unitId` | `superadmin` | Course editor: units published for everyone (see [Authored content](#authored-content)) |
| `/admin/content`, `/admin/content/:unitId` | `orgAdmin` | Course editor: units published for this organization's members only |
| `/super/tracks` | `superadmin` | Tracks and levels for every unit (see [Tracks and levels](#tracks-and-levels)) |
| `/super/languages`, `/super/languages/:code` | `superadmin` | Languages beyond English and Arabic: add, translate, publish (see [Languages and right-to-left](#languages-and-right-to-left)) |
| `/super/orgs` | `superadmin` | Every organization; create one (see [Platform administration](#platform-administration)) |
| `/super/people` | `superadmin` | All users ("All users" in the nav rail): every account, most recently active first, 50 at a time with "Show 50 more", or found by name; each opens `/super/people/:uid` |
| `/super/analytics`, `/super/orgs/:orgId`, `/super/people/:uid` | `superadmin` | Platform analytics: every account and organization, usage from phones, the Google Analytics connection (see [Analytics](#analytics)) |
| `/super/bugs` | `superadmin` | Problem reports from "Report a problem" (see [Problem reports](#problem-reports)) |

Roles are `learner` (independent), `student`, `teacher`, `orgAdmin` and `superadmin`. They live in **Auth custom claims** {role, orgId, and with Darsi Super plan and planUntil}, which only Cloud Functions set, and are mirrored to `users/{uid}` for the UI. When `users/{uid}` changes role or organization, AuthProvider refreshes the token so the security rules see the new claims.

After sign-in, `homeFor(role)` sends org admins to `/admin` and platform admins to `/super`; everyone else goes to `/learn`, and an invite code (`?code=`, carried by "I already have an account" too) to `/org`, which asks before it joins with it (see [Invite codes](#invite-codes)). Staff routes skip the onboarding gate, and so do platform admins on the learner screens (every course in every track, in the language they use). Teachers and org admins going through onboarding have a link on each step to their own page ("See my students", "Manage your organization"), since there is no navigation until it is done. Signed in, onboarding is the account's: its answers live on `users/{uid}` (see [Cloud sync](#cloud-sync)), so a new device waits a moment for the profile (`profileReady` from AuthProvider) and skips onboarding if the account has done it. Onboarding shows in place of the learning page that was asked for and never navigates when it is done, so that page (a lesson link, a project to review) then opens at its address. Accounts created by an admin with `mustChangePassword` see the set-password screen before anything else; it refuses the password the organization gave out (signing in again with the typed one succeeds only if it is the same), since `passwordChanged` can't compare passwords. A self-registered profile that a function created first (joining from an invite link on a slow connection, through `setRole`) still gets the typed name and language: `createLearnerProfile` falls back to an edit of the person's own profile, and the Google button treats a profile without an email as new.

The window title names the page (`PageTitle` in App.tsx, from the nav item: "Labs — Darsi", «المختبرات — درسي», the organization's name for its members); a screen may set a closer one in its own effect. `index.html` carries the description and the link-preview tags (Open Graph and `twitter:card`, with the 512 px app icon) that WhatsApp and invite links show, and `public/robots.txt` lets search engines index the public pages but not `/super`, `/admin`, `/teach`, `/org`, `/results`, `/__/` or `/api/`. Moving to another page in the app puts focus on its `h1` (Layout; never on the first load), each onboarding step and placement question takes focus from the button that went with the last one, and the tour gives focus back when it ends.

### Layout

At 900 px and wider, a nav rail shows the five learner tabs plus the role's staff links. On phones, the design system's bottom nav keeps five tabs (Learn, Downloads, Labs, Kit, Me), and the staff links live in Me's account section. Students have no Kit tab: the classroom kits are for teaching. Staff screens and the landing page are **lazy chunks**, so a learner's phone never downloads or parses them. The long staff pages are tabbed, with the open tab in the address (`?tab=`; see [Staff screens and tabs](#staff-screens-and-tabs)).

### When something fails

- **Error boundaries** (`app/AppBoundary.tsx`, startup code with no Firebase and no `import()` of its own): a failed chunk or a render error never blanks the app.
  - `RouteBoundary` sits around the page inside the layout (the nav, the update toast and the install prompt stay), around the routes outside it (a lesson, onboarding, the account screens, the legal pages), and around the tutor's panel (the lesson carries on and the tutor's button comes back).
  - For a chunk that did not load it asks whether the site answers (`siteAnswers`: a HEAD of `/sw.js`): online, this copy is out of date and it says "Darsi was updated. Reload to carry on."; otherwise "This isn't on your phone yet…" with Retry. Any other error: "Something went wrong on this page", with Retry, Reload and "Report a problem".
  - It clears when the address is no longer the one the failure happened at, so one broken page never blocks the others.
  - `AppBoundary` is around everything under `AuthProvider`, for what no page boundary caught. Its card uses no store, no i18n and no router: its words come straight from `app/strings/*.json`, in English or Arabic by `<html lang>`. It has Reload and "Report a problem", which loads the report sheet's chunk itself and sends the report as the signed-in person.
  - Nothing reloads by itself: Reload is always a button, so two failures in a row can't loop.
  - Every lazy screen in `App.tsx` is made with `lazyRetry`, not `React.lazy`, which keeps a rejected import for good: Retry, a new address and a new boundary really ask for the chunk again.
  - Both boundaries, the labs' own boundary and Vite's `vite:preloadError` tell the window `darsi:app-error` (once per error), which the error counts listen to (see [Analytics](#analytics)).
- **Roles survive being offline.** The role and organization come from the ID token, which needs the network to be read again. Every successful read keeps `{role, orgId, plan}` in localStorage (`darsi:claims:<uid>`, for that account only), and a failed read uses the kept copy, so a school account offline keeps its organization's courses and look. `useAuth().claimsKnown` says whether the claims came from a token in this page; `useOrgBrand` clears the organization from the store only then. Only the screen depends on the copy: the rules and callables read the real token. The claims are read again on the `online` event and when the profile listener hears from the server.
- **A signed-in start never waits on a hanging connection.** With a session stored on the device, a 2.5 s timer (`SLOW_MS`, the catalog's own limit) runs beside the SDK load. When it fires before the SDK has published the account and this device keeps a copy of the account's claims, the app starts as that account from its own local store (`offlineUid`, with the kept claims once the SDK is on the page), and the account arrives later without a remount. With no kept copy (the first start after an update, or a private window) the first screen waits for the SDK, so a staff account is never published as a learner; it waits as long as the SDK neither loads nor fails. `useAuth().connecting` is true while the account is on its way: `/results` and other signed-in-only pages, and `/org`, show the loading screen meanwhile instead of sending the person to `/learn` or the guest page. Its organization arrives with it: `configureContent` then has the screens that already asked for the catalog read it again behind what they show (as after `forgetCatalog`), so the organization's courses appear on Home without leaving it, and a lesson screen that opened before the account and shows "not found" for one of them tries once more when the account is there (once per screen, never while a lesson plays). A kept staff role shows the loading screen, not a staff page whose reads would hang, until the account arrives.
- **A session the server ended** (a password reset, a suspension, a removal, a demotion, or the Auth SDK dropping the session at start for a reason other than the network) cleans the device as Sign out does: Firestore's cache, the kept claims and the organization's cached files go, and the page restarts as a guest. The account's local store, lessons in progress and drafts go too, **unless the account has unsent work**. The unsent mark is `darsi:unsent:<uid>` in localStorage (`state/store.tsx`): set when an account finishes a lesson or saves a note, and when a new account takes over a guest's lessons or notes on this device (sync holds those back until its first server read, so they are in no queue), and cleared by cloud sync only once the server copy has been read, every lesson and note write of the session has been acknowledged, and none was refused. With the mark set (or a project draft with words that was never sent) the work stays on the device for that account alone, ready for its next sign-in here: sync sends kept lessons and notes then, and a kept draft waits in its form for the person to send it. A draft counts as unsent when it has words and was never sent, or was changed after it was sent (`editedAt` later than `sentAt`; "Send again" without a change doesn't count). A one-time notice says which happened ("You were signed out. Your unsent work is kept on this phone, ready for when you sign in again." or "…Sign in again to carry on."). A school session that ended while the app was closed goes to the school's sign-in, not the guest home page. Sign out keeps the work the same way, a typed draft that was never sent included (the project form says it is kept on this phone until it is sent); the device then keeps that account's whole local copy, and the notice repeats at every sign-out, until the draft is sent or emptied. Another tab's Sign out (`darsi:signedOut`) and a clean-up already done in this tab (`darsi:ended`) are told apart from an ended session, so nothing is cleaned twice or in a loop.
- **`lastActive` is written once a day** per person and device (when the profile first arrives in a page load and its value isn't today's), not on every start: each write wakes a function.
- **The local store never takes a failed read for "nothing stored"** (`readStore` in `state/store.tsx`): when the read fails, or what is there isn't a store this version reads, nothing is saved and a card takes the app's place, in English and Arabic at once: "Your saved progress couldn't be opened… Nothing was changed or deleted", with Try again, and after a failed retry "Carry on without saving" (nothing read, nothing written). What was read is made safe before it is taken in (`sane`). A save that fails because the phone is full is said once per page load, and the browser is asked to keep the site's storage after the first new lesson finished in a page load.
- **Project drafts belong to one person:** `darsi-project-draft:<uid or guest>:<lessonId>:<stepId>` in localStorage. The next person on a shared device opens the step empty; deleting the account clears that account's drafts, and so does signing out once nothing is unsent (a draft with words in it that was never sent counts as unsent: the account's copy, that draft included, then stays on the device for its next sign-in here); "Reset progress on this device" leaves them. A guest who makes an account on this device takes their drafts along (`adoptDrafts` in `state/store.tsx` spells the key out itself: importing it from `lesson/engine` cut that module out of the startup file; `tests/drafts.test.tsx` holds the two spellings together). A draft from before the scopes is settled the first time its step is read: one that was sent goes to the account that sent it and is deleted for anyone else; one that was never sent names nobody and goes to whoever reads the step first, signed in or not, as the app did before scopes (deleting it would lose a signed-in person's typed work).
- **An app left open stays current.** When the app comes back to the front or the connection returns, and the catalog is older than 10 minutes (`STALE_MS` in `content/load.ts`), the catalog and `content/settings.json` are read again (`forgetCatalog`, `forgetSettings`); screens that read the catalog with `useAsync(loadCatalog, [])` run again by themselves, keeping what is on screen until the new list answers (Home, the organization's chosen courses in `content/mine.ts`, the admin's course grid). `/results` and `/super/access` don't import `content/load` (it would split startup): App hands them `subscribeCatalog` in `CONTENT`, and `useCatalog` (`results/async.ts`) reads again when it fires, keeping the old list meanwhile and for good if the re-read fails. No timer.
- **The terms prompt** (`legal/TermsPrompt.tsx`, in the layout, for signed-in people only, never in a lesson or onboarding): when a published privacy notice or terms is newer than what `users/{uid}.terms` records, a small banner links to it and offers "I agree" (`acceptTerms`). Nothing is published until a platform admin does it, and then nothing is asked.
- **"Choose your own password"** shows only for an account that has a password (`providerData`): one that signs in with Google or through its school goes to its home.
- **After leaving an organization** the session ends (the claims change revokes it), so the app restarts as a guest: `auth/LeftOrg.tsx` keeps a note for the tab (`sessionStorage` `darsi:leftOrg`, 15 minutes, written before the call and taken back on a refusal), and the home page, the sign-in screen and Me then say "You left <organization>. Sign in again to carry on: your learning is with your account." An ended session otherwise says one of two things once (`darsi:endedNote`, `KeptNotice` in App): that unsent work is kept on this phone, or to sign in again.
- **Sign-up behind one address:** Firebase limits new accounts per address, which a school on one connection reaches in a lesson; sign-up then says to try again in an hour or ask the organization's admin to add you (sign-in keeps "Wait a minute and try again"). Where the Google popup can't open, the versions of the legal pages a person agreed to make the trip to Google's page in `sessionStorage` (`darsi:googleAgreed`).
- **Errors that aren't counted:** the browser's "ResizeObserver loop…" notice stays in the report log and is not an `app_error`.

## Offline and caching

The service worker (Workbox `generateSW`) installs when a person enters the app. Visitors who only read the landing page never download the offline bundle.

| What | Strategy |
|---|---|
| App shell, learner screens, the light-lesson player, the labs kit, icons, `placement.json`, `labs-manifest.json` and `offline-manifest.json` | **Precached**: 42 files, about 1.3 MB (418 KB gzipped; measured 2026-11-07, see [Performance budget](#performance-budget)). Workbox's list names 50 entries: the app icons and favicon (`includeAssets`) and the web manifest (the plugin's own) are listed a second time by `globPatterns`, with the same revision, so each is stored once |
| Fonts | **Not precached.** Every face has a `unicode-range` (`tokens.json` `type.subsets`), so a page downloads only the scripts it shows. `darsi-fonts` keeps them: cache-first, `ignoreVary`, and only `font/woff2` responses, because Hosting answers a missing path with `index.html`. `platform/fonts.ts` fills it with the page language's faces after load, on every language change and on reconnect: Latin (~76 KB) for English, plus Arabic (~131 KB) for Arabic. Arabic words on a page in another language, such as the language picker or the Arabic brand name, use the phone's own font (`.dr-lang-native`, `.dr-other-script`), so English pages never download Arabic fonts |
| The Firebase SDK (`firebase-*`, including `firebase-extra`: Functions, Storage, Messaging), staff chunks, first-visit screens (landing page, `/upgrade`, the tutor, Report a problem, the tour, `/licences`), exceljs, and their CSS | **Not precached**; `darsi-chunks` caches them cache-first on first use (hashed file names). These are left out by name (`LAZY_SCREENS` and `ON_DEMAND` in `vite.config.ts`); shared chunks the bundler names after one of their modules (`strings`, `data`…) are found at build time instead (`staffOnly`: every module under `src/admin`, `staff`, `results`, `cms` or `org`, and not loaded at startup; stylesheets don't count when it decides, and a stylesheet only such chunks use goes with them) and dropped in `manifestTransforms` |
| Lab apps (`lab-<id>-[hash].js`: 109 apps in 111 chunks, with the shared `lab-agents` and `lab-tiny`; about 1.1 MB, 481 KB gzipped) | **Not precached**; `darsi-labs` caches each cache-first when it is first opened. "Save all labs" on the Labs screen (its size comes from `labs-manifest.json`, written at build time: every lab's on-demand files, shared ones once, gzipped, so what goes over the wire) and a downloaded course pin theirs in `darsi-saved` instead (below). The labs kit (shared chunks such as `LabShell`, `Plot`, `controls`) and its stylesheet are small and precached. See [Labs](#labs) |
| What downloads and "Save all labs" need beyond the precache: the code steps (`CodeSteps`, `sandbox`), a course's labs, its light lessons' packs | **Pinned** in `darsi-saved` (`platform/offline.ts`), by group (`code`, `lab:<id>`, `pack:<id>`, `labs` for every lab; the list is `/__darsi/pins.json` in that cache). `offline-manifest.json` (precached, written at build time) lists each group's files. The `darsi-labs` and `darsi-chunks` routes answer from `darsi-saved` when they have no copy or only an expired one, and nothing ages it out. Chunk names change with every build, so `public/offline-sw.js` (imported into `sw.js` like `push-sw.js`) fetches the new version's files for the pinned groups at install, before the update can take over; without a connection the install fails and is retried later, so a phone that goes offline right after an update keeps working. Any other failure (a full phone) never holds the update back: it goes ahead with what fitted, and Downloads offers "Download again". At activate it removes the previous version's files, unless it couldn't read the new list (a Wi-Fi sign-in page answering), and neither side ever stores a web page as a chunk. Phones on the Chrome 61 build keep every legacy file they have used the same way (`offline-legacy.json`). Downloads checks the pins once per version (`useOfflineRefresh`), and Remove unpins what no other download needs. A code exercise that still can't load offers Retry and "Skip this exercise" |
| `push-sw.js` (push handlers, ~3 KB) and `offline-sw.js` | **Imported** into `sw.js` with `importScripts`, so the browser stores them with the worker; not precached. Their URLs carry a content hash (`?v=…`), so changing one changes `sw.js` and triggers the normal update prompt |
| `/content/placement.json` | **Precached** (312 KB, 72 KB gzipped: the largest file after the entry chunk), so it changes only with a new version of the app. The `/content/` stale-while-revalidate rule (`darsi-content`) is still registered, but the precache answers first and no other shipped file is under `/content/` |
| `/kits/*` | Cache-first (`darsi-kits`). No longer shipped: only phones that downloaded the old built-in kits still have entries, which "Remove" clears |
| `/media/*` | Cache-first with range requests (`darsi-media`), stored only by a download (as uploaded media below) |
| `/lib/*` (Lesson Studio's shared library for light lessons: the cast, extra icons, the mark; ~56 KB before compression) | **Not precached**; cache-first in `darsi-lib` when a light lesson first plays or a unit with one is downloaded. Versioned (`/lib/v1/`), immutable on Hosting, and "Remove" leaves it for the other units |
| `/lib/python/v1/`, `/lib/sql/v1/` and Darsi Tiny (`/lib/tinylm/app/{en,ar}/v2/`, `/lib/tinylm/scene/{en,ar}/v2/`; `/lib/tinylm/v1/` fallback) | **Not precached**; cache-first in `darsi-lib` on first use. A phone fetches only the job and language it opens (~2.8 MB compressed weights, once). Versioned and immutable; none of the four models is in the install bundle |
| Courses (Cloud Storage `…/catalog.json`, `…/units/{id}/v{n}.json`) | Kept by `content/load.ts` itself in `darsi-content`, not by a service-worker rule: catalogs network-first, with the last copy when the network fails or takes over 2.5 s (`SLOW_MS`; the network's copy is stored when it arrives); unit files cache-first (a version's path never changes). A course republished while the phone was away opens offline from the newest version saved. Storing a new version deletes the older ones, except the version a download kept (`setKeptVersions`, registered by `useOfflineRefresh`). See [Authored content](#authored-content) |
| Uploaded media (Cloud Storage videos, captions, kit files, light-lesson audio) | Cache-first with range requests (`darsi-media`) once a unit is downloaded; matched by the Storage path (`/v0/b/<bucket>/o/…`) on any host, so the emulator on the local stack behaves as live. Only the download stores them (`cacheUrls`, which skips files it already has) and only Remove deletes them (every version and language under the course's media folder): the worker's route has no entry cap and never writes, so a fourth download can't push out the first one's files, and streaming or opening a kit without a download stores nothing. A light lesson's JSON (manifest, scenes, timing) is kept by `loadStoredJSON` in `darsi-content`, cache-first |
| Firestore data | The SDK's persistent IndexedDB cache, capped at **10 MB** (the default is 40 MB) |

- **Updates** use prompt mode: a "Reload" toast appears on app screens, never in the middle of a lesson.
- **Downloads** (`screens/Downloads.tsx`) record each course's catalog version and language (`AppState.downloads`). A course whose version or language differs offers "Update", one with files missing "Download again" (checked against Cache Storage when the screen opens); either fetches only what is missing, and an update then deletes the files only the old download used. A saved course with a Darsi Tiny lab (`LAB_MODEL` in `labs/loader.tsx`) offers "Save Darsi Tiny (2.8 MB)" under its row while the model isn't on the phone (its size, read from the model's manifest, is not in the course's); it goes to `darsi-lib`, once for every course, and Remove leaves it. The first Download tap asks the browser to keep the site's storage (`persistStorage`); a download that wouldn't fit, or fills the phone, says "Not enough space on this phone" and removes what it stored. With Data saver on mobile data (a cellular connection, or Save-Data when the browser reports no connection type) it asks "Download anyway" first; "Save all labs" asks the same way.
- **Two tabs or windows** of the app on one phone share the store: after each save the store touches `darsi:saved:<key>` in localStorage, and the other tab reads the store again (also when it comes back into view) and takes it in with `syncTabs`, keeping the lessons, notes, labs and certificates only it has. A reset in the other tab ("Reset progress on this device", a newer `resetAt`) is taken as it is. An account signed out in one tab (`forgetAccount`) leaves `darsi:gone:<key>`, so another tab still open as it never writes its copy back; signing in again clears it.
- **"Reset progress on this device"** clears the learning (lessons, weeks, notes, levels, finished labs, certificates) and keeps the settings (a switched-off statistics choice stays off), the onboarding answers and the downloads.
- **The report sheet** ("Report a problem") is a `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()`, so the page behind it is inert; closing it returns the focus to whatever opened it.
- **`/__/` is Hosting's own** (`navigateFallbackDenylist`). The auth domain is the site's own address, so Google sign-in opens `/__/auth/handler` in its popup; answering that with the app made the popup show "Page not found" and sign-in never finished (fixed 2026-10-28; the e2e test "the service worker leaves Firebase's own pages" guards it). Phones still running an older worker get the fix once they take the update.
- **Hosting headers:** `sw.js`, `boot.js` and `index.html` are `no-cache`; hashed assets are cached for a year. `/api/**` (analytics, problem reports, CSP reports, school sign-in) is the functions': the service worker never answers it with the app (`navigateFallbackDenylist`).
- **The MP4 player** (design system `VideoPlayer`): custom controls drawn on the picture, in the shape people know from YouTube and in Darsi's colours. A thin progress bar thickens on hover and shows the buffered range, chapter ticks, question marks and a time preview. Under it: play, a volume slider that slides out, and the clock; on the other side, captions, a settings menu (playback speed and the caption language, taken from the VTT tracks) and full screen. A large play button sits in the middle until the video is playing, and a spinner shows while it buffers. The controls hide after about three seconds of playback and come back on hover, tap or keyboard focus. Keys: Space and K play or pause, J and L jump 10 seconds, the arrows jump 5 seconds or change the volume, F is full screen, C captions, M mute, and 0–9 jump through the video. On a phone, a double tap on the left or right of the picture does the 10-second jump. The timeline and the clock stay left to right in Arabic; the buttons follow the page. Captions are drawn by the player so they sit above the controls while those are visible. Full screen uses the Fullscreen API on the player, so the question and the captions stay on top. iPhone Chrome has no such API on elements: the video's `webkitEnterFullscreen()` opens the system player instead (`playsinline` keeps ordinary playback inline), and `webkitbeginfullscreen` / `webkitendfullscreen` keep the button in step. With neither API, the player is fixed to the viewport (`100dvh`, safe-area insets, the page stops scrolling) and turned sideways when the phone is upright (Escape or the button leaves). A checkpoint question pauses the video, also when the learner seeks past it, and the video plays on once it is answered.
- **Lesson videos are optional.** If a video can't load (not produced yet, offline, not downloaded), the step switches to the transcript, and the checkpoint question sits above it: the transcript runs on past the moment the video asks, and often gives the answer straight after. Opening the transcript to read instead of streaming does the same, for the MP4 and the light lesson. A missing video never blocks a lesson.

## Cloud sync

`src/state/sync.ts` (`useCloudSync`) keeps a signed-in person's progress, notes and certificates in Firestore, where teachers and admins read them.

- **Reads:** progress, notes and `certs` are read once per sign-in, and once more on the `online` event if a read only reached the offline cache. There are no listeners and no polling. The certificates' read stands on its own: if it fails or the rules refuse it, lessons and notes sync as before, and it isn't tried again until the next sign-in.
- **Merge** (`mergeRemote` in `store.tsx`): the newer copy wins. Lessons compare `completedAt`; notes compare their local edit time with `updatedAt`. A lesson first seen from the cloud counts toward the teaching week it was completed in, which keeps streaks correct. Certificates are a union; where both sides have one, the earlier `at` wins, so its date and code stay the first ones.
- **Writes** (`toPush`): only entries the cloud lacks or has an older copy of. Until a read has reached the server, only work finished in this session is written, so a stale phone can never overwrite newer work from another device. Firestore's offline queue carries writes made without a signal. A certificate is created once, only when the cloud lacks it (never rewritten: the rules refuse a second write), and before the certificates' read has reached the server only one earned this session goes. `certDoc` shapes it as the rules want (a course id of 2–64 lowercase letters, digits and dashes; `title`, `track` and `at` only, each within the rules' sizes, counted in UTF-8 bytes; a language that doesn't fit is left out, English first) and holds back one they would never take. Each goes at most once a session: a refused write is never retried in a loop, and it never holds up a lesson or a note.
- **The learning profile** (`learningSync` / `useLearningSync`, `LEARN_KEYS` in `store.tsx`): onboarding's answers and what Me and My tracks change later (`onboarded`, `tracks`, `levels`, `audience`, `stage`, `weeklyLessons`, `teach`) are kept on `users/{uid}` too, so a new device skips onboarding. It reads nothing itself: it works from the profile AuthProvider already reads at sign-in. A device that isn't onboarded takes all of the account's answers at once (or, if the account has none yet, only tracks someone chose there and where they teach, which onboarding starts from). After that the newer copy wins, by `learnAt` (ms; the reducer dates every change to those fields, and a pull keeps the account's date), and the same date with other values on the profile (an admin's edit) is taken. Only the fields that differ are written, each whole (`updateDoc`, so a level or track removed goes too), and the echo of a write ends it. The language and the current track stay per device. Guests are unaffected.
- **Uploads:** project photos are scaled to at most 1280 px and re-encoded as JPEG on the phone before upload (`ProjectView.shrinkImage`). PDFs and anything the browser can't decode go up unchanged. A file still over 10 MB (Storage's limit) is caught before anything goes up, and a retry after a dropped connection sends the files already up instead of uploading them again. The answers stop where the rules do (20,000 characters, 5,000 for how AI was used), and a send the rules refuse says so rather than blaming the connection.
- **Certificates** (`content/cert.ts`, `state.certs`): recorded on the phone the first time they are earned (by Home when a course's last lesson is done, or by Me), so a replay never moves their date or code, and a course republished with another lesson, or unpublished, keeps them. Finding new ones downloads nothing: only courses whose lessons the results (by `unitId`) say are all finished are opened. A capstone's certificate needs every milestone sent (the learner's own `users/{uid}/submissions`, read once; "I'll do it in class and submit later" sends nothing), so guests never get one, and Home moves nobody up for an unfinished capstone. The name on a certificate is the account's (`users/{uid}.name`, as on /super/certificates): Me saves what is typed there and takes a name changed elsewhere. Signed in, each record is also kept in `users/{uid}/certs/{unitId}` (see Writes above), so a new phone gets it back at the next sign-in, even after its course is unpublished; a guest's certificates stay on the phone (an account created from a guest takes them over and sends them). "Reset progress on this device" clears them here only: the next sign-in brings them back. A person's page (PersonDetail) reads that collection once when it opens: a course no longer offered shows the title from the certificate, and a course with a certificate shows as done. /super/certificates still works from the summaries and the catalog, so a certificate of an unpublished course isn't listed there.
- **Certificate codes** (`certCode` in `content/cert.ts`): `DRS-<COURSE>-XXXX-XXXX`, 8 Crockford base-32 characters from a hash of the course, the moment it was earned and the account's uid (none for a guest), so a rename or a replay never moves it and it can't be worked out from what a certificate shows. The code is set when the certificate is recorded (`earnCert`, which knows the store's account) and kept with it, locally and in `users/{uid}/certs` (`code`, optional for records from before). Certificates recorded before 2026-11 had their code worked out from the learner's name each time; a store saved before then freezes the code each one showed at its first load (`withCodes`, `legacyCertCode`). /super/certificates shows `certCode` for the learner's uid, which matches every certificate earned signed in since.
- **Project reviews** (`state/myWork.ts`): Me's "My projects" reads the learner's 20 newest submissions once when it opens, and a project step reads that lesson's (`lessonId ==`, so no composite index), to show each project's status and the reviewer's feedback. No listener. "Send a new version" opens the lesson at that step (`?step=`), which a learner may do in a lesson they have finished; nothing is recorded again. In a lesson they haven't finished, the link opens that project alone (`explore.only`: no step list or Skip, the bar's button is Back, nothing is saved or recorded), and only a project step: a link to any other step starts the lesson as usual. After sending, the notice says who reviews it: their teacher or admins in an organization, else the Darsi team (platform admins on /super/projects).

## Push notifications

Opt-in web push through Firebase Cloud Messaging (FCM). The tone follows the brand rule "Encourage, never punish": no guilt, no threats about losing a streak, no emoji. Setup, browser support and how to send an announcement are in [docs/FIREBASE.md](docs/FIREBASE.md#6-push-notifications).

### Turning it on, per device

The panel in Me (`src/notify/NotificationsPanel.tsx`) is shown to signed-in people only. Guests never touch messaging.

1. **`pushSupport()`** (`src/notify/push.ts`) picks what the panel shows:
   - `unconfigured`: the build has no `VITE_FIREBASE_VAPID_KEY`;
   - `unsupported`: no service worker, `PushManager` or `Notification`;
   - `ios-install`: an iPhone or iPad in a Safari tab (web push needs Add to Home Screen, iOS 16.4+);
   - a permission the person denied: how to allow it in the browser's site settings.
2. **`enablePush(prefs)`:**
   1. `Notification.requestPermission()`;
   2. `getToken()` with the VAPID key and the app's own Workbox service worker registration (there is no `firebase-messaging-sw.js`);
   3. the `registerDevice` callable, with the language, the IANA time zone, the reminder hour, the categories and the weekly goal;
   4. `darsi:push` in localStorage remembers `{ token, on: true, uid }` (the uid keeps a shared phone from refreshing one account's token into another's).
3. **`registerDevice`** stores `users/{uid}/devices/{id}` (`id` = the first 40 hex characters of sha256(token)), sets `users/{uid}.notify`, and subscribes the token to its FCM topics while Announcements are on: `all`, `lang-<code>` (the phone's language: `en`, `ar`, or one platform admins added), `role-<role>`, and `org-<orgId>` in an organization.
4. **On each app start**, `usePushRefresh()` calls `registerDevice` again if `darsi:push.on` and permission is still granted. Tokens can rotate, and this also refreshes `lastSeen`. Changing a preference calls `updateNotifyPrefs`; turning it off calls `unregisterDevice` and deletes the token. Signing out (any Sign out button: `AuthProvider.signOut`) does the same first, so on a shared phone the next person never sees the previous account's notifications; `registerDevice` also moves a token to whoever registers it last (`pushTokens/{deviceId}` records the owner).
5. **Topics follow the person.** When their role, organization or language changes, `onUserTopicsChanged` re-subscribes all their devices.

### What is sent

Each category can be switched off in Me (`notify.cats`).

| Category | Trigger | Who gets it |
|---|---|---|
| Learning reminders (`reminders`) | `dailyNudges`, at the top of every UTC hour (`0 * * * *`): people whose reminder hour (`notify.hourUtc`) is now. `pickNudge` (`functions/src/nudge.ts`) sends at most one a day (see below) | the person |
| Project feedback (`reviews`) | `onSubmissionUpdated`: a submission goes from `submitted` to `approved` or `revise` (opens `/me#projects`). `reviewOrg`: an organization request approved (`orgApproved`, opens `/admin`) or rejected with the reviewer's note (`orgRejected`, opens `/org`) | the person who submitted it, or who asked for the organization |
| Staff alerts (`staff`) | `onSubmissionCreated`: a project is submitted. `requestOrg`: a new organization request. `ssoJob` (school sign-in, once a day at most each): `ssoSecretExpiring` (a client secret within 30 and again within 7 days of the date its admin typed; opens `/admin?tab=signin`), `ssoEndpointsMoved` (the provider names other endpoints than the tested ones; the same link) and `ssoFull` (a school reached its member limit; opens `/super/orgs/<id>?tab=signin`) | the assigned teacher while they still teach in the student's organization, or else the org admins; platform admins for requests and for capstone milestones queued for Darsi staff (`queue: "staff"`, opens `/super/projects`); for school sign-in, the organization's admins (the first two) and platform admins (`ssoFull`), at most 50 people an alert, each on its own tag per school (`darsi-sso-<orgId>-<kind>`) |
| Announcements (`news`) | `sendBroadcast` from `/super` | everyone, one organization or one role, through topics |

**`pickNudge(input, now)`** is pure and unit-tested. "Today" is the person's local day in their time zone. The rules apply in order:
1. Nothing if a reminder was already sent today, or if they finished a lesson today.
2. After 7 days without a lesson: one "we saved your place" message. After 21 days: one last message, then silence until they come back.
3. On Saturday or Sunday (UTC), with lessons left for the weekly goal: how many are left.
4. With a streak of 2+ weeks (the app's `streakWeeks` rule) and this week's goal not met yet: the streak.
5. Otherwise, one of three daily messages, rotating by day.

`dailyNudges` also fixes daylight-saving drift: if the person's local hour no longer matches, it rewrites `hourUtc` and waits for the right hour.

### The message and the service worker

- **Payload:** data-only web push with `TTL: 86400` (an offline phone gets it within a day) and `Urgency: normal`. The data is `{ title, body, link, tag, lang, dir }`: in the phone's language for English, Arabic, French, Spanish, German, Portuguese and Urdu, in English for any other added language (`wordingFor`; the five are `MORE` in `messages.ts`); there is no wording in the languages platform admins add.
  - The wording lives in `functions/src/messages.ts`. Names are wrapped in Unicode isolates, and numbers use the language's numerals.
  - Announcements are written in English and Arabic and go out once each: the Arabic text to `'lang-ar' in topics`, the English text to `'all' in topics && !('lang-ar' in topics)` (English phones and every added language), each with the audience's topic added (`&& 'org-<id>' in topics`).
  - Personal messages use `sendEachForMulticast` on the person's devices, grouped by language. Tokens that FCM reports as unregistered or invalid are deleted on the spot.
- **`public/push-sw.js`:** plain JavaScript with no imports and no SDK, loaded into Workbox's `sw.js` (`workbox.importScripts` in `vite.config.ts`).
  - `push`: reads `payload.data` and calls `showNotification` with the Darsi icon, the monochrome badge, `lang` and `dir`.
  - Tags: a new reminder replaces the previous one (`darsi-nudge`); reviews, staff alerts and announcements each have their own tag.
  - `notificationclick`: focuses an open Darsi window and navigates it to the link, or opens a new window. Only paths inside the app are followed; anything else opens `/learn`.
- **Data and battery:** the messaging SDK sits in the lazy `firebase-extra` chunk, so signing in alone never downloads it. It loads when someone turns notifications on, or when the app refreshes an enabled token at start. A push is a few hundred bytes. There are no listeners and no polling.

## Backend: Firebase

Project **`darsi-f0685`**. Firestore, Cloud Functions and the Storage bucket the app uses (`darsi-f0685`) are all in **europe-west1**. Hosting serves the public address **https://darsiai.org** (a custom domain; www.darsiai.org redirects to it). The site's own address, https://darsi-f0685.web.app, still works but is never linked.

Firebase's original default bucket, `darsi-f0685.firebasestorage.app`, was created in us-west1, and a bucket's location can't change. The app points at the europe-west1 bucket through `VITE_FIREBASE_STORAGE_BUCKET`, and the US bucket was never used and was deleted on 2026-10-25.

`firebase.json` deploys the Storage rules through the deploy target `main`. `.firebaserc` maps it to `darsi-f0685`, and to `demo-darsi.appspot.com` for the emulators. Keep the target: without one, `firebase deploy --only storage` aims at the project's default bucket, which no longer exists.

### Data model (Firestore)

```
users/{uid}                 name, email (= the account's Auth email, set once at sign-up), username, role, orgId, group,
│                           teacherId, lang, the learning profile (onboarded, audience, stage, teach, tracks, weeklyLessons, levels, learnAt: state/sync.ts), mustChangePassword, passwordSetAt (ms;
│                           when an admin set the password), active, lastActive, createdAt (the server's time),
│                           createdBy (the admin whose createMembers call made the account),
│                           plan, planUntil, planSource (subscription | org), superSeat   ← Cloud Functions only (Darsi Super)
│                           projectsWaiting (projects with status submitted)   ← the submission triggers only (waiting.ts)
│                           summary {lessonsDone, graded, xp, avgAccuracy, lastActive, competencies, compAcc, units,
│                                    tracks {track: {n, acc, sec, g}}, seconds,
│                                    weeks {"2026-W43": n, … the last 12 ISO weeks, UTC}, lastLessonAt}   ← written by onProgressWrite
│                                    (avgAccuracy, compAcc, competencies and tracks' acc count only the `graded` lessons: one
│                                    with nothing marked right or wrong, a project or a capstone stage, is done, not scored)
│                           suspended {at, by, reason, byOrg?}   ← setAccountSuspended (platform admins) and setMemberSuspended
│                                    (its organization's admins; byOrg names it). Its Auth sign-in is switched off too
│                           managedBy (the organization that made the login and manages it)   ← createMembers (username logins)
│                                    and school sign-in; sso, ssoEmail, ssoLinkedAt   ← school sign-in. No client writes these
│                           terms {privacy, terms (the versions accepted), at}   ← acceptTerms only
│                           notify {on, hourLocal 0–23, hourUtc 0–23, tz (IANA), cats {reminders, reviews, staff, news},
│                                   weeklyGoal 1–14, lastAutoAt?, comeback7At?, comeback21At?}         ← notification functions only
├─ progress/{lessonId}      lessonId, unitId, track, accuracy (0–1), xp (int 0–100), seconds, completedAt (ISO), competencies[]
├─ private/summaryRows      rows {lessonId: the row the summary counts}, at   ← onProgressWrite only; no client access
├─ notes/{lessonId}         text, updatedAt (ms)
├─ certs/{unitId}           title {lang: text}, track, at (ISO): a certificate as first earned   ← its owner creates it once;
│                           nobody changes or deletes it; read like the person's lessons (canSee). Written by cloud sync
│                           (state/sync.ts certDoc) from state.certs, read back at each sign-in, and by PersonDetail
├─ submissions/{id}         lessonId, unitId, stepId, orgId, text, fileUrl, fileName, files [{url, name, type}] (Storage
│                           addresses without a download token since 2026-11: reviewers read the files through the SDK),
│                           final, explain, aiUse, name (a final project or a capstone milestone),
│                           status (submitted | approved | revise), feedback, score, reviewedBy, reviewedAt, createdAt
│                           capstone, repo, links, check (darsi-check.json), checks [{id, ok (true | false | null = not checked), en, ar}],
│                           aiReview {status, scores, summary, next, model, usd, at, capped}, queue (teacher | staff)   ← checkCapstone only
│                           moderated {by, at, reason}   ← moderateSubmission (text, links and files removed)
└─ devices/{deviceId}       token, lang (any language code), platform ("web"), createdAt, lastSeen, topics[]   ← Cloud Functions only
                            deviceId = the first 40 hex characters of sha256(token)
pushTokens/{deviceId}       uid (the account the token belongs to now), at   ← Cloud Functions only
courseAccess/{uid}          units [unitId, ≤ 50], orgs [orgId | "none", ≤ 20; fixed when granted], until (ms) | null, name, email, grantedBy,
                            updatedAt   ← grantCourseAccess, revokeCourseAccess (platform admins, /super/access); its owner
                            reads it; the results themselves come through the courseResults callable (/results)
accountDeletions/{uid}      orgId, by, at, and the worker's startedAt, wiped, held, gaveUpAt   ← deleteOrg ("delete the accounts
                            it made") and deleteAccount (conversations left to erase); onAccountDeletion works through
                            it; no client access
orgs/{orgId}                name, type, country, city, size, status (pending | active | rejected | suspended | deleting),
                            suspended {at} (since when; who and why are in private/suspension)   ← updateOrg; deletingBy, deletingAt, deletingMembers (release |
                            delete-managed), deletingReleased, deletingQueued   ← deleteOrg,
                            createdBy, memberCount, reviewNote, createdAt, reviewedAt, reviewedBy, demo? (scripts/demo-org.mjs),
                            sso {on, slug, listed}, ssoCeiling   ← manageSso (School sign-in),
                            brand {logo (Storage path under orgs/{orgId}/brand/) | null, v (+1 per change)}   ← its admins
                            (name and brand only), licence {plan, seats, until (ms)}   ← setOrgLicence and payments
                            courses {mode: "all"} | {mode: "chosen", teachers [id], students [id], groups {class: [id]}}
                                 (absent = "all"; ≤ 300 ids a list, ≤ 60 classes, ≤ 2,000 ids in all)   ← setOrgCourses only
                            site {host (its own web address, e.g. cis.darsiai.org), darsi (Darsi's identity shows there)}
                                 (absent = no site)   ← setOrgSite only
                            Every member reads this document (each phone caches it), so it holds nothing private.
├─ private/request          contact, reason, emailVerified (whether the requester's email was confirmed when they asked;
│                           never a reason to refuse), at (what the requester told platform admins)   ← requestOrg; its admins and
│                           platform admins read (before 2026-11 on the org document: scripts/migrate-org-requests.mjs)
├─ private/invites          teacher, student (the codes), policy {requireVerifiedEmail, allowedDomains []},
│                           limits {teacher | student: {expiresAt, maxUses}}, uses {teacher | student: n} (copies for the
│                           admins' screen)   ← Cloud Functions write; its admins and platform admins read
├─ private/removed          uids {uid: {at (ms), name}}, 2,000 at most   ← removeMember, bulkMembers; joinOrg and school
│                           sign-in refuse them until readmitMember; its admins and platform admins read
├─ private/export           at, by, exportId   ← exportOrgData (a new export at most once per 10 minutes; the stamp is the
│                           export's licence to write: an erasure deletes it first)
├─ private/suspension       at, by, reason   ← updateOrg while the organization is suspended; platform admins read it, its
│                           own admins don't
├─ private/sso, ssoHealth   school sign-in's configuration and health   ← the sign-in functions only; no client reads them
└─ private/seats            at   ← setSeat and fillSeats hold it in their transaction, so seat changes run one at a time
inviteCodes/{code}          orgId, role, expiresAt? (ms), maxUses?, uses?   ← Cloud Functions only (what joinOrg checks)
ssoIdentities, ssoSecrets, ssoUsed, ssoTests, ssoSlugs   ← school sign-in (see School sign-in); no client access
joinAttempts/{uid}          n, since (ms)                    ← joinOrg: tries (counted before the code is checked), 10 an hour
subscriptions/{uid}         plan, status (active | canceled | expired | past_due), source (paymob | stripe | grant),
                            currentPeriodEnd (ms), updatedAt   ← Cloud Functions only; the owner and platform admins read
credits/{uid}               plan (this period's tutor credits), planUntil (ms), packs (never expire)   ← Cloud Functions only
payments/{provider}_{ref}   provider, ref, kind (subscription | licence | credits), uid | orgId, plan, amount, currency,
                            seats, status (paid | refunded), at, refundedAt   ← Cloud Functions only; platform admins read
config/plans                plans [Plan], economics {usdPerCredit, egpPerUsd}, rev, updatedAt   ← savePlans (Darsi Super)
config/settings.superOn     false turns Darsi Super off for everyone   ← setSuperOn
config/sites                list [{host, orgId}] (one organization per host), rev, updatedAt, updatedBy   ← setOrgSite, and
                            onOrgSite when an organization is deleted; platform admins read
checkouts/{id}              uid, orgId, kind, plan (copy), currency, amount, cents, seats, provider, clientId, providerId,
                            providerOrder (Paymob's order id, which its callbacks sign),
                            status (open | paid | failed), payments {ref: true}, createdAt, paidAt   ← startCheckout and the webhooks
stripeSubs/{subscriptionId} checkout, at   ← stripeWebhook (renewals and cancelling find their checkout)
paymobOrders/{orderId}      checkout   ← startCheckout; paymobWebhook matches a payment to its checkout through it
tutorLeases/{uid}           calls {id: {kind text | voice, until, hold {fromPlan, fromPacks}}}, warmAt   ← the tutor's calls
                            under way (startCall/endCall); an expired lease gives its hold back
config/tutor                liveModel, textModel, voice, live {prices}, text {prices}, maxMinutes, on   ← platform admins (optional)
tutorUsage/{uid}/days/{day} usd, credits, text, voice (counts)   ← the tutor's charge; nobody reads it from a phone
config/capstone             ai, perDay, maxPerDay, callsPerDay, updatedAt, updatedBy   ← saveCapstoneConfig (Capstones)
capstoneLimits/{uid}        day, calls (milestones sent today), n (AI reviews today), lastAt (ms), at   ← checkCapstone; no client access
capstoneDays/{yyyy-mm-dd}   day, n (AI reviews given), usd   ← checkCapstone (the platform's daily budget); no client access
units/{unitId}              owner ("public" | orgId), title {en, ar}, track, level, lessonCount, lessons [{id, title}],
                            rev (int, +1 per save), updatedAt, updatedBy,
                            published {version, rev, at, by, entry (its catalog.json entry)} | null   ← published: functions only
                            lastVersion (the version counter, kept through an unpublish)   ← functions only
                            same {version, rev} | absent   ← the editor: draft rev `rev` holds exactly published `version`
unitDrafts/{unitId}         owner, rev, unit (the whole draft Unit, ≤ 600 KB)   ← the course editor; lists never read it
config/settings             tracks [TrackDef], rev, updatedAt, updatedBy   ← saveSettings
                            languages [LanguageDef: code, native, english, dir, numerals, version]   ← publishLanguage
                                                                 (each writer merges its own part; platform admins read)
translations/{code}         code, native, english, dir, numerals, messages {key: text}, rev (+1 per save), updatedAt,
                            updatedBy, published {version, rev, at, by} | null   ← the Languages page; published: functions only
broadcasts/{id}             title {en, ar}, body {en, ar}, link, audience {type: all | org | role, orgId?, role?},
                            sentBy, sentAt, results {en, ar} (FCM message id, or null on error)   ← sendBroadcast; platform admins read
audit/{id}                  action, by, orgId? (the organization it concerns), …, ip, rid (when a callable made it), at
                            ← Cloud Functions write; platform admins read; an organization's admins read theirs through orgAudit
config/analytics            measurementId, apiSecret, updatedAt, updatedBy   ← setGaConfig (/super/analytics); platform admins read
config/retention            periods {bugReportsClosedDays, bugReportsAnyDays, analyticsDevicesDays, checkoutsOpenDays,
                            joinAttemptsDays, auditDays, orgExportsDays: days | null}, updatedAt, updatedBy   ← saveRetention
config/legal                privacy?, terms? {draft {lang: text}, published? {version, at, by}, lastVersion}   ← saveLegal;
                            config/legalPublished holds the published copies, config/legal/versions/<doc>-<n> every version
                            ever published (never deleted)
stats/jobs                  <job> {at (ms), ok, error?, …its own numbers}: statsJob, dailyNudges, sitesJob, retentionJob, ssoJob
stats/catalogs              dirty {owner: ms}: catalogs behind Firestore, written again by sitesJob
stats/csp                   {directive: count}, at   ← cspReport
stats/overview              at, users {total, byRole, new7, new30, active7, active30, withLessons}, lessons, xp,
                            avgAccuracy, weeks, orgs {orgId: {name, country, members, teachers, students, active7,
                            active30, lessons, xp, avgAccuracy}}, orgsByStatus   ← statsJob, refreshStats
analytics/{yyyy-mm-dd}      day, n, off, ev {name: n}, role {role: {events, lessons}}, org {orgId: {events, lessons}},
                            sessions {n, lang, role, aud, installed, offline}, lessons {n, track, unit, lang, acc, accN, sec},
                            labs, downloads {n, mb}, screens, dev, devNew, devRole   ← collect (increments only);
                            revenue {EGP|USD: {value, n}, plan}, refunds   ← recordPayment, recordRefund;
                            tutor   ← the tutor's charge; capstone {calls, reviews, usd}   ← checkCapstone;
                            err {n, k {kind: n}}, errBuild {build: n}   ← collect (the app's own errors)
analyticsDevices/{id}       first, last (yyyy-mm-dd), role, lang, aud, org, inst, lastBatch (the last batch counted)
                            ← collect; id = the phone's random id
bugReports/{id}             createdAt, status (new | fixed | closed), text, url, path, user {uid, email, name, role, orgId, plan, provider} | null,
                            shot {path, bytes, type, w, h} | null, details {page, app, device, network, account, learner, log}, server {ua, lang},
                            statusBy, statusAt   ← reportBug; platform admins change only the status
```

`notify.hourUtc` is the UTC hour that equals `hourLocal` in `tz` today. `registerDevice` and `updateNotifyPrefs` recompute it, and `dailyNudges` rewrites it when daylight saving moves it. The notification queries are equality-only (`notify.on` + `notify.hourUtc`; `orgId` + `role`), so they need no composite index.

Username logins (for people without email) map to `<username>@users.darsiai.org`. The mapping lives in two places, `apps/web/src/auth/api.ts` and `functions/src/usernameDomain.ts`, which must stay in sync. The domain must be one Darsi owns with no mail set up for it: anyone can ask Firebase to send a reset email to any address, so whoever receives mail there could open every username account. Until 2026-11 it was `users.darsi.app`, a domain Darsi doesn't own. Until `scripts/move-username-domain.mjs` has moved every account off it, the old domain still counts as a username login everywhere (managed accounts, the rules' reserved domain, school sign-in never linking to it), sign-in tries it when a username's password is refused at the new one, admins' lookups find accounts there, and `createMembers` refuses a username it still holds. `/forgot` sends nothing to either domain and points to the organization admin.

### Security rules

**Staff access follows the profile, not only the token** (both rule files). A token stays valid for up to an hour after a demotion, a removal or a suspension, so a staff role needs the claims **and** the profile to agree:

| Who | Token | And `users/{uid}` |
|---|---|---|
| Platform admin | `role == "superadmin"` | `role == "superadmin"`, no `suspended` |
| Org admin of X | `role == "orgAdmin"`, `orgId == X`, not `frozen` | `role == "orgAdmin"`, `orgId == X`, no `suspended`, not `frozen` |
| Teacher of a person | `role == "teacher"`, the person's `orgId`, the person's `teacherId` is theirs, not `frozen` | `role == "teacher"`, the same `orgId`, no `suspended`, not `frozen` |

The claim test comes first, so learners and students never pay for the profile lookup; staff pay one extra document read per request. A staff token with no profile is refused. `frozen` is the claim on the teachers and admins of a suspended organization (or one being deleted): they keep what any member has and nothing a staff role gives. A suspension leaves role and `orgId` on the profile, and the token in an open tab was minted before it, so the rules read both of its marks from the profile as well (the same document, so no further lookup): `suspended` on an account (`setAccountSuspended`, an organization's own suspend) and `frozen`, which `writeClaims` mirrors onto `users/{uid}` before it writes the claims and removes when the organization is active again. No client write sets or clears either. Members stay token-only: reading the organization's document and its files needs only the token's `orgId`, so no member's phone pays a lookup at sign-in. Firestore allows 10 document lookups per request and 20 per batch; the deepest path is a project review (the person's profile and the reviewer's), and a bulk change looks the admin's own profile up for every write, which is one cached document (`tenancy.rules.test.ts` sends a 30-member batch). If a bulk change of more than 20 people is ever refused with "permission denied", look there first. Before these rules go out, `node scripts/check-claims.mjs` must report no mismatch: an admin whose profile and claims disagree is locked out (docs/FIREBASE.md §3).

**Firestore** (`firebase/firestore.rules`):
- **`users/{uid}`:**
  - *Read:* the person, a platform admin, their organization's admins, and their assigned teacher.
