  - *Self-registration:* only as an independent `learner`, with a fixed list of fields, each typed and bounded, `email` equal to the token's email in lower case (nobody can claim another person's address), `createdAt` the request's own time, and never for a `@users.darsi.app` login (those are for accounts organizations make).
  - *Self-edits:* never email, role, organization, teacher, summary, `notify` or `mustChangePassword` (only `passwordChanged` clears it). The fields a write changes are type- and size-checked, elements included (`profileOk`: at most 30 tracks joining to 750 characters, levels 0–12; the learning profile's `onboarded` a boolean, `audience` teachers or students, `stage` one of the four, `learnAt` an integer of ms).
  - *Org admins and platform admins:* may change only group, teacher, active flag and name, typed (`memberOk`), and delete nobody. Roles, organizations, suspension and deletion are the callables', which write the audit log. (The screens make these edits through `updateMembers`; the rule stays for older copies of the app.)
  - *Never from a client:* `sso`, `ssoEmail`, `managedBy`, `ssoLinkedAt`, `terms`. A person whose profile has `suspended` can no longer update it.
- **`progress`:** written only by its owner, under its own `lessonId` (`[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{1,99}`). Only the listed keys, each typed and bounded: `accuracy` 0–1, `xp` an integer 0–100, `seconds` an integer up to 86,400,000, `completedAt` a string, `unitId` ≤ 64, `track` ≤ 24, `competencies` ≤ 30 strings joining to 1,230 characters. Nobody can delete it. The summary counts only published lessons, with the unit's own course, track and competencies (`onProgressWrite`).
- **`submissions`:** created by the owner with status `submitted`, `createdAt == request.time`, the token's own `orgId`, and only the fields `ProjectView` writes, typed and bounded. `fileUrl` and each `files[].url` must be a download link of this project's Storage (or the local emulator) under `submissions/<org>/<their uid>/`. No reviewer field can be set at creation, nor a capstone's `checks`, `aiReview` or `queue`: `checkCapstone` writes capstone milestones with the Admin SDK. Reviewed by the person's assigned teacher, the admins of the organization the person is in now (not the one the work was sent from) or a platform admin, who can change only status (submitted, approved or revise), score, feedback (≤ 5,000) and reviewer fields, as themselves. Nobody reviews their own project, whatever their role, and a suspended account cannot send one.
- **`notes`:** the owner only; `{text ≤ 50,000, updatedAt}`.
- **`certs`:** created once by the owner (`title` per language, `track`, `at`, each bounded, and optionally its `code`: `DRS-` and up to 86 capitals, digits and dashes); read like the person's lessons; nobody changes or deletes one.
- **`courseAccess/{uid}`:** read by its owner and platform admins; no client writes it (`grantCourseAccess`, `revokeCourseAccess`). A granted teacher reads no other person's `progress` or `submissions` through the rules: the `courseResults` callable does, scoped to the grant. A platform admin's collection-group read of `submissions` (`/super/projects`) stays.
- **`users/{uid}/private/…`, `paymobOrders`, `tutorLeases`, `accountDeletions`, `ssoIdentities`, `ssoUsed`, `ssoSlugs`, `ssoTests`, `ssoSecrets`:** no client access (functions only), platform admins included. `orgs/{orgId}/private/sso` and `ssoHealth` are closed to its admins too: they see them through `manageSso`.
- **`devices`** (push tokens) and **`pushTokens`** (their owners): no client access at all, not even the owner's.
- **`orgs`:** readable by members, the requester and platform admins, so the document holds nothing private. Never created from a client (`requestOrg`, `createOrg`). Its admins, and a platform admin's browser, change only `name` and `brand` (below) and delete none; status, licence, country, city and deletion are the callables' (`updateOrg`, `deleteOrg`). Nobody writes `courses` from a client, platform admins included: only `setOrgCourses`, which checks its shape and size, since every member's phone reads it. The same holds for `site` (what `setOrgSite` wrote before 2026-11-09; nothing reads it now): an organization's web addresses are `sites/{host}`, one document per address, written only by the functions. `orgs/{orgId}/private/…` (the invite codes, the request's contact and reason) is read by its admins and platform admins and written only by the functions; `private/suspension` (who suspended the organization and why) by platform admins only.
- **`broadcasts`:** readable by platform admins; written only by `sendBroadcast`.
- **`inviteCodes`:** no client access at all.
- **`units` and `unitDrafts`** (course editor): `canEdit(owner)` is a platform admin, or that organization's admin for an org `owner`.
  - *Create:* `idFits`: org units need a generated id (`u` + 8 characters, `[a-z0-9]`), so they can never shadow a built-in or public unit; `rev` starts at 1 and `published` must be null. A draft is created only beside a `units` doc of the same owner (the editor writes both in one batch), and a unit can't be created over another owner's draft. `publishDraft` also checks the draft's owner.
  - *Update:* `nextRev`: every save sets `rev` to exactly the stored `rev + 1`, so a second editor with a stale copy is refused instead of silently overwriting; `owner` and `published` can't change.
  - *Delete:* only while unpublished; `deleteUnit` handles published units.
  - Students and teachers never read drafts; learners get published units from Storage.
- **`config/{doc}`** (platform settings): platform admins read; nobody writes from a client (`saveSettings` and `publishLanguage` do). Everyone else reads the published copy, `content/settings.json`.
- **`translations/{code}`** (languages being added): platform admins only. The id must equal `code`, every save sets `rev` to exactly `rev + 1`, `published` is never written from a client, and a draft can be deleted only while unpublished.
- **`orgs/{orgId}` branding:** its admins may change `name` (2–80 characters) and `brand`: only `logo` and `v`, and `logo` must be null or a file `orgs/{orgId}/brand/logo-<6–32 of [a-z0-9]>.(png|webp|jpg)` of that same org.
- **`stats`, `analytics`, `analyticsDevices`:** platform admins read; nobody writes from a client (the functions do).
- **`config/analytics`:** platform admins read it; no client writes it (`setGaConfig`, which checks the measurement ID and audits the change without the secret).
- **`audit`:** platform admins read; nobody writes from a client. An organization's admins read their own entries through `orgAudit`.

**Storage** (`firebase/storage.rules` on `darsi-f0685`):
- `media/` and `kits/` are publicly readable and never writable from the client.
- `submissions/{orgId|none}/{uid}/{unitId}/{file}` (and the older `submissions/{orgId|none}/{uid}/{file}`) is created only by its owner, in their own organization's folder (`none` without one): under 10 MB; JPEG, PNG, WebP, GIF or HEIC photos, PDF, MP4 or common audio (never SVG or HTML); named as `ProjectView` names files (`{lessonId}-{ms}-{name}`). It can be read by the owner, a platform admin, the admins of the organization the person is in **now** (not the folder the file was sent from: a former organization's admins lose a departed member's files), the teacher the person's profile names in the same organization, and university teachers granted the course (`grantedS`: the course, the grant's end date, and the organization the file's owner is in **now** (`"none"` for someone in none), which must be in the grant's stored `orgs`; a grant with no `orgs`, or a file whose owner has no profile, opens nothing; see [Course access for university teachers](#course-access-for-university-teachers)). Each path reads at most two documents; three only for a staff or platform-admin token whose own profile no longer agrees with it and who also holds a grant (if Storage refuses that, the caller is refused until the token refreshes). (Download links carry their own token, so these read rules cover SDK reads only.)
- `exports/{orgId}/{exportId}/{file}` (an organization's exports) is read by that organization's admins and platform admins and written only by the functions.
- `content/**` (public authored units) is readable by anyone, guests included. `orgs/{orgId}/**` is readable only by that organization's members and platform admins.
- Media uploads go to `content/media/{unitId}/` (platform admins) and `orgs/{orgId}/media/{unitId}/` (that org's admins, platform admins): MP4 ≤ 60 MB, VTT ≤ 512 KB, PDF or PPTX ≤ 20 MB. No client ever writes the unit JSON or `catalog.json`; the functions do.
- The org's logo goes to `orgs/{orgId}/brand/logo-<id>.(png|webp|jpg)` (its admins, platform admins): PNG, WebP or JPEG, ≤ 100 KB. Its members read it like the rest of `orgs/{orgId}/`.

### Cloud Functions (europe-west1)

| Function | Type | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `requestOrg` | callable | A signed-in person asks to register an organization (one pending request at a time). The contact and reason go to `orgs/{id}/private/request`, not the org document every member reads. Platform admins get a staff alert; a failed alert never fails the request |
| `reviewOrg` | callable | Platform admin approves (creates invite codes; the requester becomes its admin) or rejects with a note. Only a request still pending (checked in its transaction, so two admins or a stale list can't review it twice), never moving a requester who has since joined another organization (`reason: "requester-in-org"`); both outcomes are audited and pushed to the requester (`orgApproved`, `orgRejected`) |
| `joinOrg` | callable | Join an active organization with a teacher or student invite code (`normalizeCode`: case, spaces and dashes don't matter). 10 tries per account per hour, counted in a transaction before the code is checked, then `resource-exhausted`. It decides from the profile and Auth, not the token, and keeps the organization's join rules: the code's last day and uses, a confirmed email, allowed domains, and the removed list (`code-expired`, `code-used-up`, `email-unverified`, `email-domain`, `removed`) |
| `regenerateInviteCode` | callable | Org admin replaces a code (12 characters, `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX`); the old one stops working, and the new one has no last day and no limit (set them again on the Invite tab). Moves an org's codes from before 2026-11 to `private/invites`. Only for an active organization, whoever asks (`org-suspended`, `org-deleting`, `org-inactive` for one not yet approved or rejected, `no-org`) |
| `createMembers` | callable | Bulk accounts (up to 500 per call): random passwords with no look-alike characters, usernames for people without email, teacher assignment (the teacher's email or login is resolved through Auth, and must be a teacher or admin of the org). Teachers and admins are made first (`membersCore.ts`), so pupils in the same call get a teacher listed anywhere in it; a teacher column that names nobody gives `warning: "teacherNotFound"`. A made-up username is the name's first two parts in Latin letters (Arabic and Persian letters transliterated, `passwords.ts`) and 5 digits (`user.` and 6 digits when nothing can be written in Latin), drawn again when taken; `exists` is a typed email that has an account, `taken` a typed username someone has. Results come back in row order |
| `setMemberRole` | callable | Org admin changes a member's role (never their own; never the organization's only admin, `last-admin`); a teacher or admin made a student releases the pupils who had them as teacher. Every refusal in `index.ts` and `platform.ts` carries a stable code in its details (`reason`: `self-role`, `self-remove`, `self-password`, `last-admin`, `own-login`, `not-member`, …), which the admin screens show in the admin's language (`admin/refusals.ts`; the English message is the fallback) |
| `resetMemberPassword` | callable | New random password, only for logins the org made (`@users.darsiai.org` or the old `@users.darsi.app`, password only); anyone with their own email or Google resets it themselves. The person must choose their own at next sign-in |
| `removeMember` | callable | Back to an independent learner (never yourself, never the organization's only admin); their learning record stays with them, their sessions end, and they go on the organization's removed list (`private/removed`), so their old code no longer lets them in. A suspension the organization made is lifted as they leave. A teacher's pupils are released (`releasePupils`: their teacherId is cleared, so project alerts go to the admins), as when `setUserRole` moves a teacher out |
| `exportAccountData`, `deleteAccount` | callable | Data rights (`account.ts`; see [Accounts: data, deletion and suspension](#accounts-data-deletion-and-suspension)): an account's data as JSON, or the account deleted with its data. The person themself (deleting needs a sign-in in the last 10 minutes, and not an account their organization made or an admin's), platform admins for anyone but other platform admins, org admins for the students and teachers whose accounts their organization manages (`managedBy`, or a username login), not while their organization is suspended or being deleted (`org-suspended`, `org-deleting`). A session the school's sign-in opened on a personal account can't export or delete it (`sso-own-login`: the person's own login is needed). One data download per account every 10 minutes, the person's own and staff's counted apart (`data-wait`, with `retryAt`) |
| `setAccountSuspended`, `moderateSubmission` | callable | Platform admins only (abuse): switch an account's sign-in off (sessions end) or on again, mirrored as `suspended` on the profile; or remove a project's text, links and files, marking it `moderated` |
| `passwordChanged` | callable | Clears the first-sign-in flag, only once Auth's "tokens valid after" time is later than when the admin set the password (a real change) |
| `findAccount` | callable | Platform admins (anyone) and org admins (their members; not while their organization is suspended or being deleted: `org-suspended`, `org-deleting`): an account by email or username, resolved through Auth, with `emailVerified` and how many profiles claim that email |
| `onProgressWrite` | Firestore trigger (retried for six hours) | Keeps `users/{uid}.summary` up to date (`progress.ts`): a fixed few reads per write. Only published lessons the person can see count, with the unit's own course, track and competencies; the counted rows are kept in `users/{uid}/private/summaryRows`, and the summary is `summarize` (`summary.ts`) over them: lessons, XP, accuracy, time, per track, per competency (evidence and accuracy), units, last activity, lessons per ISO week for the last 12 weeks, `lastLessonAt` |
| `registerDevice` | callable | Stores or refreshes this device's push token, sets `notify` (on, reminder hour, time zone, categories, weekly goal) and subscribes the token to its topics |
| `unregisterDevice` | callable | Unsubscribes and deletes the device; with no devices left, `notify.on` becomes false |
| `updateNotifyPrefs` | callable | Changes the reminder hour, time zone, categories or weekly goal, and re-applies topics on every device (the Announcements switch) |
| `sendBroadcast` | callable | Platform admin only: an announcement in English and Arabic to everyone, one organization or one role. One FCM send per language; logged in `broadcasts` and `audit` |
| `onSubmissionCreated` | Firestore trigger | A project was submitted: recounts the person's `projectsWaiting` (`waiting.ts`), then a staff alert to the assigned teacher (while they still teach in the student's organization), or else the organization's admins, which opens the person's Projects tab (`/teach/<uid>?tab=projects`, `/admin/people/<uid>?tab=projects`); a capstone milestone queued for Darsi staff (`queue: "staff"`) alerts every platform admin |
| `onSubmissionUpdated` | Firestore trigger | A review changed a submission's status: recounts `projectsWaiting`; moved to `approved` or `revise`, the person who submitted it is told, and the notification opens `/me#projects` |
| `onUserTopicsChanged` | Firestore trigger | Role, organization or language changed: re-subscribes every device's topics |
| `dailyNudges` | scheduled, hourly (Cloud Scheduler) | Sends at most one learning reminder per person per local day, at the hour they chose (see [Push notifications](#push-notifications)) |
| `publishUnit` | callable | Course editor: validates the saved draft (the same validator as the editor, with the saved tracks and their levels), writes `…/units/{id}/v{n+1}.json`, rebuilds that owner's `catalog.json` in the saved track order, keeps the previous version and deletes older ones. With `rev` (the editor sends the revision on its screen) it refuses with `aborted` a draft someone saved since, rather than publish it unseen |
| `unpublishUnit` | callable | Takes a unit away from learners: catalog entry and JSON versions go; the draft, uploaded media and learners' progress stay (`unpublishDraft`, which `npm run seed -- --unpublish` also uses). In the editor: Unpublish on each published course in the list (after a confirmation), and in a course's own actions |
| `deleteUnit` | callable | Deletes a unit everywhere: catalog entry, JSON versions, media and both Firestore documents. Media that another draft of the same owner links to (a duplicated unit or a copied lesson) stay |
| `saveSettings` | callable | Platform admin only: saves the tracks list (see [Tracks and levels](#tracks-and-levels)) to `config/settings` and publishes `content/settings.json` |
| `publishLanguage` | callable | Platform admin only: checks a saved language draft (`validateLanguage`), writes `content/i18n/{code}/v{n+1}.json`, lists the language in `config/settings` and `content/settings.json`, keeps the previous pack and deletes older ones |
| `unpublishLanguage` | callable | Platform admin only: takes a language off the pickers and deletes its packs; people who chose it see English. The draft stays |
| `collect` | HTTPS (`darsiai.org/api/collect`, a Hosting rewrite) | Phones' event batches: checks them, adds them to `analytics/{day}`, counts active phones in `analyticsDevices`, and forwards them to Google Analytics when `config/analytics` is set (see [Analytics](#analytics)). Counts only under ids Darsi knows (published courses, Labs apps, active organizations, tracks, languages, fixed screens; anything else is `other`), drops events more than 10 minutes ahead. At most 3 instances; per instance and minute 12 batches per address and device, 300 per address, 3,000 in all |
| `createOrg`, `updateOrg`, `deleteOrg` | callable | Platform admins only: a new active organization (with private invite codes and optionally its admin: never the caller, who would lose the platform role, nor another platform admin, nor another organization's only working admin (`last-admin`, `sso-last-way-in`); checked before anything is written); its details or status (`updateOrg`: suspend and resume, with the staff's claims refreshed); or deleting it after its name is typed (`deleteOrg`, 540 s, 512 MiB, called again with its cursor until done): members are released as learners or, by the admin's choice, the accounts it made are queued for deletion; its codes, units and files go (see [Organizations: status, deletion and export](#organizations-status-deletion-and-export)) |
| `setUserRole` | callable | Platform admins only: any account's role and organization (students, teachers and org admins need an active organization; learners and platform admins have none). Moving orgs clears class and teacher and moves the member counts. Nobody changes their own role, and an organization's only admin is not demoted or moved out until someone else is its admin (`refuseLastAdmin`, `last-admin`; a suspended admin does not count as the one who stays). Making a platform admin needs the caller's sign-in in the last ten minutes (`recent-login`). Someone who leaves an organization (moved or made a learner or platform admin) also gets an entry in that organization's own log (`left: true`) |
| `savePlans` | callable | Platform admins only: the Darsi Super plans and economics, every price keeping at least 60% margin; publishes `content/plans.json` without the costs (see [Darsi Super](#darsi-super)) |
| `grantSuper`, `grantCredits` | callable | Platform admins only: complimentary Super for some days (with the plan's credits), or extra credits into packs |
| `setOrgLicence` | callable | Platform admins only: an organization's licence (plan, seats, days) |
| `setOrgCourses` | callable | The organization's admins and platform admins (`freshCaller`): which courses its teachers, students and classes see (`orgCourses.ts`; the checks are the web app's `content/orgCourses.ts`, copied to `shared/`). Refuses with a `details.reason`: `not-org-admin`, `no-org`, and `courses-mode`, `-list`, `-id`, `-many` (over 300 in a list), `-classes` (over 60), `-class` (a name the people pages can't save), `-total` (over 2,000). Stores the choice sorted, without repeats or empty classes, and audits the counts |
| `setOrgSite` | callable | Platform admins only (`freshCaller`): an organization's own web address and whether Darsi's identity shows there (`sites.ts`; the host rules are the web app's `content/sites.ts`, copied to `shared/`). `{ orgId, host, darsi }`; an empty host removes every address of the organization. Kept for phones with the app from before `/super/domains`: it does what `manageSite`'s approve and remove do, on `sites/{host}`. Refuses with a `details.reason`: `not-super`, `no-org`, `site-host` (not a lowercase hostname), `site-reserved` (darsiai.org, www.darsiai.org, `*.web.app`, `*.firebaseapp.com`, and the names kept for Darsi), `site-taken` (the address is another organization's: `sites/{host}` is one document per address, read in the transaction) |
| `onOrgSite` | Firestore trigger with the writer's identity (`orgs/{orgId}`, 120 s, at most 5 instances) | An organization with a site renamed, given a new logo (OrgBranding writes the document directly) or deleted: `content/sites.json` again; renamed or re-branded while it has an approved address, it is audited as `orgRebrand` by whoever wrote the document and logged as the warning `org-rebrand`; an approved school-finder listing goes back to `requested` (log `sso-unlisted`); deleted, its addresses are disconnected and forgotten. Anything else on the document (member counts, licences) returns at once |
| `setSeat`, `fillSeats` | callable | The org's admins and platform admins: give or take one seat, or fill the free seats (teachers, then students), in a transaction that holds `orgs/{orgId}/private/seats` |
| `checkRevenueEvent` | callable | Platform admins only: sends a sample `purchase` to Google Analytics' validation server |
| `checkCapstone` | callable (512 MiB, 120 s, at most 10 instances; secrets `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GEMINI_API_KEY`) | Signed-in learners: a capstone milestone. The limits first (one send per account every 20 s, `callsPerDay` a day: `resource-exhausted`), then the checks (GitHub with Darsi's `GITHUB_TOKEN`, darsi-check.json, localhost-only scans), the advisory AI review within the learner's and the platform's daily budget, and the submission (see [Capstones](#capstones)) |
| `saveCapstoneConfig` | callable | Platform admins only: capstone AI reviews on or off and the daily limits (`config/capstone`); audited |
| `startCheckout` | callable | Signed-in people: a checkout for a plan in EGP (Paymob) or USD (Stripe); returns the provider's page; refuses `already-subscribed` while a card subscription renews by itself (see [Darsi Super](#darsi-super)) |
| `setRenewal` | callable | Signed-in people: cancel (`renew: false`) or restore the renewal of their own Stripe subscription; Super runs to the end of what was paid. Works with Super switched off too |
| `paymobWebhook`, `stripeWebhook` | HTTPS | The providers' signed webhooks: payments, renewals, refunds and cancellations, each matched to its checkout. Paymob payments are matched through the signed order id (`paymobOrders`); a refund is believed from the transaction's own signed `is_refunded`/`is_voided`, or, for a refund that names its original, after asking Paymob for that original. A full refund takes back what it bought (the period, or a pack's unspent credits) |
| `tutorAsk`, `tutorStatus` | callable | Darsi Super: a typed question to the tutor about a course (charged in credits), and the balance with the credits a spoken minute costs. Credits are held before the model is called and settled after; two typed calls at once per person (`busy` past that); `warm` needs credits and runs once per 20 s per person. The voice tutor is the relay on Cloud Run, not a function (one call at a time per person, a minute's credits held, Origin required, revoked tokens refused) |
| `tutorSearch` | HTTPS (512 MiB, 30 s) | `search_course` for the tutor agent: the same index and ranking as the direct tutor and the voice relay. Answers only a Google ID token made for its own address by `config/tutor.agentAccount` |
| `setSuperOn` | callable | Platform admins only: Darsi Super on or off for everyone (`config/settings.superOn`, republished as `super` in `content/settings.json`) |
| `onSubscriptionWrite`, `onOrgLicence` | Firestore triggers | Rewrite the claims (`plan`, `planUntil`) when a subscription or an org licence changes |
| `statsJob` | scheduled, daily 02:30 UTC (1 GiB) | Accounts and organizations from every user's summary into `stats/overview`, read in pages; mends `orgs.memberCount`; heartbeat in `stats/jobs` (see [Scheduled jobs and their heartbeats](#scheduled-jobs-and-their-heartbeats)) |
| `refreshStats` | callable | Platform admin only: the same, now (the Refresh button on /super/analytics) |
| `reportBug` | HTTPS (`darsiai.org/api/bug`, a Hosting rewrite) | "Report a problem": anyone's report (guests too), checked and cut to size, with who sent it only from a verified ID token; the screenshot goes to `bugReports/{id}.jpg` in the bucket. At most 2 instances; per instance 8 per address per 10 minutes, 10 per account per hour, 120 per hour in all (see [Problem reports](#problem-reports)) |
| `sendFeedback` | HTTPS (`darsiai.org/api/feedback`, a Hosting rewrite) | "Share feedback": a rating of 1 to 5 stars, an optional topic (`lessons`, `videos`, `labs`, `app`, `other`) and comment (2,000 characters), the page as a path (no query or fragment), and the course when it came from Home's question; anyone may send it, guests too, with who sent it only from a verified ID token. Stored in `feedback/{id}`. At most 2 instances; per instance 40 per address per 10 minutes (a class may rate a course together), 10 per account per hour, 600 per hour in all (see [Feedback](#feedback)) |
| `manageOrgPage` | callable | An organization's homepage: get, save (a draft, with its problems), publish (only a page without problems, to `content/pages/{orgId}/page.json`), unpublish. The organization's admins (not while it is suspended or being deleted) and platform admins; audited. Refusals `bad-action`, `bad-page`, `page-too-long`, `page-problems` (see [Organization homepage](#organization-homepage)) |
| `seoPage` | HTTPS (Hosting rewrites of `/`, `/sitemap.xml`, `/llms.txt`) | Each address's home page for search and AI engines (title, description, canonical, alternates, JSON-LD, text), and its sitemap and llms.txt (see [Search and AI engines](#search-and-ai-engines)) |
| `setJoinPolicy`, `readmitMember` | callable | The organization's admins: who may join with a code (a confirmed email, allowed domains, each code's last day and most uses), and letting a removed person back in (see [Invite codes](#invite-codes)) |
| `listMembers`, `updateMembers`, `bulkMembers`, `setMemberSuspended` | callable (`listMembers` 120 s; `bulkMembers` 300 s, 512 MiB) | The organization's admins and platform admins: the member list in pages, audited class, teacher and name edits, bulk remove, suspend, restore, password reset and role, and suspending a managed login (see [Members](#members)) |
| `leaveOrg` | callable | A member who signed up themself leaves their organization and keeps their learning |
| `exportOrgData` | callable (540 s, 512 MiB) | The organization's admins and platform admins: the organization's people and their learning as gzipped JSON files under `exports/{orgId}/`, in steps; a new export at most once per 10 minutes (`export-wait`) |
| `orgAudit` | callable | The organization's admins (not while it is suspended or being deleted) and platform admins: that organization's audit entries, in pages (see [Audit log](#audit-log)) |
| `grantCourseAccess`, `revokeCourseAccess`, `courseResults` | callable (`courseResults` 120 s, 512 MiB) | Platform admins grant or revoke a university teacher's course access; a grant always stores its organizations (the teacher's own at that moment when none are named, `"none"` for a teacher in none). The teacher (or a platform admin) reads a course's results through the callable, limited to the grant's courses, stored organizations and end date; a grant with no stored organizations reads nothing (`no-grant`) |
| `onAccountDeletion` | Firestore trigger (`accountDeletions/{uid}`; retried, 540 s, 512 MiB, at most 5 instances) | Erases the accounts an organization's deletion queued, and finishes the tutor's conversations a `deleteAccount` could not erase |
| `setGaConfig` | callable | Platform admins only: the Google Analytics measurement ID and API secret (`config/analytics`); audited without the secret. Two refusals, so the page points at the right field: `bad-ga` (the ID isn't `G-…`, or a field isn't text; said first) and `bad-ga-secret` (an API secret over 100 characters). `collect` forwards with the new settings within 10 minutes |
| `saveRetention`, `previewRetention`, `deleteBugReport` | callable | Platform admins only: the keeping periods, a count of what they would delete now, and deleting one problem report with its screenshot |
| `retentionJob` | scheduled, daily 03:10 UTC | Deletes what has passed its keeping period; nothing until periods are saved (see [Retention and legal pages](#retention-and-legal-pages)) |
| `saveLegal`, `acceptTerms` | callable | Platform admins save, publish or unpublish the privacy notice and the terms; any signed-in person records the versions they agreed to |
| `cspReport` | HTTPS (`darsiai.org/api/csp`, a Hosting rewrite; one instance) | The browsers' reports of what the report-only Content-Security-Policy would block: one log line each and a count in `stats/csp` |
| `manageSite`, `sitesJob` | callable; scheduled, every 15 minutes | Organizations' own web addresses: asking, approving, checking and removing (see [Organization sites](#organization-sites-custom-web-addresses)); the job also audits an address that stopped being live (`siteLost`) and writes pending catalogs again |
| `ssoEdge`, `ssoCallback` | HTTPS (`/api/sso/**` on the app's site; `login.darsiai.org`) | School sign-in: start, finish and the school lookup; the provider's callback (see [School sign-in](#school-sign-in)) |
| `manageSso` | callable (120 s) | School sign-in's wizard, status card, listings, ceiling and platform switch |
| `ssoJob` | scheduled, daily 02:30 UTC | School sign-in: sessions that ran out, key rotation, moved endpoints, client secrets about to expire |

Every role or organization change goes through these functions, which set the claims and write an `audit` entry. Clients can never grant themselves access. A change that takes power away (a lower role, leaving or changing organization) writes the claims before the profile and revokes the account's refresh tokens (`writeClaims`), and every privileged callable checks the claims Auth holds now (`freshCaller`, which also refuses a disabled staff account), not the ones in the caller's token. The teachers and admins of a suspended organization, or one being deleted, carry the claim `frozen`, which the rules read (from the token and from the profile). The callables go by the organization's status itself: every callable an organization's admin reaches refuses them with `org-suspended` or `org-deleting` while it lasts (`requireOrgAdmin`, `orgMembers.ts`, and `refuseClosedOrg` in `closedOrg.ts` for publishing, unpublishing and deleting courses, course visibility, deleting or exporting a managed account, the organization's audit log and its web addresses); platform admins still act. Every refusal carries a stable `details.reason`, which the screens word in the person's language. A callable the server lets run past the SDK's 70 seconds is listed in `SLOW` in `apps/web/src/firebase.ts` (`tests/callable-timeouts.test.ts` reads `functions/src` and fails when one is missing). `functions/src/admin.ts` initializes the Admin SDK once and sets the region and instance cap for every function.

## Organization branding

An organization chooses its name and logo, and its members see them where the app shows Darsi's brand: the app bar on every screen, the nav rail (with a small "with Darsi" credit), certificates (the org's logo first, Darsi's mark beside it, the org as issuer), Me's account section and the browser tab's title.

- **Editing:** `admin/OrgBranding.tsx`, on the org dashboard (/admin) and on a platform admin's org page (/super/orgs/:id). The picker takes PNG, JPEG or WebP (not SVG, which `createImageBitmap` can't decode). The logo is shrunk on the admin's device to at most 256 px, WebP where the browser can write it (PNG otherwise, transparency kept) and under 100 KB, then uploaded to `orgs/{orgId}/brand/logo-<random>.<ext>` with a one-year cache. Saving writes `name` and `brand {logo, v + 1}` and deletes the previous file.
- **On members' phones:** `state/orgBrand.ts` (`useOrgBrand`, called by App) reads `orgs/{orgId}` once per sign-in, with no listener. The logo is fetched only when `brand.v` changed, and kept in the person's local store as a data: URL (`state.org`), so it shows offline and costs nothing after the first download. Leaving the org (or signing out) brings Darsi's look back.
- **In the design system:** `BrandProvider` (App sets it from `state.org`), `useBrand` and `BrandMark` (the org's logo, or Darsi's mark). `AppBar` and `CertificateCard` use them, so screens need no changes.
- **Not branded:** the installed app's name and icon (the web manifest is the same for everyone), Darsi's landing page (an organization's own web address has its own: [Organization sites](#organization-sites-custom-web-addresses)), and the classroom kits (PDF/PPTX built ahead of time).

## Organization sites (custom web addresses)

An organization can have its own web address, such as learn.school.edu.eg under its own domain (or cis.darsiai.org, which Darsi set up; 2026-11-08). Anyone who opens it sees the organization's name and logo, before signing in and after, and platform admins choose whether Darsi's identity shows there ("with Darsi") or not at all. Every address is served by the same Hosting site and the same build as darsiai.org, so the app works out at run time which organization a host belongs to.

- **Asking and approving** (2026-11-09; ownership, access and the rest of the security pass 2026-11-10): an org admin asks on `/admin` ("Web address", `OrgSitePanel` in `admin/Domains.tsx`) for an address under the organization's own domain by typing it (what is pasted is cut to a host: `https://Learn.School.edu/x` is `learn.school.edu`), and sees where each one stands; an organization holds at most 3, asked for or connected (refused ones don't count). The request carries its own ownership token (`proof`: 32 random hex characters), and the panel shows at once the two records to add, each value with a Copy button: the ownership record `_darsi.<address>` TXT `darsi-site=<token>` and the CNAME to `darsi-f0685.web.app`. They can go in while the request waits. Org admins can't ask for an address under darsiai.org (`site-zone`: Darsi runs that DNS, and a lookalike there would sit on Darsi's own domain) or for a domain itself (`site-apex`, by the public suffix list, `tldts` in the functions: every page sends HSTS with `includeSubDomains`, which on `school.edu.eg` would force HTTPS on all of the school's other subdomains for two years). A platform admin decides on `/super/domains` ("Web addresses" in the nav; `DomainsPage`, in the SuperAdmin chunk; `/super` says how many wait): **Approve and connect**, with "Show Darsi's identity" (on by default), **Refuse** with an optional reason the organization sees, or **Add an address** for an organization that didn't ask (`?org=` presets it, from "Manage web addresses" on `/super/orgs/:id`), under darsiai.org too. An organization's own request keeps its token when approved; an address a platform admin adds, or takes over from another organization's request, gets a new one, so another organization's record proves nothing. Each approved address shows its six steps, the DNS records still needed, what Hosting says and Google's own error words, with **Check now**, the identity switch and **Remove** (inline confirmation). Everything goes through one callable, `manageSite` (`{action: request | cancel | check | approve | refuse | identity | remove, host, …}`); its refusals (`site-host`, `site-reserved`, `site-taken`, `site-limit`, `site-state`, `site-zone`, `site-apex`, `no-site`, `not-super`, `not-org-admin`, `no-org`, `org-inactive`, `site-action`) show in the admin's language (`admin/refusals.ts`). **`site-taken` is the one answer for an address that is another organization's**, whichever action met it (asking for it, checking it, cancelling its request, approving it for someone else); `not-yours` is an account's refusal and is no longer sent for an address (`siteRefusal` in `admin/Domains.tsx` still words it the same way, for an older server). `setOrgSite` stays for phones with the older app and does what approve and remove do.
- **Connecting** (`functions/src/domains.ts`, as the functions' own service account, an Editor of the project: nobody runs a script or opens a console). The six steps (`SiteSteps`), each ok, wait, todo or error:
  - **owner**: Darsi's own DNS lookup (`lookupDns`: Google's resolvers, the CNAME chain and the TXT values) finds the organization's ownership record with its token. Always done under darsiai.org. An address still pointing at Darsi after its organization left can't be connected for anyone else.
  - **dns**: the lookup finds the CNAME to `darsi-f0685.web.app`, and Hosting sees the address point at it (hostState `HOST_ACTIVE`, ownershipState `OWNERSHIP_ACTIVE`; `…_CONFLICT`, another Firebase site holding it, is an error). Darsi asks for a CNAME at the address itself, never Hosting's A record (an A record says only "Firebase", not which site); Hosting's records for other names (its certificate's) and the ones it says to remove are shown too. Hosting alone isn't enough: it doesn't look at an active domain again (cis.darsiai.org's record hasn't changed since it was added).
  - **hosting**: the address is a custom domain of the Hosting site (REST v1beta1), added only once owner is done, so Darsi never claims an address in Hosting for an organization that didn't show it runs it (which could block its real owner). **cert**: `CERT_ACTIVE` or `CERT_EXPIRING_SOON`; `CERT_EXPIRED` is an error.
  - **auth** and **cors**: Firebase Auth's authorized domains (Identity Toolkit admin v2) and, as `https://<host>`, the bucket's CORS list (JSON API), given exactly while owner and dns are done (`hasAccess` in `sitesCore.ts`) and taken back as soon as either isn't: a domain that lapses and is bought by someone else, or is pointed elsewhere, can't sign people in to Darsi (or receive their Google sign-in) from the next check on.
  All done: `live`; one failed: `problem`; else `connecting`. A DNS lookup that fails (timeout, SERVFAIL) keeps the last verdict rather than act on no answer.
- **Sign-in and file access as a whole** (`syncSiteAccess` in `sites.ts`, `syncAccess` in `domains.ts`): one function sets both lists from the addresses that should have them now and takes out every other organization address (whoever put it there, `connect-site.mjs` included) and every address Darsi put there before; entries that aren't an organization's (darsiai.org, localhost, Hosting's own) are never touched. `config/siteAccess` `{hosts, lockUntil, lockedBy}` lists what Darsi put there (written before Google's lists change, so a run that stops halfway forgets nothing) and is the lock that lets one run go at a time across instances (held 90 s at most; another waits up to 30 s, then leaves it to the next run). The CORS write is conditional on the bucket's metageneration and tried again on 412. Every change to an address (approve, Check now, remove, a deleted organization) runs it after its own write, and `sitesJob` runs it every 15 minutes, which mends whatever changed the lists in between.
- **DNS** is the one part outside. An organization adds its two records at its own DNS provider ("ask whoever manages the DNS of <domain>"). An address under darsiai.org is a platform admin's to add on `/super/domains` (no ownership record: Darsi runs that DNS); its card shows the CNAME to add in Cloudflare, DNS only. There is no wildcard record (`connect-site.mjs --wildcard` exists but isn't used): each darsiai.org address is added on its own.
- **Turning live:** `sitesJob` (every 15 minutes) checks and mends every approved address that isn't live, and live ones every hour, then sets sign-in and file access: an address turns live by itself when its certificate is out, and loses sign-in within the hour if its DNS no longer shows it is Darsi's. **Check now** does the same at once for one address (at most every 15 seconds). An address approved before ownership tokens gets one at its next check. The job's first run (2026-11-09) moved setOrgSite's old `config/sites` list into `sites/{host}` (cis.darsiai.org) and deleted the list.
- **Removing** disconnects the Hosting domain (Hosting keeps it 30 days), leaves DNS alone, forgets the address, takes it out of the authorized domains and the CORS list, and publishes `sites.json` again; what couldn't be undone comes back as warnings, shown with the "Removed" message (the next run of `sitesJob` tries again). A deleted organization's addresses are removed the same way (`onOrgSite`), so another organization can have them.
- **The voice relay** accepts `https://*.darsiai.org` (`TUTOR_ORIGINS`) and any other address that may sign people in now (`hasAccess`), from one query of `sites` read again at most every two minutes, never per origin, so made-up origins cost nothing and can't crowd real ones out (`siteOrigins` in `functions/src/origins.ts`). A failed read keeps the last list and is tried again 15 seconds later. No relay deploy per address.
- **The rules for a host** (`content/sites.ts`, shared with the functions): a lowercase hostname of at least two labels whose last has a letter (so no IP address), at most 253 characters, never Darsi's own (`darsiai.org`, `www.darsiai.org`) or Hosting's (`*.web.app`, `*.firebaseapp.com`). `isDarsiHost` also counts `localhost`, `*.localhost` and IP addresses, which the app never looks up. The functions also refuse names kept for Darsi under darsiai.org (`isReservedName` in `sitesCore.ts`: api, app, admin, auth, login, signin, signup, account(s), mail, email, smtp, support, help, status, docs, blog, static, cdn, assets, media, dev, staging, test, pay, billing, security). That list is in the functions only: the app's startup bundle holds `content/sites.ts` whole, since a lazy chunk shares it.
- **The data:** `sites/{host}` (`SiteDoc` in `content/sites.ts`, shared with the functions): `{host, orgId, orgName, darsi, status (requested | refused | connecting | live | problem), requestedBy, requestedAt, approvedBy, approvedAt, refusedReason, steps, records, issues, error, checkedAt, liveAt, proof}`, times in ms; `proof` is the ownership token (null under darsiai.org). The document id is the host, so an address belongs to one organization (a refused one is free again). Only the functions write it (`manageSite`, `sitesJob`, `onOrgSite`); platform admins read them all, an org admin its own organization's (a query on `orgId`), and members none (rules). `config/siteAccess` (functions only; platform admins may read it) lists the addresses Darsi put in Google's lists, with the lock. `orgs/{orgId}.site` and `config/sites` are what setOrgSite wrote before 2026-11-09: nothing reads them now and no client writes them.
- **The public file** `content/sites.json` (public, `max-age=60`, gzipped like the other JSON files): `{ v (when it was published, ms), sites: { "<host>": { orgId, name, logo (a public https URL, or null), darsi } } }`. An organization's own logo (`orgs/{orgId}/brand/…`) is readable by its members only, so it is copied to `content/sites/{orgId}/{sha256 of its bytes, 20 hex}.{webp|png|jpg|jpeg}` (public, one-year immutable cache) and the file points at the copy (`publicFileUrl`: Firebase Storage's REST address, which answers every origin). Copies nothing lists any more are deleted at each publish. It lists every approved address (connecting, live or problem), so an address shows its organization as soon as it answers. `manageSite` publishes the file when an address is approved, changes its identity or is removed, and `onOrgSite` when an organization with an address is renamed, re-branded or deleted (it also keeps `orgName` on its addresses current). Storage refuses two changes to one file within about a second (429), so a publish tries again up to four times, waiting 1.2 s and then twice as long each time.
- **On a phone** (`state/site.ts`, startup code, no Firebase): on a host that isn't Darsi's own, the app reads `content/sites.json` once per start with a plain `fetch` (`loadSitesFile` in `content/load.ts`: network-first, the last copy in `darsi-content` answering offline or after 2.5 s). The host's entry is kept in localStorage (`darsi:site:<host>`), the logo as a data: URL (at most 160 KB, PNG, WebP or JPEG), so the next start shows it at once, offline too, while the file is re-read behind it; a change shows as soon as it arrives. Only a first visit waits for the file (the loading screen), for at most 8 s, after which Darsi's look shows until it comes. Darsi's own hosts read nothing. On this machine, `?site=<host>` on localhost stands in for a host until the tab closes (`?site=` stops it), for testing on the local stack; on a real address it is ignored.
- **The brand** (`identityOf`): a member of an organization sees their own, as before (on their organization's site, its published name and logo fill in what their phone doesn't have yet); anyone else on a site sees the site's. Whether Darsi's identity shows is the site's choice, and it always shows on Darsi's own addresses. App passes the brand to the design system's `BrandProvider` (`logo`, `name`, `darsi`), so the app bar, the nav rail ("with Darsi" only when it shows), certificates (Darsi's small mark only when it shows) and `BrandMark` everywhere follow it. Without a logo and without Darsi's identity, `BrandMark` shows the name's first letter instead of Darsi's mark. The window title is "<page> — <organization>", or the organization's name alone where Darsi's was.
- **Where it shows:** the app bar, the nav rail, the window title, the landing page (`SiteLanding` in `landing/Landing.tsx`: the logo, the name, "with Darsi" when it shows, then Sign in, Create an account and Continue without an account, with none of Darsi's marketing sections), the sign-in and sign-up screens (the logo and the name above the title), onboarding's welcome (the logo and the name) and certificates.
- **Without Darsi's identity**, `I18nProvider` gets the organization's name (`brand`), and `t()` puts it, isolated, wherever a UI string says "Darsi" or «درسي» (`rebrand`). It does so before the variables go in, so a name someone typed is never changed. That covers every string of the app and of its lazy chunks (admin, editor, tour, tutor, Super): "A new version of CIS Training Platform is ready". Kept as written: "Darsi Tiny" («درسي الصغير»), a model's name that the labs and courses use too; the "Darsi skills" framework name; the branding panel's hints and the web address panel's strings, which are about Darsi's identity; `/licences`, which keeps naming Darsi's software; and course and lab content, which is authored material (lessons and narrated videos that mention Darsi still do).
- **Not per organization:** the web manifest and icons (the installed app is named Darsi, with Darsi's icon), `index.html`'s title and description until the app starts, push notification wording, Firebase Auth's emails (password reset), and payment return addresses (`SITE_URL`, darsiai.org). Auth keeps a session per origin, so people sign in again on an organization's address.
- **On the emulators** the functions never call Google or real DNS: they go to a stand-in (`scripts/local/domains.mjs` on 127.0.0.1:4798, started by `npm run local`; `DOMAINS_API` overrides where the calls go). An address under darsiai.org points at Darsi 5 seconds after Hosting gets it and has its certificate 15 seconds after; any other waits until `GET http://127.0.0.1:4798/dns/<host>?txt=darsi-site=<token>` stands in for its organization adding both records (`?off=1` takes them away, to see sign-in taken back). `GET /resolve/<host>` answers Darsi's lookup. The emulator doesn't run `sitesJob`; Check now does its work.
- **Tests:** `functions/test/sites.test.ts` (the host rules and kept names; `manageSite`'s every action and refusal, darsiai.org and domain-itself refusals, ownership tokens, `setOrgSite`, `sitesJob` with the old list moved in, and `onOrgSite`, with an in-memory Firestore and Storage and the Google calls stood in; sign-in given and taken back as DNS changes, the sweep of addresses nothing lists, an address added by hand, the lock; the logo copies; a 429 retry; Hosting's states, the list planners), `functions/test/domains.test.ts` (Darsi's DNS lookup with a stand-in resolver; Hosting only after the ownership record; the CNAME asked for; an address Hosting still calls active but DNS sends elsewhere; `syncAccess` with the conditional CORS write; against a stand-in that keeps their state), `functions/test/origins.test.ts` and `relay.test.ts` (the relay's origins: the list read every two minutes, only addresses that may sign in), `apps/web/tests/domains.test.tsx` (the org admin's panel with both records, `/super/domains`, the org page's summary and `/super`'s count, in English and Arabic), `apps/web/tests/sites.test.tsx` (`siteHost`, the lookup and what it keeps, `identityOf`, `rebrand`, the site landing page with and without Darsi's identity), `apps/web/tests/site-host.test.tsx` (the real App on a site host without Darsi's identity: the landing page, the title, the account screens and onboarding, with no Darsi name or mark and no Firebase for a guest), the design system's brand tests, and rules cases in `tests-rules/security.rules.test.ts` (`sites/{host}`, and nobody writing `site` on an organization).

## Course visibility in organizations

An organization's admins choose which courses its teachers, students and classes see (2026-11-08). Without a choice, everyone sees every course for them, as before.

- **The choice** (`orgs/{orgId}.courses`, `content/orgCourses.ts`, shared with the functions): `{ mode: "all" }` (also when the field is absent or can't be read), or `{ mode: "chosen", teachers, students, groups }`, lists of catalog course ids (`COURSE_ID`, the editor's `UNIT_ID`). A teacher sees the `teachers` list, a student the `students` list, and both also their class's (`groups[profile.group]`). Org admins and platform admins always see everything. The lists narrow and never widen: each person still sees only courses for their audience (students' courses for students, university courses for university teachers), in visible tracks. Bounds keep the document small, since every member reads it: ≤ 300 ids a list, ≤ 60 classes, ≤ 2,000 ids in all, class names as the people pages save them (≤ 60 characters, trimmed).
- **Saving:** only `setOrgCourses` (its admins and platform admins) writes the field; the rules refuse it from every client. It stores the choice sorted, without repeats or empty classes, and audits the counts. Refusals carry a `details.reason` (`courses-*`), which `admin/refusals.ts` puts in the admin's language.
- **On members' phones:** `state/orgBrand.ts` reads the org document once per sign-in or app start, as for branding (no extra read, no listener), and keeps only this member's list (`seesFor`: role and class, sorted) in `state.org.courses`, so it holds offline. It waits for the profile (the class) first, and reads again only if the role or class changes. A narrowed list reaches people at their next sign-in or app start; the admin screen says so. Signing out forgets it with the rest of `state.org`.
- **Applied** (`content/mine.ts`): `useOrgSees()` (null for admins and without a choice), `useMine().isMine` (Home, Kit, Downloads), `offered` (the visible tracks with a ready course for them: onboarding's track step, My tracks, the track switcher; every visible track until the catalog has been read), `usePlacement` and `useStagesOffered` (only levels and school stages with a visible course). With nothing chosen for them, Home says their organization chooses their courses, and onboarding lets them carry on. The catalog is read for this only when a choice applies (`useUnitsWhen`), so other learners pay nothing.
- **What stays:** finished courses keep their results, certificates and progress (Me and the reports don't filter). A course saved for offline that is now hidden stays in Downloads so it can be removed. A lesson link to a hidden course still opens: this narrows what people are offered, it isn't access control (public courses are public, and every member can read the org's own courses through Storage's rules). Labs are not courses and are unaffected.
- **The admin screen** (`admin/OrgCourses.tsx`, a lazy staff chunk): `/admin/courses` for org admins ("Courses" in the nav, a panel on the dashboard saying what holds now, and a stop in the tour), and the same panel on `/super/orgs/:orgId` for platform admins.
  - Two choices at the top: "Everyone sees every course" or "Choose which courses each group sees". The first time an organization chooses, every course starts ticked for all teachers and all students, so nobody loses everything before the admin has unticked what they shouldn't see; new courses (Darsi's or the organization's own) then stay hidden until ticked.
  - The courses: Darsi's catalog and the organization's own (an org admin's catalog already holds them; a platform admin's page reads `orgs/{orgId}/catalog.json` once), by visible track, with level and audience labels and a search box. Columns: all teachers, all students, and each class among the members (read once, like the other admin screens; a class a saved choice still names stays, marked "No one in this class now"), each with how many people it covers and how many courses are ticked. A column offers only courses for its people: all teachers the teachers' courses, all students the students', a class the courses for the roles in it (anyone's when it is empty); elsewhere a dash. Ids the screen doesn't list (a course unpublished for now) stay as they were saved.
  - From 900 px a grid of checkboxes, with "all in this track" helpers (tri-state) per column, the course column staying in view while classes scroll sideways inside the table. Narrower: who first (a button per column), then a checklist by track with the same helper.
  - A save bar stays in reach (above the bottom nav and the round Report button), saying whether everything is saved; "Saved" says when people see it. With unsaved changes, closing the tab asks, and a tap on a link or the app bar's Back is held while the bar asks on the page (Save and leave, Leave without saving, Stay here). The browser's own Back button isn't held (the app's router has no navigation blocker).
- **Tests:** `functions/test/orgCourses.test.ts` (the checks and bounds, `seesFor`, the callable with an in-memory Firestore), `apps/web/tests/org-courses.test.tsx` (`brandUpdate`, Home and `useMine` per role and class, onboarding's stages, tracks and placement, the panel on a phone and on a wide screen saving the right shape, leaving with changes, a refusal), and a rules case in `tests-rules/security.rules.test.ts`.

## Organization analytics

An organization's report on its students and teachers, against international standards. TECHNICAL.md's [Analytics](#analytics) is the platform's.

- **Where:** `/admin/analytics` (org admins, a button on the dashboard and "Analytics" in the nav), `/super/orgs/:orgId/report` (platform admins) and `/teach/analytics` (a teacher, for their own students). One component, `admin/OrgReport.tsx` (`mode="teacher"`), a lazy chunk.
- **Data:** `users/*.summary` only, which the member list already holds, so the report costs no extra reads. A teacher's report reads their students (`useMyStudents`) and their own profile, as the rules allow.
- **The summary** (`functions/src/summary.ts`, shared by the trigger, `npm run resummarize` and the demo script) carries per competency the evidence (lessons) and the accuracy summed, per track the lessons, accuracy and time, and the total time. `npm run resummarize` rebuilds every summary after the fields change.
- **Graded lessons only (2026-11-06):** a lesson with nothing marked right or wrong (a project, a capstone stage, a reading) records 100%, which says nothing. Its row carries `graded: false` (from the published unit, `lessonGraded` in `courses.ts`), and the summary counts it in lessons, XP, time, courses and weeks but not in accuracy, competency evidence or a track's accuracy (`graded`, `tracks[].g`). Screens use `scoredOf(summary)` (`auth/types.ts`) for "has a score" (no score: "—"; it never makes someone "need support"), person pages and course results show such a lesson as done, without a percentage, and `lesson_complete` sends no `acc` for it. Run `npm run resummarize` once after deploying, so older rows learn which lessons are ungraded.
- **Standards** (`content/standards.ts`): each framework is a list of items, each item the competency codes that give evidence for it.
  - UNESCO AI competency frameworks for students and for teachers (2024), and DigComp 2.2: the lessons' own codes.
  - ISTE Standards for Students (2016) and for Educators (2017), and the CSTA K–12 CS Standards (2017): Darsi's crosswalk at the level of their standards and core concepts. The screen says it is not an official alignment.
  - Darsi strands: IoT and the newer tracks.
  - A test checks every code exists and every UNESCO, DigComp and Darsi code appears somewhere.
- **Strength** per person and item, from the lessons that count for it (`strength`): not yet, emerging (1), developing (2), secure (3+), with the average score on them.
- **The report** (`admin/orgStats.ts`, pure and tested):
  - Filters: students, teachers or everyone; class; teacher. Tiles: people and activity, lessons, average score, learning time.
  - Status: on track, needs support (average under 60% over 3 lessons or more), inactive (nothing for 14 days), not started. Choosing one filters the people table.
  - Lessons per week (12 weeks), the chosen framework's items (reached, strength bar, score), tracks, courses (started and completed), classes, and every person with their status and secure items.
  - Org admins also get a Teachers table: each teacher's own learning (UNESCO for teachers) beside their students'. A teacher's own report shows their own learning instead.
  - Export to Excel: summary, people (with their strength on each item), the framework's items, classes and courses.
  - The People tab shows 100 rows, then "Show more", and works out each person's secure items only for the rows on screen, cached per list and framework, so a search, a re-sort or "Show more" counts only rows not counted before.
- **The person page** shows learning time and a standards panel; it opens on the framework with the most evidence for that person.
  - Its numbers, capabilities and standards come from `users/{uid}.summary`, which counts only published lessons with their competencies from the course itself. The lesson results (the phone's word) only list which of a course's real lessons were done.
  - It downloads only the courses the person has results or projects in, each on its own (`Promise.allSettled`), not every course. A course unpublished since is listed as no longer offered under its title from `admin/courseTitles.json` (every course in `content/units` and `content/retired`: id → title in English and Arabic, track, level; written by `node apps/web/scripts/course-titles.mjs`, which must be run again after a course is added, renamed or retired, or `tests/course-titles.test.ts` fails). The report and the /super pages use it too. Only `src/admin` imports it (~4 KB gzipped, in a staff-only shared chunk, never precached); /results doesn't, since importing it there merged it with WorkBody's chunk under a name the precache rules don't know. A course that isn't in it (an organization's own) shows its id.
  - On your own page there is no role picker, password reset, Remove or deletion (the server refuses them all). Remove asks on the page, not in a browser dialog. Removing or deleting someone goes back to the list, which says it was done.
- **While a list loads**, the staff screens say so (`useOnce`'s `loading` in `admin/data.ts`): never "0" or "No people yet", which once read as an empty school and invited a second import.
- **Exports** (the people CSV, the report and the credentials workbook) carry no invisible direction marks (`plainText`, `cleanBook` in `admin/importer.ts`); role names are the translated, capitalised ones (`rn.*`). The people CSV exports the rows shown, and its button says how many when a search or role is picked. The credentials workbook's sheet is named in the admin's language and reads right to left in Arabic.
- **Charts** (`admin/charts.tsx` `Bars`) caption the busiest day or week, never their tallest bar as if it were a total, and say there is nothing yet instead of drawing an empty chart.
- **The org dashboard's capability coverage** lists the competencies at least one member has evidence for, from the members' summaries it already holds: no course files are downloaded.
- **Adding and importing people:** `createMembers` gets 25 people per call, teachers and admins first so later calls can link their students. If a call fails, the accounts already made stay on screen with their passwords, and "Try the rest again" sends only the rows never sent. The client waits as long as the function may run (540 s). A call whose answer was lost (a dropped connection, a timeout) may still have made its rows; before sending again a row with no login of its own (the server would make up a second login), the app reads once the accounts that admin made in the organization since (`madeSince`, `createdBy`) and lists a match by name, role and class as "Made before the connection dropped" instead (`alreadyMade`). Results and the credentials workbook number rows as the spreadsheet does, and the slips promise a new password only when the admin asked for one.
- **Several people at once:** the dashboard's people table has checkboxes to set a class or a teacher for everyone selected (batched writes the org-admin rule allows).
- **Reviewing projects:** the card (`admin/SubmissionCard.tsx`; its body, `admin/WorkBody.tsx`, is shared with /results) shows everything the learner sent: the text; a capstone milestone's name (its prompt's first line), repository, links, checks and AI review by its rubric's criteria; a final project's or milestone's explanation and AI use; and every file.
  - Files open in the card, read through the Storage SDK with the reviewer's own sign-in (`evidencePath`, `shownType`: photos, sound and mp4 show; anything else is only saved). New projects store addresses without a download token (`lesson/ProjectView.tsx`); older token links are never shown.
  - /teach and /admin show each person's projects waiting for a review (`projectsWaiting` on the profile, recounted by the submission triggers in `functions/src/waiting.ts`, so the lists need no extra reads): a tile with the total, a column, and a "To review" sort, the default. /teach can also show only the students with projects to review, or only those who need support. `npm run resummarize` fills the count in for projects sent before it existed.
- **Demo data:** `npm run demo-org` (`--emulator` locally) makes "Nile Valley Demo School" (`orgs/demo-school`, `demo: true`): an admin, 4 teachers and 48 students in 4 classes with 12 weeks of lessons from the real courses. It is the same on every run, and `--remove` deletes it. The admin's, a teacher's and a student's logins go to `.demo-org.local.json` (gitignored).

## Platform administration

Platform admins manage every organization and account (`admin/OrgsAdmin.tsx`, the functions in `functions/src/platform.ts`, all audited):
- **/super/orgs:** every organization with its status and members, and "New organization" (name, type, country, city, and optionally its admin by email or username).
- **An organization's page** (/super/orgs/:id), under "Manage this organization": its details (and "Make active" for a pending or rejected one); "Add a person" (any account by email, username or the start of the name, as student, teacher or org admin; someone in another organization moves); each member's role, or Remove (back to an independent learner); and deleting it after typing its name.
- **A person's page** (/super/people/:uid): "Role and organization" sets any role, platform admin included, with the organization where the role needs one.
- **Data requests and abuse:** see [Accounts: data, deletion and suspension](#accounts-data-deletion-and-suspension) below.
- **An organization's own courses:** the lesson editor's list (/super/content) has "Show the courses of" for platform admins: Darsi's, or any active organization's, to open, unpublish or delete one.
- **Everything learners see and make** (2026-11-04):
  - **Every course:** `useMine().all` (`content/mine.ts`) gives platform admins every track and every course, for teachers, for students and for university teachers. Hidden tracks are included. Learn opens on "All tracks" (`AllCourses` in `screens/Home.tsx`): every course, track by track, one line each from the catalog, and a course is loaded only when it is opened. Picking one track in the switcher lists that track's courses from Beginner up, each labelled with who it is for; the admin's pick is `adminTrack`, kept apart from the learner's `track`. Kit, Downloads and My tracks follow `isMine`; Kit has every kit open for them, one line per course by track, loaded when opened (`AllKits` in `screens/Kit.tsx`). They can also open any lesson and any step: the course path's lessons stay open for them (`PathNode.open`), and the lesson player (`explore` in `screens/LessonScreen.tsx`) has a "Go to step" list and Skip on questions, and Continue doesn't wait for a video. A lesson they jumped or skipped through isn't recorded as finished. `?step=project` opens a lesson at its project, and `?step=<step id>` at that step: a project's lesson title links to the very step it answers (a capstone's milestone) on /super/projects and person pages. Nobody else gets any of this.
  - **/super/projects** (`admin/AllWork.tsx`, in the SuperAdmin chunk): every project anyone submitted, newest first. "Waiting for review" is the default, "All projects" the other view, 50 at a time. Reviewing uses the same card as a person's page (`admin/SubmissionCard.tsx`).
    - It is a collection-group query on `submissions`. The rule `match /{path=**}/submissions/{subId}` allows reads only to platform admins.
    - It needs the `submissions (status, createdAt desc)` collection-group index and a collection-group single-field index on `createdAt` (`firestore.indexes.json`).
  - **/super/certificates:** everyone who finished a course, worked out from `users/*.summary.units` against the catalog's lesson counts. The 300 accounts with the most lessons are read first, with "Show more" for the rest.
    - Opening a certificate reads that person's progress in the course for the finishing date. The card and its code are the ones the learner sees in Me (`content/cert.ts`).

### Accounts: data, deletion and suspension

`functions/src/account.ts`; who may do what is `accountActionProblem` in `roles.ts`, pure and tested; all audited.

- **A managed account** (`managedAccount` in `roles.ts`; `orgMembersCore.isManaged` asks the same for one organization; `managedByOrg` in `admin/data.ts` on the screens): the profile's `managedBy` names the organization, or, with no `managedBy`, its email is a username login (`<name>@users.darsi.app`). `createMembers` sets `managedBy` on the username logins it makes, and school sign-in on the accounts it makes. `createdBy` alone gives an organization nothing: a teacher whose real email an admin typed keeps their own account.
- **Who may export or delete:**

  | Caller | May |
  |---|---|
  | The person | Export: always. Delete: unless an admin (`self-staff`: hand the role over first), or managed and in an organization (`self-org`: its admin deletes it). Deleting needs a sign-in in the last 10 minutes and no Stripe subscription that renews by itself |
  | A platform admin | Anyone but themself and other platform admins |
  | An org admin | A student or teacher who is its member and whose account it manages |
  | Anyone else | Nobody (`not-yours`) |

- **`exportAccountData`** gives the person and platform admins everything Darsi keeps about the account: the Auth account, profile and notification settings, lessons and the rows the summary counts, projects, certificates, course access, notes, devices (without the push token), tutor credits and subscription, payments and checkouts, tutor usage, the tutor's conversations (from Agent Runtime, 20 s at most; `complete: false` when that was not enough), problem reports in full, organization requests, and the audit entries about the person (500 at most; an entry someone else made comes without that person's address). An org admin gets the learning record only: account, profile, notifications, lessons, projects, certificates and course access. One download per account every 10 minutes (`data-wait`, with `retryAt`; the stamp is `users/{uid}/private/export`, checked and written in one transaction before anything is read, the person's own downloads and staff's counted apart): each one asks Agent Runtime, whose quota the live tutor shares, and writes an audit entry.
- **Erasure** (`wipeAccount`, behind `deleteAccount`), in this order: the sign-in is switched off; the profile goes before anything under it (so `onProgressWrite` writes nothing back); then everything under `users/{uid}`; the person's project files in each organization's folder; the export files of their organizations (`exports/{orgId}/`, which name every member: the stamp `orgs/{orgId}/private/export` is deleted first, always, so an export under way stops writing, then the files, so an admin can make a new one at once; `exportFiles` in the audit entry, `exportsLeft: true` when Storage refused). Only organizations the server vouches for lose their export: the profile's, the deletion queue's and each project's own `orgId`. A folder named only inside a project's file link is used for the person's own files and nothing else; credits, subscription, course access, join tries, capstone limits and tutor leases; the tutor's conversations on Agent Runtime (60 s at most; a failure never stops the rest, and the audit entry then says `sessionsFailed: true`, with `sessionsQueued: true` once they are on the deletion queue below); tutor usage; problem reports with their screenshots (found by the verified sender and by the account a phone named in older reports); organization requests (one never approved goes whole; an organization that runs keeps its document, loses `private/request`, and its `createdBy` becomes a hash); payments and checkouts stay for the accounts with the uid replaced by `deleted-<hash>`; the member count drops and a teacher's pupils are released; the person comes off the removed lists of organizations that removed them (found through the `removeMember` and bulk `remove` audit entries, so a removal whose entry retention already deleted is missed; a list whose school sign-in tombstone stays is left; `unlisted` in the audit entry, and a failure only logs); the Auth account; and last a look through the bucket for stray files (60 s at most; `leftoversChecked: false` in the audit entry when it did not finish). Afterwards the account's id appears nowhere but the audit log (`functions/test/accountWipe.test.ts` holds it to that, and to parity with the export). Other people's records that name the account (`reviewedBy` on a project, `createdBy` on members an admin made) stay; the id leads to nobody.
- **The deletion queue** (`accountDeletions/{uid}` = `{orgId, by, at}`; no client access): deleting an organization with "delete the accounts it made" queues each managed account, and the trigger `onAccountDeletion` (retried, 540 s, 512 MiB, at most 5 instances) erases each as `deleteAccount` would, audited with the entry's `via` (`deleteOrg` by default; `sso-link` when school sign-in replaced an empty school-made account on link). On its first try it skips, and drops from the queue, a platform admin and an account that is no longer managed or is another organization's by now. An account with a Stripe subscription that renews by itself is **held**: the document is kept with `held: "renewing"` for a platform admin. A retry finishes an erasure without asking again; when only the tutor's conversations are left the document is marked `wiped`. Six hours after it was queued the worker gives up (`gaveUpAt`, the document kept, an error in the log). The worker's own fields: `startedAt`, `wiped`, `held`, `gaveUpAt`. The worker runs only on an entry's creation, so a direct `deleteAccount` that has to queue the tutor's conversations for an account whose entry no worker is behind any more (`queueStopped` in `accountCore.ts`: held, given up, or queued over six hours ago) deletes that entry and creates it again (`wiped: true`, `via: "deleteAccount"`, `replaced: held | gave-up | expired`), which starts a worker and a fresh six hours; an entry a worker is still retrying is only marked `wiped`. A repeated `deleteOrg` call never queues an account twice.
- **The tutor's conversations** (`agentSessions.ts`): listed, exported and deleted through the agent's `:query` endpoint as the service account, one call after another, since each is one of Agent Runtime's roughly 30 queries a minute for the whole project. With no `agentId` in `config/tutor` it does nothing. `node scripts/purge-agent-sessions.mjs` lists conversations not used for `--days` (90 by default) for every profile or one `--uid`, and deletes them only with `--apply` (`--agent <id>` for another agent); nothing schedules it.
- **On the screens:** "Account and data" in a person's Account tab (`admin/AccountActions.tsx`): "Download their data", "Delete account and data" (typed confirmation), and for platform admins "Suspend account" / "Restore account" with a reason (`setAccountSuspended`: Auth `disabled`, sessions revoked, `suspended` on the profile; signing in then says the account is suspended; a suspended person can no longer change their profile or send a project). "Your data" in Me (`account/MyData.tsx`): "Download my data" for everyone signed in, "Delete my account and my data" for an account the person made, and "Leave <organization>" (`leaveOrg`) for a member who signed up themself.
- **Problem reports** name an account only from a verified token: `details.account` is `{uid}` and nothing else (the phone sends no more, and the server replaces whatever came with the token's uid), and without a token it is dropped (`ownDetails` in `bugsCore.ts`). Who sent a report is its `user`, from the token. Platform admins delete a report with its screenshot (`deleteBugReport`).
- **"Remove content"** on any project card for platform admins (`moderateSubmission`): its text, explanations, links and files go (`moderated` {by, at, reason} is set), and every reviewer then sees "Removed by Darsi staff". Its status and feedback stay.

### Course access for university teachers

`courseAccess/{uid}` = `{units [≤ 50], orgs [≤ 20 organization ids, or "none"], until (ms) | null, name, email, grantedBy, updatedAt}` (`functions/src/access.ts`). No client writes it, and no client reads other people's results through it: three callables do.

- **`grantCourseAccess`** and **`revokeCourseAccess`** (platform admins, audited; `/super/access`): a grant replaces the person's grant whole. Its organizations are fixed when it is made: sent without `orgs`, it stores the teacher's own organization as Auth holds it at that moment (`"none"` for a teacher in no organization; the answer and the audit entry, marked `own`, say which), and `/super/access` then names it. Reading goes by the stored list alone, never by where the teacher is now: they can leave, be removed or join another school with its student code, and the grant must not follow them to other people's students. A grant stored without `orgs` (made before they were stored) reads nothing until it is saved again; `npm run pin-course-access -- --apply` pins those once at release (docs/FIREBASE.md §3). Without `until` it lasts until revoked. Refusals: `bad-units` (not 1 to 50 course ids), `bad-orgs` (not 1 to 20 ids or `"none"`, or an organization that doesn't exist), `bad-until` (not a time to come, at most ten years ahead), `no-account`.
- **`courseResults({unitId})`** (120 s, 512 MiB; `/results`): for a platform admin, every organization's rows; for a teacher, only with a grant that lists the course and has not run out, and only the rows of people whose profile is in the grant's organizations now. The teacher's account is read from Auth on every call, so a disabled one is refused at once. It answers `results` (lesson, score, date), `work` (projects and capstone milestones, final projects first; never the review) and `more` when a cap was reached (2,000 results, 500 projects; for a teacher the scan goes on past other organizations' rows for up to five pages of that size, so a small faculty is not crowded out of a busy course). Without a usable grant (none, ended, another course, or one that stores no organizations) it answers `no-grant`; rows of a deleted account are dropped for teachers.
- **On the screens:** `/super/access` takes an optional last day, and that whole day is included on the admin's clock (`endOfDay` in `results/CourseAccess.tsx`); saving with no course ticked removes the grant; a grant with no stored organizations shows "None set" and asks to be saved again. On `/results`, a grant whose last day has passed shows "Your access has ended" with no course picker, and a notice says when the server reached its cap.
- **Not checked, the owner's call:** a pinned grant outlives the teacher's membership of the organization it names (nothing removes it when they leave or are removed: revoke it on `/super/access`), and `courseResults` checks no role, so a grantee who is now a student is still served within the stored organizations.
