- **Storage** opens a project's files to a granted teacher by the same grant (`grantedS` in `storage.rules`): the course, `until`, and the organization the file's owner is in now (their profile; `none` for no organization), which must be in the grant's stored `orgs` (never the token's own organization). That is what `courseResults` lists by, so a student who changed organization after uploading is listed and opened alike; the folder the file was sent into doesn't decide.

## Staff screens and tabs

The long staff pages are tabbed (`admin/Tabs.tsx`: `Tabs`, `TabPanel`, `useTab`, `useTabActive`; `styles/tabs.css`, classes `tb-`; tab names are `tabs.*` in `admin/strings`). It is staff code: no learner's phone downloads it.

- **The open tab is in the address:** `?tab=<id>`; the default tab has no parameter. Choosing a tab changes only that key (other keys, the hash and `location.state` stay) and adds a history entry, so Back returns to the tab before. An id the list doesn't have (unknown, or hidden for this role or organization) opens the default tab.
- **Panels are lazy.** A panel is not rendered until its tab is first opened, so a data hook inside a panel's own component reads nothing before that. Once opened it stays on the page, hidden and not re-rendered, so typed text stays and coming back reads nothing again; `keep={false}` takes it off the page instead (a list that should read again).
- **Tabs are links** (`a[role=tab][href]`), so each view has an address, and a leave guard that holds link clicks holds a tab too. Keyboard: one Tab stop; the arrow keys move along the list in the reading direction (`useI18n().dir`), Home and End go to its ends, Enter or Space opens the tab in focus (manual activation, so arrowing past a tab starts no reads). A tab can carry a count ("Requests 3") and a mark for unsaved changes. When the open tab changes without a click on it (a tile, Back, a tab the page stops listing) and focus was on nothing or inside the panel that closed, focus goes to the open tab.
- **A trailing action** (`<Tabs action={…}>`, optional): one control for the whole page, whatever the tab, at the inline end of the tab row instead of on a line of its own above it (`tb-action`). It is not a tab: it sits outside the tablist, after it in the Tab order, and the arrow keys never reach it. On a phone only its icon shows; its words stay as its name, so it is given both (`<Button size="sm" variant="ghost" icon="sync">Refresh</Button>`). `/admin` (Refresh: the organization and its people) and `/super/domains` (read the addresses again) use it.
- **A school sign-in return is never the hash that stays:** no tab's link and no tab change carries `#sso=…` (`kept`; see [School sign-in](#school-sign-in)).
- **In tests:** `getByRole("tab", { name })`, or start at `?tab=<id>`.

| Screen | Tabs (`?tab=`; the first is the default) |
|---|---|
| `/admin` (`AdminHome`) | `overview`, `people`, `invite` (the codes, the join rules and the removed list), `seats` (only with a licence), `look`, `signin` (school sign-in and the web address), `activity` (the organization's audit log) |
| `/super` (`SuperAdmin`) | `requests` (with a count), `announce`, `orgs`, `records` (platform admins: the activity log, payments, legal pages, data retention and status, one at a time) |
| `/super/orgs` (`OrgsAdmin`) | `all`, `new` |
| `/super/orgs/:id` (`OrgAnalytics` in `admin/Analytics.tsx`) | `overview`, `people`, `courses`, `super`, `join`, `signin` (school sign-in; a platform admin's own test of it comes back to this tab), `settings`, `records` (its activity log and its export), `status` (suspend, resume, delete); an organization being deleted shows only `overview`, `people`, `records` and `status` |
| `/super/analytics` | `overview`, `learning`, `audience`, `orgs`, `people`, `ga` |
| `/super/people/:uid`, `/admin/people/:uid`, `/teach/:uid` (`PersonDetail`) | `progress`, `courses`, `projects` (with the count waiting for review), `account` (admins only) |
| `/super/plans` | `plans`, `switches`, `check` (the revenue event) |
| `/super/domains` | `requests`, `addresses`, `add`, `refused` (when there are any), `sso` (school finder listings) |
| `/admin/analytics`, `/teach/analytics`, `/super/orgs/:id/report` (`OrgReport`) | `overview`, `standards`, `courses`, `classes`, `people`, `me` (a teacher's own learning) |
| `/super/projects` | `waiting`, `staff`, `all` |
| `/super/bugs` | `new`, `all` |
| `/super/languages/:code` | `translate`, `sheet`, `details` |

Other screens link to a tab by its id: a "to review" count in the people table opens `<person>?tab=projects`, and Back from a person goes to `/admin?tab=people`.

**Staff forms that must not lose work:**
- **The leave question** (`admin/formGuard.tsx`: `useFormGuard(dirty)` and `<LeaveQuestion>`, on `staff/useLeaveGuard`): while a form is dirty, a reload, closing the tab, a nav link and the app bar's Back are held and a question is asked on the page (Stay has the focus; "Leave anyway"; and where the form can, "Save and leave" or "Download first"). A tap on one of the page's own tabs is not held, since the edits stay in a kept panel; a question inside a hidden panel opens that panel's tab. It guards the plans and costs, an organization's name and logo, a half-written announcement, the join rules, the tracks page, the organization's courses, the legal and retention editors, and the results of Add people or Import while a row has a password that was neither downloaded nor printed. A phone's own Back gesture is not held (the router has no navigation blocker).
- **One-time passwords:** the results' download tries the Excel workbook and, if its chunk can't load, downloads `darsi-accounts-<date>.csv` with the same columns and says so. CSV cells are quoted and a cell starting with `= + - @` gets a leading `'`, except the password column, which is written exactly as it is (a changed password is a wrong password).
- **Import preview:** 200 rows at first with "Show more", "Rows to fix only" with no cap, and "Download rows to fix (CSV)".
- **Numbers are typed as text** (`admin/typedNumber.ts`): Latin, Arabic-Indic and Persian digits and `٫` as the decimal mark, in `inputMode="decimal"` text fields (a `type="number"` field refuses Arabic digits); whole numbers (an organization request's size, the capstone limits, the join rules' most uses, keeping periods, school sign-in's session length and ceiling, the seats on `/upgrade`) are `inputMode="numeric"` text fields read the same way (`latinDigits`, `typedWhole`: "1e1" or "12.5" is no whole number); an empty field is no value, never 0. A field that can't be saved as typed says so and Save waits.
- **Nothing acts on one tap that can't be undone:** removing, suspending, resetting passwords, a role change, turning Super off, sending an announcement, deleting a report or an organization each ask on the page first; one action runs at a time, and a second tap while a call is out sends nothing.
- **Many people at once** (the dashboard's people table): the actions wait for the whole list; more than 200 people go in calls of 200 one after another with "Working: n of total done"; a call that fails stops the run, what was done is said and patched into the list, and the refused people stay ticked, each with the server's reason. The table draws 100 rows and "Show n more"; search, sort, "Select everyone matching" and the CSV export work on every matching row, drawn or not.
- **Deleting an organization** (`OrgManage`): the page calls `deleteOrg` again with the answer's cursor until `done`, showing "People handled so far: n"; it stops after three answers in a row without progress and when the page is closed, keeps the place on a failure and turns the button into "Continue" with the choice locked. **Export** saves each part as `darsi-<name>-<date>-part-000n.json.gz` through the Storage SDK, and offers "Download the files again" without a second export.
- **The course editor** shows a `catalog-pending` answer as saved, not failed (the status becomes Published or draft, with "the course list follows within 15 minutes"), and reads the catalog again only after a change that fully succeeded. It words every failure with `failText(e, t)` (`cms/entStrings.ts`): an error thrown as `Said` already holds the author's language, anything else goes through `errorText`. `Field` (`cms/fields.tsx`) ties its hint to the control (`aria-describedby`). When the exceljs chunk can't load, the Languages page's Excel download says so, and the CSV beside it needs no chunk. Its full-screen preview is a real modal: the page beside it is `inert` and gets back exactly what it had, Tab wraps inside, and closing returns the focus to the button that opened it.

**Staff tables on a phone** (`.data-table` in `styles/base.css`): below 600 px each row is a card and the header row is hidden (kept for screen readers); from 600 px it is a real table that scrolls sideways inside `.table-wrap`. Without a hint each cell is a line of its own: its `data-label`, a third of the card, then its value. A table can say more on its cells, as the people table, the report's people and teachers and the standards table do: `data-cell="main"` (who or what the row is: first on the card, across it, no label), `data-cell="end"` (one small control, such as the select box, at the inline end of that first line), every other cell after the main one a short value under its label, two to a line, `data-cell="row"` (a value that needs the card's width, such as a bar, on a line of its own under the pairs) and `data-empty` (the value is only a dash: the card leaves the pair out, the table keeps its cell). The row is a wrapping flex line, so it mirrors by itself right to left.

**Shared staff parts:** `admin/PeopleTable.tsx` (the people table), `staff/InlineConfirm` (a question on the page, Cancel focused, Escape closes: no browser dialogs), `staff/errors.ts` (every refusal code the functions write, in English and Arabic, with what to do; `tests/admin-refusals.test.tsx` scans `functions/src` and fails when a code has no words, except the `sso-…` codes, which the sign-in screens word). `errorText(e, t)` is what a staff screen shows for any failed read, write or callable, never raw English: first the server's `details.reason` in the kit's own words (`REASONS`), then in `admin/refusals.ts`' words where the screen has loaded `admin/strings` (the kit doesn't load them itself: a teacher on `/results` would download 25 KB for nothing), then the SDK's code ("You're offline" when the browser says so and the code is one a dropped connection gives), then one generic sentence; the raw text goes to `console.error`, so a problem report carries it. `reasonText(reason, t)` words a code that is not a thrown error (a bulk row's reason). `staff/LoadState.tsx` has `useRead(load, deps)` (one read, again on `reload()`; a failed reload keeps the data beside the error), `<LoadState>` (loading as `role="status"`, a failure with nothing to show as `role="alert"` with Retry and never the "empty" words, older data kept under a small alert) and `<ErrorNote>`. A `listMembers` row is typed `Profile`, but its `createdAt` arrives as milliseconds and it carries a top-level `lastActive`: a screen that sorts or shows either reads them as the server sends them. The kit's own strings are `staff/strings/en.json` and `ar.json`, registered by `staff/strings.ts` when the first staff chunk that uses them loads; their keys are not in `MessageKey`, so `k()` vouches for one (`t(k("st.loading"))`). The other staff lists (`usePeople`, `useAllSubmissions`, `useLearners`, `useBugReports` in `admin/data.ts`) read with a cursor: "Show more" reads only the rows after the last one shown, `reload()` reads what is shown again from the top, and a search in `usePeople` has no cursor. `useNames` keeps each account's name in memory for the page load. Also `admin/formGuard.tsx` and `useLeaveGuard` (unsaved changes hold a tap that would leave; a link that only opens another tab of the same page is not held, and a question left unanswered when another tab is picked counts as Stay), and `admin/auditLog.ts` (the words for audit actions, the CSV download, `usePages`).

## Members

An organization's admins manage their people through callables (`functions/src/index.ts`, `orgMembers.ts`; the pure decisions in `membersCore.ts` and `orgMembersCore.ts`). Each checks the claims Auth holds now (`freshCaller`), and for an org admin also the organization's status: `org-suspended` and `org-deleting` are refused, while a platform admin still passes, to sort such an organization out. Every refusal carries `details.reason`.

- **The member list** (`listMembers({orgId, view, after, limit})` → `{members, next}`): one query ordered by document id with a `select()`, 1,000 a page by default (2,000 at most). A row holds what the lists show (name, login, role, class, teacher, projects waiting, seat, first-sign-in flag, `managed`, `suspended`, dates and a cut-down summary), never notification settings, levels, tracks or plan fields. `view: "report"` carries each whole summary, for the learning report. On the screens `useMembers` (`admin/data.ts`) calls it page after page; counts and exports wait for the last page, while the rows so far can be shown and searched. The list is kept in memory per organization and view; the page's own actions patch it, and a list nobody is showing is read again once it is older than 10 minutes. Adding or importing people calls `forgetMembers(orgId)` after every send, failed ones too (a lost answer may still have made accounts). The dashboard also reads the organization's `memberCount` on every open, and when a list read earlier has a different number of rows it reads the list once more (once per read of the organization, so a count that is itself off never loops).
- **Edits** (`updateMembers({orgId, uids ≤ 200, patch: {group?, teacherId?, name?}})`): class, teacher (a teacher or admin of this organization, for students only) and name (one person at a time), each hundred in one transaction, audited. Any other key is refused (`bad-patch`). A class is at most 60 characters (`null` or `""` for none), a name 1–120, a suspension's reason at most 500; every uid must be a Firestore document id (`bad-uids`; more than 200 is `too-many`).
- **Bulk actions** (`bulkMembers({orgId, uids ≤ 200, action})`): `remove`, `suspend`, `restore`, `resetPassword`, `setRole`. The checks run once; then each person is handled on their own, ten at a time, and one refusal is only that person's row. The rules are the single callables': never the caller, members only, the organization keeps an admin who can sign in. One audit entry (`op`, the counts and the uids); new passwords are never logged. On the screen a bulk reset shows the results as "New passwords" and downloads them as `darsi-new-passwords-<date>.xlsx`.
- **Suspend** (`setMemberSuspended`): for managed logins only (`own-login` otherwise: remove the person instead). The profile gets `suspended {at, by, reason, byOrg}`, Auth is disabled and the sessions end. Only a suspension this organization made can be lifted here; one a platform admin made answers `platform-suspended`, for a suspend as for a restore (suspending it again would stamp the organization on it and let its admin restore it). Restoring an account that isn't marked suspended is refused (`not-suspended`), so one switched off for another reason (a deletion in progress) is never switched back on. The profile is written before Auth, so a call that fails half-way can be repeated.
- **Leave** (`leaveOrg`; Me): leaving ends the session, so Me first sends what is still on the phone (`flushSync` in `state/sync.ts`: up to 5 s for Firestore's queue, then the unsent mark); if work is still unsent it says so and the button becomes "Leave anyway" (the work then stays on the phone for that account, as after any ended session). A member who signed up themself becomes an independent learner again and keeps their learning; class, teacher and seat are cleared and a teacher's pupils released. Refused for a managed account (its organization removes it), a suspended one, and the organization's only admin. Leaving does not put the person on the removed list.
- **Remove** (`removeMember`, bulk `remove`): back to an independent learner, their sessions end, and they go on the **removed list** (`orgs/{orgId}/private/removed`: `uids {uid: {at, name}}`, 2,000 at most, the oldest dropped first), which `joinOrg` and school sign-in read: a removed person cannot rejoin until an admin lets them back in (`readmitMember`, on the Invite tab). The list entry is written first, then a suspension this organization made is lifted (`liftOrgSuspension`: Auth first, then the mark; a platform admin's suspension stays), then the role change, then the member count, so a removal cut short is safe to repeat; the bulk path writes its list in one transaction trimmed the same way.
- **Who counts as a member** for a change (`memberOf` in `index.ts`): the profile names this organization, or the profile names **no** organization while Auth's claims still name this one (a change that stopped half-way, which its admin can then repeat to finish). A profile that names another organization is that organization's, whatever old claims say (`not-member`).
- **A half-approved organization is mended by approving it again:** `reviewOrg` on one that is already `active` answers `reviewed`, unless it was approved here before (`reviewedBy`; never one made with `createOrg`) and has no admin, or its requester's profile says admin while the claims don't. Then the requester becomes its admin, with no new codes and no second member count (`{status: "active", repaired: true}`, audited `repairOrg`).
- **Role changes** write the claims and the profile in the order a failure can afford (`setRole`, `platform.assign`): a change that takes power away (`losesPower` in `roles.ts`: a lower role, leaving or changing organization) writes the claims first, so access has ended even if the profile write fails, and the admin sees it did not finish; anything else writes the profile first. `node scripts/check-claims.mjs` (read-only; `--emulator`, `--project=<id>`, `--json`) lists staff accounts whose claims and profile disagree, and exits 0 when all agree.
- **Bulk accounts** (`createMembers`): every password is generated on the server (8–32 characters, 10 by default; a typed one is ignored), accounts are made six at a time with teachers and admins first, typed logins are reserved before any is made up, and an account whose claims or profile write fails is deleted again, so the row can be resent.

## Organizations: status, deletion and export

`orgs/{orgId}.status` is `pending` (a request), `active`, `rejected`, `suspended` or `deleting` (`functions/src/platform.ts`).

- **Suspend and resume** (`updateOrg` with `status`, platform admins; the Status tab of `/super/orgs/:id`): the document gets `suspended {at}` (every member reads it, so it says only since when), who suspended it and why go to `orgs/{orgId}/private/suspension` (platform admins only; the rules refuse its own admins), and the organization's teachers and admins get fresh claims with `frozen`, which the rules and callables read: they keep what any member has and lose what a staff role gives. Nobody new joins or is added (`org-inactive`). Students keep learning, and the organization's courses and web address keep serving. Resuming removes the mark, the note and the claim. `updateOrg` answers `{ok, staff, failed}`: how many staff accounts were tried and how many could not be refreshed (25 at a time; each account's claims are refreshed from what its claims hold now, never from the profile, so a refresh can't hand back a role an admin just took away). Only an active organization is suspended, a suspended one only resumes to active (never rejected in one step), and one being deleted takes no status change: anything else is `bad-status`. Sending `suspended` again refreshes the claims again (the retry when some failed), and `onOrgLicence` refreshes the same people when the status moves, retrying for six hours. Audited as `suspendOrg` and `resumeOrg`.
- **Deletion in steps** (`deleteOrg({orgId, confirm, members, cursor})` → `{done, cursor, processed, units}`; 540 s, 512 MiB): the typed name is checked on every call, and the screen calls again with the answer's `cursor` until `done`. The platform admin chooses what happens to the members: **release** everyone (the default: each becomes an independent learner and keeps their learning; the claims are lowered first, then the profile loses its class, teacher and seat, while the person's own subscription and `managedBy` stay), or **delete the accounts the organization made** (`delete-managed`: each managed account goes to the deletion queue above, everyone else is released). The first call marks the organization `deleting` (with who, when and the choice, so the deletion can be resumed from its page), deletes its invite codes and audits `deleteOrgStarted`. The first call's choice (`deletingMembers`) binds every later call: one that names the other choice is refused (`delete-option`, with the stored choice in `details.members`). Members go 100 a page, for up to 240 s a call. When none are left: the codes once more, the organization's units and drafts, school sign-in's secrets and identities, its files under `orgs/{orgId}/` and `exports/{orgId}/`, then the document and everything under it; audited as `deleteOrg` with the counts. A member that fails stops the call after its page with `delete-retry` and the cursor before that page; repeating is safe. A Storage failure is logged, sets `filesLeft: true` in the audit entry and does not stop the deletion.
- **Export** (`exportOrgData({orgId, exportId?, cursor?})` → `{exportId, done, cursor, files, members}`; the organization's admins and platform admins; a suspended organization, or one being deleted, is exported by platform admins only (`org-suspended`: its own admins are frozen and could not download the files); `admin/OrgExport.tsx`): a new export starts at most once per organization per 10 minutes (`export-wait`, with `retryAt`; the stamp is `orgs/{orgId}/private/export`, checked and written in one transaction), and continuing one is never limited. A first call that fails before it wrote its first file takes its own stamp back (`releaseExport`; a failure there is logged as `export-stamp`), so the admin can try again at once. Continuing needs the `exportId` and `cursor` of the answer before, and goes on only from where the last part stopped (`bad-export`); a finished export answers the same again and writes nothing. The stamp is the export's licence: a continuing call whose stamp is gone or names another export is refused `no-export` (a newer export replaced it, or an erasure in the organization removed it), and a part saved while the stamp went is deleted again. Each call writes one gzipped JSON file of members to `exports/{orgId}/{exportId}/part-0001.json.gz`, … (about 24 MB of JSON or 400 s a call), and the screen calls again until `done`. A member's entry holds the profile's learning fields and every lesson, project and certificate; never notes, payments, subscriptions, credits, devices or problem reports. A new export deletes the one before. Each part is stored as `application/gzip` (`private, no-store`) with its own metadata (where it started and stopped, how many members, whether it was the last), so a call repeated with the same cursor because its answer was lost returns the same answer and writes nothing. The files are read by the organization's admins and platform admins only (Storage rules) and written only by the function.
- **A new platform admin** (`setUserRole` to `superadmin`) needs the caller to have signed in during the last ten minutes (`recent-login`).

## Audit log

Every change the functions make for someone is written to `audit/{id}`: `{action, by, …details, at}`, and when a callable made it also `ip` (the client's address: Cloudflare's header only behind Cloudflare, else Hosting's, else the last forwarded hop) and `rid` (8 hex characters, one per request). The same `rid` is on one log line per entry (`audit {action, by, orgId, rid}`, never the details), so Cloud Logging finds the rest of that request. The request is remembered in Node's async context by `caller` / `freshCaller` / `requireSuper` (`admin.ts`); a new callable must call one of them before its first `await`. `requireSuper` refuses anyone but a platform admin (`not-super`). `freshCaller` also refuses a staff token whose account Auth has disabled (`disabled`).

- **Platform admins** read the whole log on `/super` → Records → Activity log (`admin/Ledger.tsx`): 50 at a time with a cursor, an organization filter on the `audit (orgId, at desc)` index, actions in words, and a CSV download of what is shown. The action and person filters have no index, so they filter only the entries already read (the screen says so).
- **An organization's own log** (`orgAudit({orgId, before?, action?, uid?, limit?})` → `{entries, next}`; its admins and platform admins; the Activity tab, `admin/OrgActivity.tsx`): the entries whose writer passed that `orgId`, newest first, with the name of who acted. An organization's admin never gets `ip`, and from a `deleteAccount` entry not the deleted person's payments, problem reports, tutor sessions, devices, requests, school identities or removed-list count (platform admins see everything; nothing stored changes). The organization's admins can't read it while it is suspended or being deleted (`org-suspended`, `org-deleting`). The action and person filters run on the server over at most 500 entries a call, so the screen offers "Show more" whenever `next` is not null. Entries about no organization are in no organization's log, and neither are entries written before their call site passed `orgId` (nothing was migrated; `VITE_AUDIT_SINCE` lets the screen say from which day the log is whole) or course-access grants (`grantCourseAccess` has no single organization to name; `scripts/pin-course-access.mjs` writes none either). A platform admin's removal or move of a member (`setUserRole`, and `createOrg` for a first admin who came from another organization) writes a second `setUserRole` entry with `left: true` under the organization the person left, so that organization's log has it; the entry in the organization they are in now doesn't say where they came from, and the old one's not where they went. `limit` is 1 to 100 (50 by default); `next` is milliseconds with the microseconds as the fraction, so two entries of one millisecond are neither skipped nor repeated, and a `before` that is no date is ignored. Reading the log leaves no entry. The user agent is remembered with the request (`requestContext()`) and not stored in the entry.
- Every action has words in both languages (`gv.act.<action>` in `admin/governance-strings`); `tests/governance.test.tsx` reads `functions/src` and fails when one has none.

## Invite codes

- **Join rules** (`setJoinPolicy`, the organization's admins; `admin/OrgPolicy.tsx` on the Invite tab): an organization may require a confirmed email (`email-unverified`), list the email domains that may join (at most 20, matched exactly; `email-domain`), and give each code a last day and a most-uses number (`code-expired`, `code-used-up`; `inviteCodes/{code}` holds `expiresAt`, `maxUses`, `uses`, with copies in `private/invites` for the admins' screen). The policy binds `joinOrg` only: people an admin adds are the admin's own choice. A new code (`regenerateInviteCode`) starts with no last day, no limit and no uses: the old code's limits go with it. The admin is told so in the confirmation and after it, and the rules form below mirrors it at once (its fields for that code emptied, unless the admin had typed an unsaved value there, which then goes onto the new code on Save). The page's Refresh, and a bulk Remove on People, read the rules again (fields the admin hasn't changed follow, typed ones stay). What isn't sent stays as it is, `null` clears a limit and an empty list the domains; domains are trimmed, lower-cased and merged, a last day must still be to come and a most-uses number is 1 to 1,000,000; anything else refuses the whole save (`bad-policy`). A call with only `orgId` reads the state and writes nothing, which is how the screen shows an organization whose codes still sit on the org document. `joinOrg` decides from the profile and Auth, not the token (an admin, a member of another organization or a disabled account is refused; when Auth can't be read, nobody is let in on the token alone: `try-again`), counts a use in a transaction that checks the limit again, and refuses a person on the removed list (`removed`) until `readmitMember`.
- **Big cohorts:** the Invite tab says that more than about 100 people should be imported rather than invited: Firebase limits new sign-ups from one network per hour. Copy says "Copied" only when the clipboard write went through; otherwise the code or link is shown selected.
- **Format:** 12 characters from 32 with no look-alikes, in groups of 4 (`K7QP-2MXR-9DHA`), about 60 bits. Codes from before 2026-11 (`K7Q-2MXP`) keep working until an admin makes new ones; the dashboard suggests it.
- **Private:** `orgs/{orgId}/private/invites`, readable by the org's admins and platform admins only. They used to sit on the org document, which every member reads, so a student could see the teacher code. `npm run migrate-invites` moves them.
- **Guessing:** `joinOrg` allows 10 failed tries per account per hour (`joinAttempts/{uid}`).
- **Sign-up:** "Invite code (optional)". An invite link (`/signup?code=…`, "Copy invite link" on the dashboard) fills it in. The code stays in the address because signing up remounts the app; `SignedIn` (App.tsx) then sends the new account to `/org?code=…`. **Joining always asks first** (`org/OrgScreen.tsx` `JoinForm`): `joinOrg` is called only from the Join button of a question on the page that says who will see the person's learning (the organization's admins and their teacher, the earlier record included), with "Not now" as the focused, safe answer. A typed code and an invite link get the same question; the link's code is taken out of the address before it is asked, once, so "Not now" keeps the code in the field and a reload asks nothing. A refusal closes the question and shows its reason on the field, in the person's language. Google sign-up works the same way.

## School sign-in

An organization can sign its people in with its own identity provider over OpenID Connect: Google Workspace, Microsoft Entra ID, Okta, Keycloak, or any other OpenID provider. It is built into Darsi (no Identity Platform, no fee per user). One connection per organization. A person the school's provider signs in who has no Darsi account joins as a **student** (or a teacher by the school's rule); nobody becomes an admin this way. Not built: SAML, SCIM, several providers per organization, forcing school sign-in on password accounts, and single log-out (the page only links to the provider's end-session page). Setup is in [docs/FIREBASE.md](docs/FIREBASE.md#10-school-sign-in); what to do when it fails is in [docs/OPERATIONS.md](docs/OPERATIONS.md).

### The pieces

| Piece | Where | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `ssoEdge` | HTTPS, behind the Hosting rewrite `/api/sso/**` on the app's site, so it answers on darsiai.org and on every organization address (80 at once per instance, at most 20 instances) | `POST /api/sso/start`, `POST /api/sso/finish`, `GET /api/sso/school?slug=[&try=]`. It never calls a provider |
| `ssoCallback` | HTTPS, served at `login.darsiai.org` (the second Hosting site, target `login`, whose only rule rewrites everything to this function; 40 at once, at most 30 instances, 30 s, 512 MiB, warm instances from `SSO_CALLBACK_MIN_INSTANCES`) | `GET /callback/<orgId>`: the only function that calls a provider. It verifies the answer and seals a ticket; it changes no account |
| `manageSso` | callable (120 s) | The wizard, the status card, and the platform's approvals, ceiling and switch (actions below); audited |
| `ssoJob` | scheduled, daily 02:30 UTC (540 s, 512 MiB, not retried) | Sessions that ran out, keys, provider endpoints that moved, client secrets about to expire, schools that were full; the last three also as staff alerts by push (see [What is sent](#what-is-sent)). Heartbeat `stats/jobs.ssoJob` |
| `ssoCore.ts`, `ssoProviders.ts`, `ssoCrypto.ts`, `ssoFetch.ts` | pure modules (no Firestore, no Auth, no logging) | Refusal codes, the rules per issuer, who a sign-in becomes, the presets; the key ring, state, ticket and sealed secret; `safeFetch`, the token request and ID-token verification (`jose`) |
| `ssoStore.ts` | the Firestore and Auth side | Configuration (with a 60 s copy in memory), health, identities, and the hooks the rest of the server calls |

The four functions declare the secret `SSO_KEYS` and run as the service account in `SSO_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` (`darsi-sso@…`; empty locally, which means the project's default account). Nothing else reads the keys or signs sign-in tokens. The redirect address is on its own origin, with no service worker and no app: each school registers exactly `<SSO_CALLBACK>/<its organization id>` (`https://login.darsiai.org/callback/<orgId>`), and the id never changes. `login` and `sso` are reserved names for organization sites. The service worker never answers `/api/` (`navigateFallbackDenylist`).

### Keys and secrets

- **`SSO_KEYS`** is JSON: `{"current": "k1", "keys": {"k1": "<32 bytes, base64>", …}}`. Unless every key decodes to exactly 32 bytes, the feature is closed (`sso-key`), so `unset` never becomes a key.
- **Derived keys** (HKDF-SHA256, one label each): `state` and `nonce` and `pkce` (HMAC), `ticket` and `secret` (AES-256-GCM).
- **The school's client secret** (always required: Darsi is a confidential client) is in `ssoSecrets/{orgId}`, sealed in the slot `live` or `next`. The sealed text repeats the issuer, the token endpoint's host and the client id, and the same values are the cipher's additional data (a JSON array, with the organization and the slot). Before every token request they must equal what is about to be used; otherwise nothing is sent, the status drops to `draft` and the status card shows a problem. Saving a changed issuer, client id or token host clears that slot, and the wizard asks for the secret again.
- **Rotation:** a state leads with its key id, so one signed just before a rotation still verifies. `ssoJob` seals again every secret whose key is not the current one and writes the key ids still in use to `stats/jobs.ssoJob.kids`; a key may leave `SSO_KEYS` only when that list no longer names it.
- **Logs** hold codes from a closed list and nothing else: never a request, a provider's answer, a token, a code or a secret. An error's message is never logged, only its code or name.

### Data

Only the sign-in functions read or write these; the rules refuse every client, platform admins included. An organization's admins see the configuration through `manageSso`, which leaves out what no browser should hold.

```
orgs/{orgId}/private/sso        status (draft | tested | on | off), connectionId, preset (google | microsoft | okta | keycloak | oidc),
                                issuer, tenant, clientId, authMethod (basic | post), scopes, endpoints {authorization, token, jwks,
                                endSession?}, testedEndpoints, configRev, testedRev, studentTestRev, allowedDomains [], openToAll,
                                emailsVerifiedBySchool, the teacher rule, classClaim, maxSessionDays (1–90, default 30),
                                personalDevices, slug, listing {state: none | requested | approved | refused, name, logo, at, by},
                                secretExpiresOn, next {the same fields, being edited and tested}, wip (the wizard's unfinished
                                step), lastTest, updatedAt, updatedBy
orgs/{orgId}/private/ssoHealth  reason → {n, firstAt, lastAt, hint} (discovery, endpoints-moved, secret-expiring, secret-mismatch,
                                secret-key, full); jit {h<hour>: n} (accounts made in each hour, raised unread; `ssoJob` deletes
                                the hours that are over); alerts (which alert went out, and when)
ssoSecrets/{orgId}              live?, next? {kid, iv, ct}: the sealed client secrets
ssoIdentities/{id}              id = sha256(orgId, connectionId, subject); subject = `sub`, or `tid:oid` for Microsoft.
                                uid, orgId, connectionId, issuer, subject, email, emailVerified,
                                linked (jit | org-made | self | admin-map), state (active | removed | blocked), blockedBy (admin |
                                suspension), moved, accountDeleted, pending?, createdAt, lastAt, expiresAt, revokedAt
ssoUsed/{stateId}               expireAt   ← a state spent at the callback (create(), so a replay fails); Firestore TTL
ssoTests/{token}                orgId, by, expireAt   ← a one-time "test as a student" link, 24 hours; Firestore TTL
ssoSlugs/{slug}                 orgId, at, tombstone?   ← never deleted: a freed slug is never given to another school.
                                The server makes it (slugify): the name's Latin letters and digits (an Arabic name
                                gives "school"), at most 30, "-", and 4 random characters without look-alikes; the
                                names kept for Darsi's addresses and sso, login, auth, school(s), darsi, callback,
                                oidc, saml are never slugs
orgs/{orgId}.sso                {on, slug, listed}   ← for the organization's own screens (every member reads the org document)
orgs/{orgId}.ssoCeiling         the most members school sign-in may bring it to   ← manageSso setCeiling (platform admins)
users/{uid}                     sso: true, managedBy: orgId, createdBy: "sso", ssoEmail (the provider's verified address, else
                                Entra's sign-in name, else null) on accounts school sign-in made: no `email`, no password;
                                ssoLinkedAt on any account connected to one. Rules: no client writes these four fields
config/settings.ssoOn           false closes school sign-in for everyone (published as `sso` in content/settings.json)
```

Public files: `content/sso.json` (the school finder: only listings a platform admin approved, `{slug, name, logo (a data-URL copy), host}`) and, in `content/sites.json`, `sso: {slug, host}` on the entry of an organization whose sign-in is on, so its own address shows the button without a listing.

### The flow

Authorization Code with PKCE (S256), `state` and `nonce`. Every call to the provider is made by the server.

1. **Start** (`POST /api/sso/start`, `{orgId | slug, mode: signin | link | test, challenge, testToken?, signedOutHere?}`). The page makes a PKCE pair of its own and sends the challenge. The origin it came from must be Darsi's own or an address of **this** organization that currently has sign-in (`sites/{host}`), else `sso-origin`. The connection must be on (or under test), the organization active and the switch on. `link` and `test` need the caller's ID token (revoked ones refused); `link` also needs a sign-in in the last 10 minutes (`sso-recent`) and is refused for platform admins; `test` needs the organization's admin, a platform admin, or a valid student test link. No document is written: the state is `<key id>.<fields>.<HMAC>` with a random id, the organization, the mode, the challenge, the return origin and the configuration's revision, good for 60 minutes; the provider's `nonce` and Darsi's own PKCE verifier for the provider are derived from that id. What the provider is asked for (`promptFor`): link and test always want a fresh sign-in there (`prompt=login`; Google has none, so `select_account` with `max_age=0`); a sign-in shows the account chooser on Google and Microsoft unless the school chose "personal devices", and asks other providers for a fresh login only when this device signed out of a school session (`signedOutHere`). The answer is the provider's address and a flow id; the page keeps `{verifier, flow, mode, next, at}` in localStorage (`darsi:sso`) and goes there.
2. **Callback** (`GET login.darsiai.org/callback/<orgId>?code&state`). The state's signature and age are checked and its organization must be the one in the path **before any outbound call**; then it is spent (`ssoUsed`). Where the provider's discovery promised an `iss` parameter, it must equal the issuer. The code is exchanged at the stored token endpoint through `safeFetch`, and the ID token is verified: RS256, PS256 or ES256 only, the exact issuer, `aud` holding the client id (`azp` too when present or when there are several audiences), the derived `nonce`, 60 s of clock skew. The rules per issuer and the teacher and class rules are applied to the token (below). Then the callback seals a **ticket** (2 minutes) with the verified identity and redirects to a constant path on the return origin, `/signin`, `/me` or the admin's sign-in tab, with the ticket in the URL fragment: `#sso=<ticket>&f=<flow>`, or `#sso_error=<code>&f=<flow>`. For an organization address it reads `sites/{host}` again first, and falls back to Darsi's own site.
3. **Finish** (`POST /api/sso/finish`, `{ticket, verifier, confirm?}`). The page calls it only when it holds an unexpired `darsi:sso` record for that flow; it clears the fragment (`history.replaceState`) first and spends the record. Without a record it says to go back to the tab the sign-in started in, or start again, and no ticket is spent. The server checks that the verifier hashes to the ticket's challenge and that the request's origin is the ticket's, checks the switch, the connection and the organization again, and only then acts:
   - **signin:** decides who this is (below), re-reads the Auth account and the profile (it exists, is not disabled, its claims and profile both say this organization, it is not a platform admin), and answers a Firebase custom token with the claim `{sso: orgId}`. The page signs in with it (`signInWithCustomToken`, the SDK loaded then).
   - **link:** needs the same account as the ticket's. The first call answers what would be connected (`{confirm: {name, email, school}}`); the second, with `confirm: true`, connects it. Who may (`decideLink`): a member of this organization whose claims and profile both say so, not disabled and not staff of the platform; an identity that is another account's, or a second identity for the same account, is refused (`sso-linked`), a blocked or removed one with its own code. The confirmation may come up to 10 minutes after the callback (`LINK_CONFIRM_TTL_MS`; every other use of a ticket stays within its 2 minutes). One exception: an identity held by an account the school's sign-in made that has no lesson, project or certificate yet, is not an admin and teaches no pupils (the person signed in through the school before connecting their own account) moves to the caller, and that empty account goes to the deletion queue (`accountDeletions`, `via: "sso-link"`). The link is `self`, or `org-made` for an account an admin created.
   - **test:** records `lastTest` (the decision in words, the claims that arrived, the revision) and, when it passed, marks the revision tested; a passing test of `next` promotes it. A test never links, creates or signs in anyone. An admin's own test is finished only by the account that started it, still this organization's admin or a platform admin as Auth says now. A student's test link is one use: `finish` deletes it in the result's transaction, so a second flow started from the same link answers `sso-state`. A failed test is recorded at the callback, so the admin who sent a student a link sees why.

`start` allows 300 sign-in starts per address and organization per 10 minutes, 20 per account or test link, and the school lookup 3,000 per address (`SCHOOL_MAX`; each per instance; every open of `/signin?school=` makes one lookup, and a whole school behind one address opens it in the same few minutes). `finish` has no limit of its own: it needs a sealed ticket and the browser's verifier, and a school behind one address signs hundreds of people in at 8 o'clock.

The address is only as good as the way in (`clientIp.ts`): through Hosting it is the visitor's, but on a function's own URL the caller writes `fastly-client-ip` themself, and nothing in the headers proves which way a request came. So two more limits use the **hop**, the last `x-forwarded-for` entry, which Google's front end adds: the caller's real address on the function's URL, Hosting's CDN node through Hosting (`lastHop`). Both are per instance, and both come before anything is read or written. Through Hosting the hop is a CDN node that a school and everyone else who arrives through it share, so both are ceilings sized above a large school's own morning, not limits per person: at the first numbers (1,500 starts; 60 and 40 places) a school's own traffic through one node used them up, and one caller could use them up for it.
- **Sign-in starts:** 30,000 per hop and organization per 10 minutes (`SIGNIN_HOP_MAX`: 50 a second from one connection; a start writes nothing), beside the 300 per address.
- **Callbacks that prove nothing** (`UNPROVEN`): a state is free to get, and each is good for one `ssoUsed` write and one token request carrying the school's client secret. A callback takes a place before it spends its state, 300 a minute per hop and organization and 120 per address and organization (the callback function's concurrency is 40, so answers under way at once never use either up by themselves; one caller on the function's own URL is held to 300 junk callbacks a minute per instance, and using a school's places up through Hosting takes five a second at each node), and gives it back once the provider exchanged its code (or when `safeFetch`'s own brakes turned it away: `busy`, `breaker`, `budget`). One that ends otherwise (a code the provider refuses, a replayed state, the provider's own error) keeps its place for the minute. With no place left the callback answers `sso-provider` (`busy`) with its state unspent, so the same answer works a minute later.

Neither is a cap per organization alone: one bucket for a whole school is one anyone could fill to keep that school out. What they don't stop is a flood from many real addresses; that needs a limit in front of the functions.

**The rules per issuer** come from the issuer in the token, not the preset the admin picked (`rulesFor` in `ssoCore.ts`):
- **Google** (`https://accounts.google.com`): allowed email domains are required; `hd` must be present and in the list, `email_verified` true, and the email's domain equal to `hd`.
- **Microsoft** (`login.microsoftonline.com`, `sts.windows.net`, the government and China clouds): the tenant must be a GUID (never `common`, `organizations`, `consumers` or the consumer tenant), the token's `tid` must be that tenant and `oid` present; the subject is `tid:oid`. The address is `email`, else `preferred_username`; domain checks read `preferred_username`; an address counts as verified only when the `email` claim came with `xms_edov: true`.
- **Apple and other shared consumer issuers** are refused (`sso-issuer`), and consumer mail domains (gmail.com, outlook.com…) are refused as allowed domains.
- **Any other issuer:** allowed domains, or "everyone my provider signs in may join" chosen explicitly; an address is verified by `email_verified`, or always when the admin ticked "this provider's emails are verified by the school".

Google's and Microsoft's endpoints are constants; discovery only checks them. For the others, discovery runs on the server when the admin checks or saves, and endpoints are never taken from the browser.

### Who a sign-in becomes

Decided in `finish` (`decideSignIn`), inside a transaction where it writes:

1. **A known identity** (`ssoIdentities/{id}` exists): `blocked` is refused (`sso-disabled`), `removed` is refused (`sso-removed`: the school removed this person). A platform admin is refused (`sso-staff`); claims and profile that disagree are refused (`sso-other-org`) and logged (the warning `sso-claims-profile`). Otherwise the person signs in. The teacher rule may raise a student to teacher, never lower anyone and never make an admin.
2. **An account the organization made, still on the password it was handed:** connected by itself only when all of this holds: the account is in this organization, made by its admin, a student or teacher, has no identity yet, still has `mustChangePassword` (or never signed in), and the token's address is verified under the issuer's rules and equals the account's Auth email (a `@users.darsi.app` username login never matches). The password is never set or changed.
3. **The same address as another member of this organization:** `sso-connect-first` ("sign in the way you usually do, then connect school sign-in in Me"). Nothing is created.
4. **Nobody yet: join as a student.** Within the allowance: at most 200 new accounts per organization per hour (or a quarter of its members when that is more), and never past the organization's ceiling (`ssoCeiling`; by default its licence seats × 1.5, or 2,000), else `sso-full`. The identity is reserved first (`create()` with `pending`), so two sign-ins at once make one account; then the Auth account (no email, no password), the claims, the profile and the member count. A pending identity over a minute old is finished with the same uid. Both transactions read the person's own identity and nothing else: the hour's counter (`ssoHealth.jit.h<hour>`) and `memberCount` are raised with increments that read nothing, because a transaction that read those two shared documents would lock them and a class joining together would abort each other's. So the allowance is the one read just before deciding: the hourly limit and the ceiling can be passed by as many first sign-ins as are under way at once.

Removal holds through all of this. People on `orgs/{orgId}/private/removed` are refused, also when they never connected (the Auth account holding the verified address is on the list). A personal account's link whose owner left the organization becomes a tombstone that counts as removed only while the person is on that list; someone who left of their own accord joins afresh. Personal means not managed by the organization: a link the person connected (`self`), and also one matched to an account an admin made with the person's own email (`org-made` without `managedBy`); a login the organization manages keeps a plain tombstone. An account another person deleted leaves its school identities as `removed` tombstones, so the school's directory does not quietly make it again; `allowBack` clears them.

### Sessions, removal and leaving

- **Session length:** each identity carries `expiresAt` (`maxSessionDays`). `ssoJob` signs out (revokes the refresh tokens of) accounts that have no other way in: made by school sign-in, or matched while still on the handed-out password. It never does so while the school's sign-in is off, since they could not get back in; the end moves forward instead.
- **On the phone**, a school session (the token's `sso` claim) that ends keeps its unsent work like any ended session and goes to `/signin?school=<slug>` with "Your school asks you to sign in again", never to the guest's store. That holds when it ended while the app was closed, the usual case (`ssoJob` revokes overnight): no token is read at that start, so the school comes from the claims kept on the device (`darsi:claims:<uid>`). Signing out of one notes it (`darsi:ssoSignedOut`), so the next start asks the provider for a fresh sign-in, and says the person is still signed in at the school, with the provider's end-session link when there is one.
- **Removed or suspended:** `removeMember` and bulk remove mark the person's identities `removed`; `setMemberSuspended` and a platform suspension mark them `blocked` (and lifting a suspension never undoes an admin's own block). Someone removed while blocked stays blocked, with the removal remembered, and lifting the block then leaves them `removed` (never active), so `allowBack` finds them; an organization's own suspension lifted as the person leaves does the same. For one person, `manageSso` has `person` (a read: `{state: active | blocked | removed | none, linked, blockedBy: admin | suspension, ownLogin}`; `ownLogin` is Auth's word on whether the account has a password or Google login, and null for an account that isn't this organization's to ask about; nothing is written or audited), `block` and `unblock` (a block also ends the sessions of an account only the school lets in), `unlink`, and `allowBack`: a removed person's identities become active again and the person comes off the removed list; an account the school's sign-in made, or a login the organization manages (`managedBy`, a username login), goes back into the organization as a student (`rejoined`); a person's own account is never put back by an admin, whether the person connected it (`self`) or an admin once made it with the person's own email (`createdBy` alone gives the organization nothing): its link is only deleted and the person joins again with a code; and a deleted account's tombstones go so the next school sign-in makes a new one. It answers `{allowed, rejoined}`; `allowed: false` means there was no removed school sign-in and nothing changed. **On the screens:** the Account tab of a person's page has a "School sign-in" panel (`SchoolSignIn` in `admin/PersonDetail.tsx`) that reads `person` once when the tab opens, says how the school's sign-in stands for them and offers only what applies (`ssoOffered`): Block, and Disconnect unless the school is the account's only way in; Unblock for an admin's own block (a suspension's block is lifted by restoring the account); "Let back in" for someone removed. Each asks first on the page and reads the state again. The removed list on the Invite tab (`admin/OrgPolicy.tsx`) does both halves with one "Let back in": `allowBack`, then `readmitMember` for the codes, and says which happened (an organization's own admin can't open a removed person's page, so the list is their place for it; a platform admin can, from `/super/people/:uid`, where the organization is the account's `managedBy`). Every organization change passes `writeClaims`, which does the same when the organization in the claims changes.
- **Accounts the school made have no email at Auth.** Their address is the profile's `ssoEmail`, so `createMembers` treats a typed email that equals a member's `ssoEmail` as an existing account (no second one is made), and `findAccount` finds such an account by it.
- **Deleting an organization** deletes its sealed secrets and identities and tombstones its slug; the accounts school sign-in made follow the deletion's choice for managed accounts.
- **Accounts with no login of their own:** Me offers "Add an email login" (the SDK's `verifyBeforeUpdateEmail`, then "I opened the link", which reloads the account, then a password linked with `linkWithCredential`), so a person keeps their learning when they leave the school. `resetMemberPassword` answers `sso-login` for them, and Forgot password on a page with a school button says to use it.
- **A personal account opened through the school** (a `self` link) may not delete itself, export its data, start a checkout or change a renewal in that session (`sso-own-login`: "sign in with your own Darsi login").
- **No lock-out:** turning sign-in on, and any role change that would leave the organization with only admins who have no login of their own, are refused (`sso-last-way-in`).

### The wizard and the screens

- **Setup** (`admin/SsoSetup.tsx`, a staff chunk with its own strings and `styles/sso.css`; in the "Sign-in & web address" tab of `/admin` and the `signin` tab of `/super/orgs/:id`): five steps, each saved on the server. Choose the provider; connect (the console steps for that provider, the redirect address to copy, tenant or issuer, client id, the secret, its expiry date, and "Check connection", which runs discovery on the server and answers a plain hint); who can sign in (domains, the teacher rule, the class claim, session length, personal devices); test ("Test with my account", and "Test as a student": a one-time link, good for 24 hours, that needs no account); turn on. Turning on needs a passing test of the current revision, and for Google and Microsoft a passing student test or a ticked "I checked with a student account".
- **The status card** afterwards: on or off, connected people, the last sign-in, problems from `ssoHealth` with hints, the secret's expiry, the link to share (`/signin?school=<slug>`), "Ask to be listed in the school finder", Turn off and Edit. Editing works on `next`: the live connection keeps serving until the new one passes its test (an admin's or a student's), which promotes it. Editing without typing the secret again works while the issuer, the client id and the token host stay the same (the live secret is sealed again for `next`); saving what is already there changes nothing and keeps the test that passed (it only clears the wizard's kept steps, `wip`, so the wizard doesn't open on step 3 again). Every save, test and promotion stores the connection whole: a key the new connection lacks (an end-session address, an earlier kind of teacher rule's fields) is deleted, never kept from the old one. Turn off drops an untested `next` with its secret, and answers how many accounts the school's sign-in made (`ssoOnly`), for the page to say who can't get in meanwhile. The wizard's unfinished step is kept on the server as typed (`save` with `partial: true` → `wip`, at most 16,000 characters, never the secret). A connection whose provider host changes goes back to `requested` in the finder until a platform admin approves it again.
- **`manageSso` actions:** `get`, `discover`, `save`, `testLink`, `turnOn`, `turnOff`, `requestListing`, `listing` (platform admins: approve or refuse), `person`, `allowBack`, `block`, `unblock`, `unlink` (a person may unlink themself; never a blocked identity, never the account's only way in), `setCeiling` and `setSwitch` (platform admins). An unknown action answers `bad-action`.
- **Changing the provider** (`save`): a changed issuer or client id with people already connected is refused with `sso-migrate` until the admin says `migration: "same"` (the same people: the connection and its identities stay) or `"new"` (a different provider: a new `connectionId`, and people are found again by their verified address). **A new provider address (issuer) is always a new connection:** `"same"` is refused for it, since another issuer's subjects can't be told apart, and with nobody connected the connection id changes without asking. Under a new connection, the account whose identity under the old one carries the same verified address is the person's (exactly one match, never a personal account's own link), and someone the organization removed or blocked is still found by that address instead of being made a new account. That match signs in **only an account the school's sign-in made that is still a student or teacher** (`reattaches` in `sso.ts`), and each one is audited (`sso.rekey`, with the old and new connection). A member whose account has a login of its own (one an admin made, matched or mapped), or who has become an admin, is answered `sso-connect-first`: they sign in the way they usually do and connect again from Me, where the new identity replaces their active one under the old connection (a blocked or removed one still refuses). **An admin whose only way in was the school's sign-in is therefore kept out after a move to a different provider** until another admin makes them a teacher (they are then found by the address) and an admin again; the lock-out guard guarantees such another admin exists. Old-connection identities are not expired, and the match trusts "this provider's emails are verified by the school" as every other rule does.
- **Signing in** (`auth/sso.ts` in startup: the flags, the fragment's pattern and `stashReturn`, below; `auth/ssoFlow.ts`, `auth/SchoolFinder.tsx` and `auth/ssoWords.ts` load only when the page has something of a school's): on an organization's address with sign-in on, and on `/signin?school=<slug>` (one fetch of `/api/sso/school`), a "Sign in with <school>" button above the usual options, naming the provider's host; on darsiai.org, "Sign in with your school" opens the finder over `content/sso.json`. The return shows "Signing you in…", and every refusal has its sentence and what to do in both languages. The provider's own error code is shown only when this browser holds the flow's record and the code is plain (`[a-z_]`, at most 40), so a crafted link can't put words on the page. When a link names a school Darsi doesn't know, darsiai.org offers the finder once the lookup has said so. On an organization's own address, "Sign in" is the second choice from the first render. A guest loads no Firebase until the custom token arrives.
- **The return from the provider** is taken out of the address before the router starts: `auth/sso.ts` runs `stashReturn()` as the module loads (App imports it before it renders anything). A fragment `#sso=…` or `#sso_error=…` is kept in memory only and removed from the address and the history entry (`history.replaceState`), so no screen, tab link, reload, bookmark or Back ever carries the ticket; the screen of the page it came back to takes it once (`takeStash`, read by `ssoFlow.takeReturn`). The callback sends a sign-in, and a test started from a student's test link (`/signin?school=<slug>&try=<token>`), back to `/signin`; a link to `/me`; an admin's own test to their sign-in tab (`/admin?tab=signin`, or `/super/orgs/<id>?tab=signin` for a platform admin; `returnPath` in `ssoCore.ts`). When this browser's own `darsi:sso` record says the flow was a test with a token, `stashReturn` moves the return to `/sso-test` (`SchoolTestDone`, routed in App), which finishes it without an account and shows the result whoever is or isn't signed in. Staff tabs never keep such a fragment in their links either (`kept` in `admin/Tabs.tsx`).
- **Me** (`auth/SchoolLink.tsx`, lazy): "School sign-in" for members of an organization with it on: Connect (asking again for the password or Google when the sign-in is older than 9 minutes), the confirmation page, "Connected as … since …" with Disconnect, and "Add an email login".
- **A test link's page** (`/signin?school=<slug>&try=<token>`) shows only the test, never the password form. The wizard's Test step comes before Turn on, so the school lookup carries the link's token (`/api/sso/school?slug=…&try=…`): while the link is good (this school's, not used, not expired) the server names the school whatever its sign-in's state, with the host of the connection under test and `no-store`. Any other token is answered `sso-state` whatever the slug (a used link says so on the page, with no button), and another school's link `sso-no-school`. Without a token, a school that isn't on still answers as an unknown one.
- **Startup holds little of this:** `auth/sso.ts` and four strings of the sign-in screen itself (`sso.yourSchool`, `sso.signingIn`, `sso.forgot`, `sso.testTitle`); everything else is in the lazy chunks (`SchoolFinder`, `SchoolLink`, `ssoWords`, `ssoFlow`, `SsoSetup`, all in `LAZY_SCREENS`, none precached). `SchoolFinder.tsx` must not import `AuthProvider` (it split startup into 8 files): `SchoolReturn` gets `ensureFirebase` as a prop.
- **Platform admins:** the switch on `/super/plans`, listing approvals in the "School sign-in" tab of `/super/domains` (`SsoListings` asks `manageSso get` for each organization whose sign-in is on and not listed, four at a time: there is no list action), and on an organization's page its status, its ceiling, and whether the keys and the token signer work.

### What each rule defends against

| Rule | Defends against |
|---|---|
| The callback is on `login.darsiai.org`, per organization (`/callback/<orgId>`), and the state must name that organization before any outbound call | One school's provider answering for another (mix-up), and a made-up state making the server call somewhere |
| The state is signed, lasts 60 minutes and is spent once (`ssoUsed`) | Forged and replayed callbacks |
| The page's own PKCE verifier is needed at `finish`, from the origin the flow started on; the ticket travels in the fragment and lasts 2 minutes (a link's confirmation, by the same account, 10) | Login CSRF (finishing someone else's sign-in in the victim's browser), tickets leaking through logs or referrers, a ticket carried to another site |
| `nonce`, exact issuer, `aud` and `azp`, asymmetric algorithms only, the `iss` parameter where promised | Tokens made for another client or by another issuer, replayed ID tokens, `alg: none` and HMAC confusion |
| Rules by issuer, not preset; Microsoft's shared tenants and consumer issuers refused; `hd` and verified addresses required | "Anyone with a Google or Microsoft account" getting in through a school's connection; an address a person typed themself standing in for a verified one |
| `safeFetch`: https only, IPv4 only, every answer of a name's lookup checked against the non-public ranges, the connection made to the checked address, no redirects, 8 s and 512 KB, 20 s per callback, at most 10 calls in flight per organization and a 30 s breaker after 5 timeouts | Server-side request forgery through an issuer an admin types (cloud metadata, internal addresses, DNS rebinding), a slow provider holding every instance |
| The client secret sealed with the issuer, token host and client id it belongs to; endpoint changes found by `ssoJob` are reported (`endpoints-moved`) and never applied. A secret that doesn't open takes the connection back to draft only when the configuration, read afresh, still is the one the flow started on: a callback behind an instance's one-minute cache after an edit was promoted is an expired flow (`sso-state`) and changes nothing | A changed configuration sending a school's secret to another host; a cache that is behind switching a school's sign-in off |
| Sign-in starts and unproven callbacks counted by hop as well as by address, before anything is read, written or sent | States collected with a made-up address per request, then spent into writes and token requests that carry the school's secret; a school's provider calls starved by junk codes |
| All effects in `finish`; the account re-read just before the token is made; nobody becomes or signs in as a platform admin; the teacher rule only raises | A stale or half-finished flow changing an account; privilege through a provider's claims |
| Automatic connection only for an organization-made account still on its handed-out password with a verified, equal address, and after a move to a different provider only for an account the school's sign-in made that is still a student or teacher; otherwise `sso-connect-first`; link needs a recent sign-in and a confirmation page | Taking over an existing account by controlling an address at the provider |
| The removed list, tombstones, blocks and the hourly allowance and ceiling | A removed or suspended person coming straight back; a misconfigured provider filling an organization with accounts |
| A dedicated service account, alone with the keys and token signing; a closed list of logged codes | Other functions minting sign-ins or reading secrets; secrets in logs |

Open: a personal account opened through the school can still change its password or email, or delete itself, straight from the client SDK (a custom-token sign-in counts as recent there; `sso-own-login` covers the callables only). A staff alert that could not be pushed (no device with staff alerts on, or the sign-in functions' account not allowed to send) leaves no log line: the problem still shows on the status card.

### Local stand-in

`scripts/local/oidc.mjs` is a school provider on `http://127.0.0.1:4797` (discovery, an authorize page with a small account picker, token, keys) that `npm run local` starts beside the emulators. In the wizard choose "Other (OpenID Connect)" with issuer `http://127.0.0.1:4797`, client `darsi-local` and secret `local-secret`. Plain http on loopback, and a loopback page as the return origin, are accepted only under `FUNCTIONS_EMULATOR`. `functions/test/ssoFakes.ts` has the same provider for the unit tests, signing real RS256 tokens and counting its calls.

## Analytics

Two sources, both read with one-shot reads on `/super/analytics`:

- **Accounts and organizations** (`stats/overview`): `statsJob` (daily) and `refreshStats` (the Refresh button) read every user's role, organization, dates and summary with a `select()`, and every organization. They count accounts, roles, new and active accounts (by `lastActive` or the last lesson), lessons, XP, the average score and lessons per ISO week, and for each active organization its members, teachers, students, activity, lessons and score. Pending and rejected organizations are only counted.
- **Usage from phones** (`analytics/{day}`, `analyticsDevices`), guests included. Every phone queues events and sends them in batches to `collect`.

**On the phone** (`analytics/track.ts`, startup code, no Firebase; `useAnalytics` in App):
- `track()` adds an event to a queue in IndexedDB (`darsi:events`, at most 300) and never waits for the network. Events made offline carry `o: 1`.
- A batch (at most 100 events) goes to `/api/collect` on the same site, gzipped with `CompressionStream` where available (`?z=1`). It is sent when 30 events have built up, when the app goes to the background (at most every 15 minutes), when the connection returns, and at once when someone signs in or out, so events go out as the account that made them. With Data saver or the browser's Save-Data, it waits for 100 events or a day. No timers, no polling. A failed send keeps the batch; one refused as malformed (400/413) is dropped.
- Events: `session_start`, `screen_view` (the path's first part), `lesson_start`, `lesson_complete` (unit, lesson, track, score, seconds, XP), `lab_open`, `unit_download` (unit, MB), `onboarding_done`, `sign_up` and `sign_in` (method). None from the course editor's preview.
- A batch says who sent it: a random id made on the phone (`darsi:device`), the role (`guest` without an account), audience, language, organization id and whether the app is installed. Never a name, email or answer. Me has a switch to stop it ("Share anonymous usage statistics"); switched off, the queue is emptied.
- **The build's name** (`build.ts`: `BUILD`, the `DARSI_BUILD` the release script sets, else `<short commit>-<yyyymmdd>`, `dev` in tests and the dev server) travels with every batch as `c.v`, and with problem reports as `details.app.build` (beside `details.app.entry`, the entry file the page really runs).
- **Every batch has an id** (`b`, 16 hex characters), remembered with the queue before the request leaves. A batch that got no answer goes again first, as it was, under the same id, even after a restart, so the server counts it once; events queued since follow in a second request with a new id.
- **The app's own errors** are counted, not only kept for a problem report. `bugs/log.ts` (startup, imports nothing) tells its listeners, and `useAnalytics` queues `app_error {s, k, h}`: the screen, the kind (`error` from the window's error event, `rejection`, `chunk` for a lazy chunk that did not load, `render` for a screen that crashed while drawing, both from `darsi:app-error`), and a 12-hex fingerprint of the message and the first stack line, with the site's address and query strings taken out. The message never leaves the phone this way. At most five a page load, once per fingerprint, and none with the statistics switch off. They go with the next batch: no request of their own.
- Cost: about 2 KB of startup JavaScript (gzipped). A batch of 30 events is about 1 KB gzipped, usually one request per session.

**On the server** (`functions/src/analytics.ts`; the pure parts, tested, in `analyticsCore.ts`):
- `parseBatch` keeps only known events and parameters (ids, short words, numbers clamped to a range) from the last 30 days, and never more than 10 minutes ahead.
- `sanitize` then keeps a phone's ids only where Darsi knows them: published courses (public, and the org's own for its members), Labs apps (`functions/src/shared/labs.ts`, generated from the app's lab folders by `copy-shared.mjs`), active organizations, tracks, published languages and a fixed list of screens. Anything else counts as `other`, so the day's document can't be filled with made-up keys.
- `dailyCounts` adds them to `analytics/{yyyy-mm-dd}` with increments, so batches arriving together add up.
- A phone's first batch of the day adds to `dev` (and `devNew` the first time ever). `analyticsDevices/{id}.last` gives active phones for 1, 7 and 30 days through count queries.
- Organization figures from phones are what the phone says, so the dashboard takes members, activity and scores from `stats/overview` and uses the phones' counts only for the org's lessons-per-day chart.
- **A batch is counted once.** After the day's counts are written, the phone's record gets `lastBatch`; a batch whose id equals it answers 204 and counts nothing. If the phone's record can't be written after the counts went in, `collect` still answers 204 (log `analytics: device record not written`): a 500 would make the phone send the batch again and count it twice. The server keeps a build name only when it is 1 to 24 letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`, starting with a letter or digit (`BUILD` in `analyticsCore.ts`); a longer one is dropped without an error, so keep release names short. The record is written after the counts, in one write, so a failed count leaves no trace and the batch sent again is counted in full. One phone's batches are handled one at a time per instance.
- **One write for the batches of one moment:** the counts of the requests that arrive within 250 ms on an instance are added up and written in one commit, each request answering by it. With three instances that is at most 12 writes a second on a day's document, however many phones report.
- **Errors by build:** `app_error` is counted per day as `err {n, k: {kind: n}}` and `errBuild {build: n}` (at most 20 build names a day, the rest under `other`), and never forwarded to Google Analytics. A bad release shows as its build's count climbing; `/super` → Records → Status shows today's and yesterday's.
- **`statsJob` reads in pages** (2,000 accounts at a time into an accumulator, 1 GiB), so memory does not grow with the number of accounts, and afterwards mends `orgs.memberCount` where the stored number differs from the counted accounts (at most 400 organizations a run).
- **Google Analytics** (Firebase's web stream) can neither fail nor hold a request: it is forwarded after Darsi's own counts are in, each payload once with 3 s, 4 s in all. Once a platform admin saves the measurement ID and a Measurement Protocol API secret on /super/analytics (the `setGaConfig` callable writes `config/analytics`; no client does), `collect` forwards each batch: 25 events per request, the last 72 hours only, the phone's random id as `client_id`, role, audience, language and installed as user properties, and `engagement_time_msec` so visits count as engaged. Every account type is forwarded, students included (the user's choice), with no personal data. The Firebase Analytics SDK (gtag) is not used: it would cost every phone about 50 KB, load Firebase for guests and lose events made offline.

**The screen** (`admin/Analytics.tsx`, a lazy chunk with its own strings in `admin/strings/` and `styles/analytics.css`):
- Tiles: active phones, accounts, active accounts, lessons, average score, organizations.
- Bars per day for 30 days (active phones, lessons, visits), plus the offline and installed shares and downloads.
- The top tracks, courses, labs, languages, roles and screens.
- The organizations table, people (the 50 most recently active, or a name search), and the Google Analytics connection.
- `/super/orgs/:id` has one organization's figures, its lessons per day, its branding and its people; `/super/people/:uid` is the person page.

## Scheduled jobs and their heartbeats

Every scheduled job ends by writing `stats/jobs.<job> = {at (ms), ok, …its own numbers}` (merged, so the other jobs' entries stay), with `error` (200 characters) when `ok` is false. Platform admins read the document; nobody writes it from a client.

| Job | Runs (UTC) | Its entry also says |
|---|---|---|
| `statsJob` | daily, 02:30 | `users`; a failed run is rethrown, so the scheduler's retry still happens |
| `dailyNudges` | hourly | `hour`, `seen`, `sent`, `failed`, `complete`. It takes 50 people at a time and stops taking people 500 s into its 540 s; `complete: false` means people of that hour got no nudge that day, also logged as the error `dailyNudges incomplete`. At about a second per group of 50, one run covers roughly 25,000 nudged people an hour (an estimate, never load-tested); splitting an hour over several runs is left until that flag turns false |
| `sitesJob` | every 15 minutes | `checked`, `live`. One address whose check throws no longer stops the others; the checks stop after 200 s of its 300 s, and the last step (taking sign-in away from an address whose DNS no longer points at Darsi) always runs. It also writes again every catalog marked in `stats/catalogs` (see [Authored content](#authored-content)) |
| `retentionJob` | daily, 03:10 | `deleted` (by kind), `stopped` (the time ran out), `failed` (the kinds that failed) |
| `ssoJob` | daily, 02:30 | `sessions`, `revoked`, `resealed`, `moved`, `kids` (the key ids still in use). A step that failed (one organization, the sessions page) doesn't stop the others: the entry then says `ok: false` and, in `error`, the first step's name and the error's kind, never its words; a run that worked clears it. A run that throws outright still leaves `ok: false` before it is rethrown |

`/super` → Records → Status (`admin/PlatformStatus.tsx`) shows this build, the errors by build, all five jobs (`JOBS`: `statsJob` late after 26 hours, `dailyNudges` 2 hours, `sitesJob` 30 minutes, `retentionJob` and `ssoJob` 26 hours; a heartbeat is `ok`, `late`, `failed` (`ok: false`, shown with its `error`), `incomplete` (`complete: false`) or `never`), the audit entries that need attention (`siteLost`, `orgRebrand`, `paymentUnapplied`, `paymentUnmatched` among the newest 200) and the CSP reports. What to alert on is in [docs/OPERATIONS.md](docs/OPERATIONS.md).

Triggers that matter are retried: `onProgressWrite`, `onSubscriptionWrite`, `onOrgLicence` and `onAccountDeletion` retry for six hours and then log `gave up`. A recorded payment also refreshes the buyer's claims itself, right after its transaction, so paid access does not hang on one trigger run.
