A payment a provider confirms that Darsi cannot match is kept in the audit log (`paymentUnmatched`, with `why`: `unknown checkout`, `amount differs`, `unknown order` for Paymob, `unknown subscription` for a Stripe renewal), once per payment and reason on an instance, so a payer who can see their own transaction's signature can't fill the log by sending it again. **An Auth error is never read as "no such account"** (`accountOf` in `billing.ts`): only `auth/user-not-found` means there is none, so a hiccup no longer skips a claims refresh for good; when Auth couldn't say what an account holds before a claims write, the new claims are still written and the sessions end, since nobody can tell whether the change took power away.

## Retention and legal pages

Two mechanisms that do nothing until a platform admin sets them (`functions/src/retention.ts`, `legal.ts`; `/super` → Records). **No period is set and no legal wording is written anywhere in the code**: both are the owner's decisions.

**Retention.** `config/retention.periods` holds whole days (1–3650) or nothing per kind; `retentionJob` deletes what is older, at most 500 documents or files a kind a night, each kind on its own, and audits `retention` when it deleted anything. A missing period means that kind is never touched.

| Period | What goes when it is older |
|---|---|
| `bugReportsClosedDays` | Problem reports marked fixed or closed, by when the status last changed, with their screenshot |
| `bugReportsAnyDays` | Any problem report, by when it was sent, with its screenshot |
| `analyticsDevicesDays` | `analyticsDevices`, by the phone's last active day |
| `checkoutsOpenDays` | `checkouts` still open or failed; never one that was paid, refunded or left unapplied |
| `joinAttemptsDays` | `joinAttempts` |
| `auditDays` | `audit` entries (the log itself, entries about deleted accounts included), except two kinds that are kept for ever: `publishUnit` entries (they hold the units' version counters) and `paymentUnmatched` entries (the only record of money a provider took that matched no checkout). Those two only lose the address and request id of the call that wrote them (`ip`, `rid`) |
| `orgExportsDays` | Files under `exports/` in the bucket |

Accounts, learning records, projects, payments and subscriptions are never read or deleted by it. `saveRetention` saves the periods whole; `previewRetention` counts what the typed periods would delete now, and the screen (`admin/RetentionAdmin.tsx`) shows that count and asks before it saves anything that would delete. Every published version of a legal page is kept for good, whatever the periods say. `saveRetention` refuses 0, a fraction, text, a number over 3650 or an unknown key (`bad-periods`); `previewRetention` answers `{counts, partial}` (`partial` when its 100 s ran out). The job uses single-field queries only (no composite index), asks checkouts by status rather than age (paid ones are kept for ever and would be read again every night), deletes a report's screenshot before the report (only paths directly under `bugReports/`), and stops after 480 s (`stopped`), carrying on the next night.

Before choosing periods, the owner should know: `auditDays` deletes the audit log itself, the entries about deleted accounts and about this job included, and keeps only the `publishUnit` entries (unit id, owner, version and the publisher's account id), because a unit's version counter may be recorded nowhere else, and the `paymentUnmatched` entries (provider, its reference, a checkout id, the reason, amount and currency; nothing personal), because no `payments` document exists for such money (a payment that was recorded but gave nothing keeps its `payments/{id}` with the status `unapplied`, which the job never touches). Once a kept entry is older than the period, the job takes off the address it was written from and its request id (`ip`, `rid`; at most 500 entries a night, not counted as deleted or in the preview), so a publisher's address is not kept longer than anyone else's; the publisher's account id stays; a `checkoutsOpenDays` shorter than the time a provider may still confirm a payment leaves that payment without its checkout; a phone that comes back after its `analyticsDevices` entry was deleted counts as a new phone; `orgExportsDays` covers every file under `exports/`, whatever made it; a closed report without `statusAt` (changed outside the app) is covered only by `bugReportsAnyDays`.

**Legal pages.** `saveLegal({doc: "privacy" | "terms", text: {lang: plain text}, publish})` keeps drafts in `config/legal`, and on publish gives the next version, keeps that version for good (`config/legal/versions/<doc>-<n>`: what someone who accepted version n agreed to), and writes the public `content/legal.json` (`{v, privacy: {version, at, text} | null, terms}`), which the app reads with a plain `fetch` (`legal/published.ts`; guests load no Firebase). Publishing needs an English text. Unpublishing takes the document out of the public file and keeps the draft; a version number is never used twice. If the file cannot be written the call answers `legal-file`, and doing the same again writes it. A text is plain, at most 60,000 characters a language and 400,000 bytes a document (`legal-text`, also for a publish without English); the languages sent replace those in the draft, one sent empty is removed, and the rest stay. The past versions (`config/legal/versions`) are opened by no rule: they are read on the server or in the console. `users/{uid}.terms` holds the latest acceptance only, with one time for both documents; the texts of all versions are kept, a history of each person's acceptances is not.

- **The pages** are `/privacy` and `/terms`: the version, its date and the text, rendered as plain text (blank lines split blocks, `# ` starts a heading), never as HTML.
- **Links** show only for a document that is published: the home page's footers, Me, and under the sign-up form.
- **Agreeing:** while either is published, the sign-up form has a required checkbox naming what is published (it covers "Continue with Google" there too), and after the account exists `acceptTerms({privacy?, terms?})` records the versions on `users/{uid}.terms` (`{privacy, terms, at}`; server-written). Each version given must be the one published now (`legal-version`). `acceptTerms` never creates a profile: with none yet it answers `{ok: true, recorded: false}`, and the app calls again once the profile exists. People who never passed that screen are asked by the terms prompt (see [When something fails](#when-something-fails)). A new version signs nobody out and blocks nothing on the server.

**Problem reports** can be deleted one by one by platform admins (`deleteBugReport`: the screenshot, then the report; audited).

## Organization homepage

An organization's admins put together the page people see at the organization's own web address (`/admin/page`, "Edit the homepage" under Look; platform admins at `/super/orgs/:orgId/page`). The page goes under the organization's name and the sign-in buttons that were always there.
- **Blocks** (`content/page.ts`, one checker for the editor and the server, copied into the functions by `copy-shared.mjs`): text, picture, cards (up to 6), questions (up to 12), contact (email, phone, address, website), button. Up to 30 blocks. Every text is per language; a language left empty shows the page's first ("main") language's text, and the editor says so under the field.
- **The editor** (`admin/PageBuilder.tsx`, a lazy chunk with `styles/page-builder.css`): "Add a block" buttons insert after the open block; blocks go in order by dragging the handle (pointer events, so a finger works too; `touch-action: none`), by the arrow keys on the handle (the position is read out), or by Move up / Move down. Languages: which ones the page has, which one is being written, which one is main. Preview beside the blocks from 900 px, a tab on phones; it is the same component the homepage uses. Save keeps a draft; Publish shows it; Unpublish takes it off (the draft stays). Leaving with unsaved changes asks first.
- **Safety:** everything is drawn as text, never HTML. Links are https only (a button may also be `mailto:` or `tel:`); pictures only the organization's own uploads. Pictures are shrunk on the device (1,600 px, 400 KB, WebP or JPEG) and uploaded to `content/pages/{orgId}/img-<random>.<ext>` (storage rules: that organization's admins and platform admins; public to read).
- **Server** (`functions/src/orgPage.ts`, `manageOrgPage`: get, save, publish, unpublish): the organization's admins (not while it is suspended or being deleted) and platform admins. The draft is `orgs/{orgId}/private/page` {page, rev, updatedAt, updatedBy, publishedRev, publishedAt}; a draft with problems is kept and the problems come back, but only a page without problems is published, to `content/pages/{orgId}/page.json` (five minutes in caches). Audited: `saveOrgPage`, `publishOrgPage`, `unpublishOrgPage`. `deleteOrg` removes the folder.
- **On the address** (`landing/SiteBlocks.tsx` `useOrgPage`): one plain fetch, network first, the last copy kept in `localStorage` for the next start and offline; a 404 removes it. Guests never load Firebase.

## Search and AI engines

The app draws its pages in the browser, so a crawler that reads only the first HTML found an empty shell, and every organization's address said its canonical page was darsiai.org. Now (`functions/src/seo.ts`, pure parts in `seoCore.ts`):
- **Hosting sends `/`, `/sitemap.xml` and `/llms.txt` to `seoPage`**, for darsiai.org and every organization's address (the address comes in `X-Forwarded-Host`). Every other page is `app.html`, a copy of `index.html` the build writes (`appShell` in `vite.config.ts`); `index.html` isn't uploaded (`ignore` in `firebase.json`), which is what lets `/` reach the function. A local preview still has `index.html`, and the service worker still keeps it (Hosting answers it with `app.html`'s content), so installed phones never call the function.
- **`/`** is `app.html` with that address's title, description, canonical, language alternates (`/` and `/?lang=ar`; a first visit with `?lang=` opens in that language, `state/store.tsx`), social card, schema.org JSON-LD, and the page's text inside `#root` (`.seo-pre` in `app.css`), which the app replaces as it starts. darsiai.org: what Darsi is, its tracks, and every public course with its summary (an `ItemList` of `Course`). An organization's address: its name, its homepage's blocks, its contact details (`EducationalOrganization` with `contactPoint`) and its questions (`FAQPage`). Everything is escaped. The security headers Hosting puts on `app.html` are passed on; `Cache-Control: no-cache`.
- **`sitemap.xml` (both languages of `/`, plus `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/licences`) and `llms.txt`** (llmstxt.org: the name, the summary as a quote, the text and links; any origin may read it) are per address. There is no static `public/llms.txt`: Hosting serves a file before any rewrite, so one would hide `seoPage`'s from every address (`tests/agentic-discovery.test.ts`).
- **`/.well-known/ard.json`** (Agentic Resource Discovery v0.91, for Lighthouse's agent-discoverability audit) is a static file in `public/` pointing agents at `llms.txt` and the web manifest, served as JSON any origin may read. The service worker never answers it, `llms.txt`, `robots.txt` or the sitemaps with the app (`navigateFallbackDenylist`).
- **`robots.txt` is the static `public/robots.txt`, the same for every address** (it names darsiai.org's sitemap; submit an organization's own in Search Console). Never rewrite `/robots.txt` or `/favicon.ico` to a function: Google's Functions Framework answers both itself with an empty 404 before any function code runs (the emulator does the same, which is how a local run caught it). `tests/security.test.ts` guards it.
- **Cost:** the shell, `sites.json`, the catalog and each homepage are kept in memory for a minute (an hour if a refresh fails), so a visit is no Firestore read and at most a few Storage reads a minute per instance. The shell comes from `${SITE_URL}/app.html`, so it is always the live release's. If anything fails, the plain shell is sent.
- **Links to the public pages:** once a release has [public pages](#public-pages), `seoPage` reads their list (`${SITE_URL}/site-pages.json`, kept a minute) and darsiai.org's `/` links each course to its own page (else to `/learn`), adds the Explore links, and `llms.txt` lists the pages and `sitemap-pages.xml`. A course published after the release has no page yet, so it gets no link.
- **Not done:** first visits to `/` wait for a function (a cold start can add a second or two); keeping one instance warm (`minInstances: 1`, a few dollars a month) is the owner's call. Search Console: submitting each address's sitemap is the owner's.

## Public pages

Static HTML pages for search engines and AI answer engines (2026-11-15), complete without JavaScript, in English and in Arabic (`apps/web/scripts/site/`).

- **The pages:** `/courses/` (every published course, by track); `/courses/<track>/` (every visible track); `/courses/<id>/` (every public published course: who it is for, level and stage, time, download size per language, every lesson with its video's length and chapters, key terms, question kinds, labs, projects, kit files and pages, the competencies by framework, an FAQ; a capstone shows its parts and milestones); `/features/` and `/features/<key>/` (`lessons`, `labs` with every lab on its shelf, `offline`, `languages`, `kits`, `code` with the courses that have code exercises, `projects`, `schools`, `standards` with every competency the lessons build); `/why-darsi/` (the needs in figures, each with its source and year, and how Darsi answers them). Arabic pages are under `/ar/`; `/ar/` alone sends people on to `/?lang=ar` (a meta refresh with `noindex`: the Hosting emulator ignores `redirects`, so a file is what can be tested). Also `sitemap-pages.xml`, `site-pages.json` (the list, for `seoPage` and tests) and `404.html`.
- **Built with the app:** `publicPages()` in `vite.config.ts` writes them into the build folder after every build (`DARSI_SITE=off` skips it; `DARSI_SITE_SOURCE` is `live`, `repo` or a folder holding `catalog.json`, `settings.json` and `units/`). A production build reads the live catalog, settings and unit files from Storage and never falls back to `content/units` (`strict`); offline it uses the last copy in `apps/web/node_modules/.cache/darsi-site` (unit files by version). By hand: `node apps/web/scripts/site/build.mjs --out <folder> [--source live|repo|<folder>]`, which also copies the fonts it needs. **Course pages change with Hosting releases**, not when a course is published in the editor.
- **Nothing typed about Darsi:** course pages are written from the unit files. Feature pages are handwritten (`copy.mjs`) with every number a `{placeholder}` counted at build time (`facts()` in `features.mjs`: lesson and video lengths as the middle 80%, light-lesson and MP4 sizes as medians, course sizes, labs, code exercises, competencies, runtimes from `content/runtimes.ts`, stages from the app's own strings). A section with `when` shows only while its count is above 0 (the capstones); "every lesson" is said only when it is true of every published lesson (`share`). The rules are in `copy.mjs`'s header: only what the app does today (Darsi Super has no page while it is off), world figures only with a named source and year (`why.mjs` `SOURCES`), Arabic in friendly Modern Standard Arabic with the app's own labels («حسابي»، «مساراتي»), never "·", counted words in the right plural form (`count` in `util.mjs`, `strings.mjs`). Words the app has (track, level and stage names, labs, competencies) come from the app's files (`data.mjs`).
- **Each page:** one stylesheet (`/assets/site-<hash>.css`: the light tokens, the app's own font files and `src/styles/pages.css`, whose `pp-` classes `check-css` owns), no script but one JSON-LD graph (`WebPage`, `BreadcrumbList`, then `Course`, `ItemList`, `Article` with its citations, `FAQPage`; never ratings or reviews), the canonical address, `hreflang` en, ar and x-default, and a social card. English pages load only the Latin fonts: Arabic words on them are `.pp-other` spans in the phone's font. Courses link to the labs they use (`/features/labs/#lab-<id>`), labs and competencies to the courses that use them.
- **Hosting and the service worker leave them alone:** the app's catch-all rewrite skips the page roots (`!/@(assets|lib|courses|features|why-darsi|ar)/**`), so Hosting serves the files, adds the slash to a folder (301) and answers a page that doesn't exist with `404.html`. The service worker never answers them with the app (`PAGE_PATH` in `navigateFallbackDenylist`) and never precaches them (`PAGE_IGNORES` in `globIgnores`). A new root goes in `roots.mjs` and in `firebase.json`'s catch-all; `tests/site.test.ts` checks both. A course id may never equal a track id (both live at `/courses/<id>/`): the build stops.
- **Links in:** the home page (`landing/Landing.tsx`: "See the courses" on each track card, an Explore section and the footer), `seoPage` and `llms.txt` (above), and `robots.txt`, which names `sitemap-pages.xml`.
- **Sizes:** a course page is about 8 KB gzipped, the Labs page about 17 KB, the standards page about 6 KB, the stylesheet about 3 KB.
- **Known limits:** an organization's own address serves the same files, with Darsi's pages and the canonical darsiai.org. "Open this course" (`/lesson/<id>/<first lesson>`) sends a new visitor through the landing page first. Cloudflare adds its own script (and AI Labyrinth's links, while on) to the live HTML. The owner's: submit `sitemap-pages.xml` in Google Search Console and Bing Webmaster Tools; in Cloudflare, Browser Cache TTL "Respect existing headers", Crawler Hints on, Email Address Obfuscation and AI Labyrinth off (none of the pages has an email address).

## Feedback

Anyone can rate Darsi and say what they think, guests included (`bugs/Feedback.tsx`, `functions/src/feedback.ts`).
- **Where:** the round button at the inline start of every screen now opens a two-item menu, "Share feedback" and "Report a problem" (`bugs/Fab.tsx`; `role="menu"`, arrow keys, Escape back to the button, placed above the button from where it is, so it also works on the landing page where the button sits in the page). From 900 px the nav rail has "Share feedback" above "Report a problem". Under a finished course on Home, "How was this course?" offers five stars once per course (`AskCourse`; `state.feedbackAsked`, unit id → time, set by a star or by "Not now"); a star opens the sheet on that course with those stars.
- **The sheet** (lazy chunk `Feedback`, kept out of the precache, strings in `bugs/strings`): five stars (radio buttons drawn as stars, 48 px each, the chosen one's word under them), topic chips, a comment, and a line saying what goes with it. A plain `fetch` to `/api/feedback` with the ID token when signed in (guests never load the SDK). Offline it waits in `darsi:feedback` (10 at most, a month at most) and is sent at start and on `online`, as reports are (`flushFeedback`).
- **What is kept** (`feedback/{id}`): `rating`, `text`, `topic`, `path`, `unitId`, `source` (`button` or `course`), `lang`, `queuedAt`, `user` {uid, email, name, role, orgId} from the token or null, the user agent, `status` (`new`, `read`, `archived`) with `statusBy` and `statusAt`. No screenshot, no device details. Rules: platform admins read it and change only the status; no browser creates or deletes one. Index: `feedback (status, createdAt desc)`.
- **Platform admins** (`/super/feedback`, "Feedback" in the nav, in the SuperAdmin chunk): the average and count in all and in the last 30 days, and how many gave each number of stars, all counted by aggregation queries (no documents downloaded); then New and All, newest first, 30 at a time, with Mark as read, Archive and Mark as new.
- **Accounts:** a person's own feedback (signed in) is in their data download and goes with their account (`account.ts` `feedbackOf`). Guests' feedback names no one.
- **New organization requests:** platform admins get a push (`alertOrgRequest`, opening `/super?tab=requests`), but only on a phone where notifications and Staff alerts are on. Until they are, the Requests tab says so and turns them on in one tap (`admin/StaffAlerts.tsx`).
- **Not done:** no retention period for feedback yet (the retention job knows problem reports, not feedback), and organization admins don't see their members' feedback (the user's choice: platform admins only).

## Problem reports

Anyone can report a problem from any screen, guests included: a round button with a bug at the inline start of the screen (above the bottom nav and the lesson's Continue bar), and "Report a problem" at the foot of the nav rail from 900 px, where the floating button hides (`Layout` sets `data-rail` on `<html>`). On the landing page, whose main button sits where it would float, it closes the page after the footer instead.
- **It never covers a control or the answer sheet** (nor does the tutor's button): both stand on `--fab-end` (app.css), which rises above the answer sheet while it shows (LessonPlayer puts the sheet's height in `--sheet-h` on `<html>`). Below 900 px they also step aside (`useFabsAside` in `bugs/Fab.tsx` sets `data-fabs` on `<html>`): "away" while the page scrolls down (back when it scrolls up, reaches its top or end, or another page opens) and while a video's checkpoint question shows; "sheet" while the answer sheet shows, when the Report button goes and the tutor's sits in the sheet's head beside "Correct!", so "why?" stays in reach without covering the chosen answer. Hidden means faded and not tappable, never removed: keyboards and screen readers still reach them, focus shows them, and so does the tour. One passive scroll listener read once per frame, and observers on `<html>`'s style and (lesson screens only) class changes; no polling. Pages leave `--fab-clear` at their foot (the lesson, the app frame on phones, the pages with no bar, the editor's preview) so the last line scrolls clear, the root's `scroll-padding-block-end` keeps focused controls above the band and the bars (WCAG 2.4.11), and the editor's bar leaves its inline start to the button on phones. No `:has()` (old WebViews). `tests/fab-room.test.tsx` guards all of it.

**On the phone:**
- `bugs/log.ts` (startup, imports nothing, ~1 KB with the button): from `main.tsx` on, it keeps the last 25 errors, unhandled rejections, `console.error` calls, failed loads (scripts, pictures) and failed or refused requests (method, path and status; never a query or a body; requests their own code cancelled are skipped), plus the last 15 pages. In memory only; it leaves the phone only inside a report.
- `bugs/Fab.tsx` (startup): the button, and `ReportBug` in App. The first tap loads the `Report` chunk (~14 KB gzipped, with `modern-screenshot` 4.7.0; not precached), which takes a picture of the screen first, without the button (nor index.html's `<noscript>`, whose text would show as raw markup; `inShot`), then opens the sheet over it.
- The picture: the part of the page on screen (`domToCanvas` of `<body>`, shifted by the window's scroll), at most 1600 px on the long side, JPEG at 0.72 (lower if it would pass ~1.1 MB): about 35 KB on a phone and 110 KB on a laptop. Its fonts are the page language's own (`familiesFor`), inlined from the fonts cache, so an English page never fetches the Arabic files. A browser that can't draw it gets the sheet without one, and anyone can add a picture of their own (shrunk on the phone).
- The sheet (`bugs/Report.tsx`, `styles/bug.css`; a bottom sheet on phones, a panel at the inline start from 900 px): "What went wrong?" (at least 3 characters), the picture with a switch to leave it out, a plain list of what else is sent, and "See everything that is sent" with the exact JSON.
- What goes with the words and the picture (`gather`): the page (address with secret-looking query values replaced by "…", title, scroll, language, direction), the app (build file, service worker, installed, storage used), the device (user agent, platform, languages, viewport, screen, pixel ratio, orientation, touch, memory, cores, time zone, local time, reduced motion), the connection (online, type, speed, Data saver), the account's id and nothing else about it (`details.account = {uid}`, with `offline: true` for a session opened offline; never an email, a role or a plan), the learner's settings (language, audience, stage, tracks, levels, weekly goal, lessons finished, downloads, Data saver, organization) and the log.
- It goes to `/api/bug` with a plain `fetch` (guests never load Firebase); signed in, with the ID token. Offline, or when the request can't get through, the report waits in IndexedDB (`darsi:reports`, at most 5, dropped after 30 days) and goes at the next start or when the connection returns, with the token only if the same account is still signed in (its details still say whose it was). 429 says to wait a few minutes.

**On the server** (`functions/src/bugs.ts`; the pure parts, tested, in `bugsCore.ts`): `reportBug`, an HTTP function behind the Hosting rewrite `/api/bug` (max 2 instances).
- `parseReport` checks everything: the words (3–4,000 characters), the address (secrets replaced again), the details (`clean`: plain data only, strings ≤ 2,000 characters, ≤ 80 keys and 60 items, 6 levels, ≤ 48 KB), and the picture (a JPEG, PNG or WebP data: URL of ≤ 1.2 MB whose own bytes say it is that image; SVG and anything else is refused). The body is at most 1.7 MB.
- Who sent it comes only from the ID token, verified with revocation checked (a bad token is refused with 401, never stored as a guest), plus the profile's name: uid, email, name, role, organization, plan and sign-in method.
- Limits per instance: 8 reports per address per 10 minutes, 10 per account per hour, 120 per hour in all.
- It writes `bugReports/{id}` {createdAt, status "new", text, url, path, user, shot {path, bytes, type, w, h}, details, server {ua, lang}} and the picture to `bugReports/{id}.{jpg|png|webp}` in the app's bucket.
- Rules: only platform admins read either; they change only `status` ("new", "fixed", "closed"), signed with their own uid and the request time; nobody creates or deletes a report from a client (`tests-rules/security.rules.test.ts`). Index: `bugReports (status, createdAt desc)`.

**For platform admins** (`/super/bugs`, "Problem reports" in the nav; `admin/Bugs.tsx` in the SuperAdmin chunk): the new reports or all of them, newest first, 30 at a time, read once. Each shows the words, who (a link to their person page) or "A guest", the page, the browser and screen at a glance, the connection, the last errors, the picture (downloaded only when opened), all the details, and Mark fixed, Close it, Reopen and "Copy the details" (the whole report as JSON, for a ticket or a coding assistant).

## The tour

A guided look around the app with [driver.js](https://driverjs.com) 1.10.2: the real screen dimmed, one thing lit at a time, with a popover that says what it is for. It is in English and Arabic (right to left), and fits the nav rail (from 900 px), tablets and phones.

**When it starts:**
- By itself once per account on this device: the first time a signed-in person reaches the learning screens with onboarding done (tracks chosen), on Home; org and platform admins, who skip onboarding, on their dashboard. Just before it opens it checks again that the app's layout is on screen, no onboarding step is, the page hasn't changed and there is navigation to point at, so it never lands on "Who are you?" when the layout showed for a moment first. It never starts by itself for guests, on a lesson, on the account screens or on "choose a new password", or while a sheet or the track menu is open. `state.toured` (the local store, so each account on each device) records it when the tour starts.
- From Me, "Take the tour" (`openTour()`), for anyone, guests included: it goes to Learn first (the staff dashboard if onboarding isn't done; platform admins, who skip onboarding, go to Learn), so every stop can be shown. For a platform admin, Learn's and the track switch's stops say what they hold for them: every course in every track (`tour.learnAllD`, `tour.trackAllD`).

**On the phone:**
- `tour/Start.tsx` (startup, a few hundred bytes): `AppTour` in App, beside `ReportBug`. It imports no Firebase and nothing lazy.
- The tour itself is the `Tour` chunk (`tour/Tour.ts`, ~11 KB gzipped with driver.js and its stylesheet; not precached), loaded only when a tour starts. Offline before it is cached, the tour simply waits for the next time (`toured` isn't set).
- Its strings are `tour/strings/{en,ar}.json`, registered with `addMessages` when the chunk loads; stop titles reuse the nav's own labels. Other languages show English.
- `stops()` builds the list from what is on screen when the tour starts, so missing things are left out: welcome; the track switcher, this week and the next lesson (Home only); Learn, Downloads, Labs, Kit (not for students) and Me in the rail or the bottom nav, whichever is shown (`shown()` measures, since both are in the page); on the rail, Organization and the staff pages (My students, Admin, Platform, Lesson editor); Report a problem (the rail's item, or the floating button); done. On phones staff hear that their pages are in Me (`tour.meStaffD`).
- Steps give sides as start/end, turned into driver.js's physical sides by the page's direction. The progress line uses the page's numerals ("٣ من ١٢"). Right to left, ← is Next and → is Back (driver.js's own keys are left to right, so they are off there). Highlighted things can't be tapped during the tour (`disableActiveInteraction`), so a tab can't leave the page mid-tour. Reduced motion turns off its fade and smooth scrolling.
- `styles/tour.css` (with the chunk, after driver.js's own sheet): the popover in Darsi's look (tokens, a hard shadow, 48 px buttons, logical sides). driver.js places the arrow by physical side, so the arrow alone is `direction: ltr`.
- Tests: `tests/tour.test.tsx` (the stops on a phone, a wide screen and for staff, Arabic sides, numerals and keys, and when it starts).

## Darsi Super

The paid tier: a live tutor for the courses (docs/SUPER.md has the plan and phases). Phase 0 (the groundwork), phase 1 (payments) and phase 2 (the tutor) are built. Server code is `functions/src/billing.ts`, `payments.ts`, `tutor.ts` and `relay.ts`, with the pure parts, tested, in `billingCore.ts`, `paymentsCore.ts` and `tutorCore.ts`.

**Who has Super:**
- Their own subscription (`subscriptions/{uid}`: plan, status, source `paymob`/`stripe`/`grant`, `currentPeriodEnd`), paid or cancelled but not yet ended; or a seat in their organization's licence (`orgs/{id}.licence` {plan, seats, until} and `users/{uid}.superSeat`). If both apply, the one that lasts longer counts (`entitlement`).
- It goes into the custom claims beside role and organization: `{role, orgId, plan, planUntil}`. Every claims write goes through `writeClaims(uid, role, orgId)`, so a role change (`setRole`, `setUserRole`, `joinOrg`, deleting an org) keeps Super. `createMembers` still sets claims directly, because a new account has no plan. The profile mirrors it (`plan`, `planUntil`, `planSource`) for the admin screens.
- `onSubscriptionWrite` and `onOrgLicence` (a licence change refreshes every seat holder) rewrite the claims. AuthProvider refreshes the token when the profile's `planUntil` differs from the claim's, and exposes `useAuth().plan`.
- Changing organization clears `superSeat`.

**Plans, credits and the margin:**
- Platform admins edit the plans on /super/plans (`admin/Plans.tsx`; it loads with the SuperAdmin chunk, because a lazy route of its own split the startup bundle). `savePlans` stores `config/plans` {plans, economics} and publishes the active plans, without the costs, as `content/plans.json` for the Upgrade screen.
- **The switch:** /super/plans starts with "Darsi Super for everyone", on or off (`setSuperOn`, platform admins). It is `config/settings.superOn` (missing means on), published as `super` in `content/settings.json`, which every phone already reads once per start; `useSuperOn()` (`content/settings.tsx`) remembers the last value in localStorage so a phone where it is off never flashes the tutor. Off: the tutor's button and the Me card are hidden, and /upgrade shows only "Darsi Super isn't available right now" (App passes `superOn` into Upgrade; the plans aren't read), plus Cancel renewal for a card subscription that still renews; `tutorConfig()` reports `on: false`, so `tutorAsk`, `tutorStatus` and the voice relay refuse with `tutor-closed`; `startCheckout` refuses with `super-off`. Subscriptions, credits and the webhooks are untouched, so payments already under way still complete and turning it on again gives everyone back what they had. The relay reads the switch only after it is redeployed with this code.
- A plan is for one person (monthly or yearly), a seat in an organization (priced per seat), or a pack of extra credits (kind `credits`, for people who already have Super). Each has prices in EGP (Paymob) and USD (Stripe) and a number of tutor credits.
- **Credits** (`credits/{uid}` {plan, planUntil, packs}): each paid period brings the plan's credits, and what was left of the last period lapses at its end. A period paid while the last one still runs (an early Paymob renewal, or a plan on top of a running one) adds its credits to what is left, all of it to the new end (`extendPlanCredits`); a new period of the same Stripe subscription replaces them. Packs never expire. Spending takes the period's credits first (`spendCredits`). **A seat brings no credits yet:** `setSeat`, `fillSeats` and a licence payment write the seat and the licence only, so a seat plan's `credits` count in the margin check and nowhere else, and a seat holder has Super in their claims with a balance of 0 (the tutor answers `no-credits`) until they buy a pack or a platform admin gives credits. Until that is built the screens say so: the tutor tells a seat holder (`planSource: "org"`) that the seat gives Super without credits for now (`tu.noCreditsSeat`), and the licence and seat panels say a seat brings no tutor credits yet (`sp.seatNoCredits`); both go when seat credits are built. How a seat's allowance is given, renewed and taken back is the owner's to decide (docs/SUPER.md §1). A stored figure that isn't a number counts as 0 and heals on the next write, and a cost that isn't a number charges the whole hold (fail closed).
- **Economics** {usdPerCredit, egpPerUsd}: what one credit costs Darsi at the AI provider, and the exchange rate. The tutor will debit each call at its measured cost (`creditsForCost`, rounded up).
- **The user's rule: at least 60% margin** (`MIN_MARGIN`). `validatePlans` refuses any price whose credits, all used, would cost the provider more than 40% of it: margin = 1 − credits × usdPerCredit ÷ price in USD. The page shows each price's margin as you type. The margin counts the AI provider's cost only, not payment fees or hosting. Other limits (`validatePlans`; the page repeats them, the server decides): at most 20 plans; an id of 3–32 of `a–z`, `0–9` and `_`; a name in English and Arabic; a price above 0 and at most 1,000,000; credits 0–100,000 (a pack at least 1); `usdPerCredit` above 0 and at most 10; `egpPerUsd` 1–1,000. Each refusal has its own reason (`plan-price`, `plan-credits`, `plan-margin`, `eco-cost`, `eco-rate`, …).
- Platform admins can give complimentary Super (`grantSuper`, days, never counted as revenue; it brings the plan's credits for those days) and extra credits (`grantCredits`, into packs) on a person's page. Taking Super back (`revoke: true`) ends a running subscription and its period's credits now, in one transaction with `credits/{uid}`, and keeps the packs (`revokeEffects`; a second take-back writes nothing). The person page words the plan (its name from `config/plans`), status and source, and decides "Has Super" from the subscription it just read, since the profile's copy is written by a trigger a moment later.
- `savePlans` refusals carry `details: {reason, …numbers}` (`PlanError`: `plan-margin` with `margin` as a fraction, `plan-id`, `eco-rate` …), so /super/plans says them in the page's language with its numerals; a reason it doesn't know shows the server's English.

**Organizations:** `setOrgLicence` (platform admins: plan, seats 1–100000, days ≥ 1; removing is its own `remove: true`, as taking Super back is `grantSuper`'s `revoke: true`, so an empty field never does either, and both ask first on the page) on the org page. Org admins give and take seats (`setSeat`, refused when all are taken) or fill the free ones (`fillSeats`: teachers first, then students; org admins get one only by hand) on their dashboard, which also links to /upgrade with the licence's seats filled in (`?seats=`).
- **A licence sells seat-time** (`licenceAfterPayment`): an expired or missing licence becomes the seats paid for, for the plan's period; a live one gets the larger seat count and an end that keeps every seat-day it had plus every one paid for. Renewing the same seats adds a year; one seat bought for a 1000-seat licence adds about 9 hours.
- **A licence that shrinks frees seats** (`onOrgLicence`, `seatsToRelease`): holders beyond its seats lose theirs, staff first kept (org admins, teachers), then students, by name; a removed licence takes none (they come back with it). The trigger runs up to 540 s, retried for six hours, and refreshes claims 25 at a time; `writeClaims` skips claims that already match, so a retry is cheap. Besides the seat holders and the members it just freed, each run refreshes every member whose profile still says `planSource: "org"` (the mirror `writeClaims` clears only once the claims are written): a member freed by an earlier try whose refresh failed is found again that way, as is a seat taken back by hand whose claims didn't follow (`setSeat` tries them twice).

**Checkouts** (`payments.ts`):
- `startCheckout` {plan, currency, seats?, clientId?}: EGP goes to Paymob, USD to Stripe. A plan for one person needs an account; a pack needs Super already; seats are bought by an org admin for their organization (the price per seat × seats).
- It stores `checkouts/{id}` {uid, orgId, kind, plan (a copy), currency, amount, cents, seats, provider, clientId, status}, the record every webhook is checked against, and returns the provider's page:
  - **Paymob:** an intention (`/v1/intention/`: amount in piastres, the integration ids, the account's email and "NA" for the other billing fields, `special_reference` = the checkout id, `notification_url` = `paymobWebhook`, `redirection_url` = `/upgrade?done=<id>`), then Unified Checkout with the public key and the client secret. Each payment is one period: Paymob's wallets and kiosks can't renew.
  - **Stripe:** a Checkout Session with inline prices: a subscription that renews itself for a plan for one person, a one-off payment for a pack or a licence. The checkout id rides as `client_reference_id` and in the metadata.
- A provider without keys (missing, or the secret set to `unset`) answers `failed-precondition: payments-closed`, and the Super screen says payments open soon.
- **Keys:** secrets `PAYMOB_SECRET_KEY`, `PAYMOB_HMAC_SECRET`, `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`; params (`functions/.env`) `PAYMOB_PUBLIC_KEY`, `PAYMOB_INTEGRATIONS`, and optionally `PAYMOB_BASE`, `STRIPE_BASE` and `SITE_URL`. Every functions deploy needs the four secrets to exist (docs/FIREBASE.md §9).

**Webhooks** (`paymobWebhook`, `stripeWebhook`; HTTPS, at most 5 instances):
- **Paymob:** the "transaction processed" callback, checked with HMAC-SHA512 over Paymob's 20 fields (`?hmac=`). A successful, not pending transaction pays the checkout named by `order.merchant_order_id`; its transaction id is the payment's ref. A full refund or a void refunds it; a partial refund is left alone.
- **Stripe:** the signature (`Stripe-Signature`, HMAC-SHA256 of `t.body`, 5 minutes) on the raw body. Sessions and invoices are read back from Stripe in API version 2024-06-20, so their shape doesn't depend on the endpoint's settings. `checkout.session.completed` (and `async_payment_succeeded`) pays the checkout and maps the subscription (`stripeSubs/{id}`, and `stripeSub` on `subscriptions/{uid}`); `invoice.paid` pays the renewals (not the first invoice); each runs to the end of the period its invoice's lines paid for (calendar months) plus 2 days' grace (`STRIPE_GRACE_MS`) for Stripe's hour-late charge and card retries, but never less than that period's length after time already paid (a card subscription started while a Paymob or complimentary period runs is added after it, and its renewals keep that lead); `charge.refunded` (full only) refunds; `customer.subscription.updated` (read back from Stripe) records a renewal cancelled or restored, or a failing card (`past_due`); `customer.subscription.deleted` marks the subscription cancelled, and Super lasts to the end of what was paid. Events of a subscription the person no longer has (their `subscriptions/{uid}` isn't a card one, or holds another `stripeSub`) change nothing. Both webhooks refuse everything while their signing secret is the placeholder `unset` (`realSecret`): that word is public, so it can't count as a signature. The payment intent id is the payment's ref. A failure answers 500 so Stripe retries.
- Every payment must match its checkout's amount and currency, or it is logged and not recorded.

**Payments and revenue** (only the webhooks call these; nothing calls them from a phone):
- `recordPayment` writes `payments/{provider}_{ref}`, what it buys (`paymentEffects`) and its checkout's status in one transaction: all of it or none, so a webhook that failed half-way is retried in full and a repeated one does nothing. A Paymob subscription payment adds a period from the current end (on a card subscription that still runs, that subscription keeps its source, renewal status and `stripeSub`; otherwise a stale `stripeSub` is dropped); a Stripe one runs to its invoice's period end; a pack adds credits; a licence gets seat-time. The payment keeps what it did (`added` {ms, credits}, or `licence` {before, after}) so a refund takes back exactly that: a licence goes back to `before` unless something changed it since (then it loses the seat-time paid). A licence for an organization that is gone is kept as `unapplied` and audited (`paymentUnapplied`) for a platform admin.
- Each payment adds to `analytics/{day}.revenue.{EGP|USD}` {value, n} and the plan count, and is sent to Google Analytics as a GA4 `purchase` through the Measurement Protocol (`revenueEvent`): `transaction_id`, value, currency, affiliation (individual, organization or credits), the item (a licence is priced per seat), and `client_id` from the phone when the checkout passes it. Nothing in the event names the buyer: there is no `user_id` (even hashed it was one stable id per account), and without the phone's own id the event gets a fresh random `server.<16 hex>` client id. `recordRefund` marks the payment, counts it in `refunds` and sends `refund`; access runs to the end of the period.
- `checkRevenueEvent` (the Plans page) sends a sample purchase to Google's validation server, which records nothing.
- `stats/overview.super` counts live subscriptions by plan and source, and licences with their seats and seats used. /super/analytics shows revenue for 30 days per currency and a Super tile.

**Rules:** `subscriptions` and `credits` are readable by their owner and platform admins; `payments` and `checkouts` by platform admins only; `stripeSubs` by nobody. No client writes any of them.

**On the phone:**
- `/upgrade` (`upgrade/Upgrade.tsx`, a lazy chunk with its own strings, not precached, no Firebase) reads `content/plans.json` with a plain `fetch`, so guests see it too. App passes the address in (`plansUrl`): importing `content/load` from the chunk split the startup bundle. It shows EGP for Arabic or Egypt's time zone and USD otherwise (switchable), the per-period credits, licences for organizations (org admins choose seats and buy), and packs only to people who have Super. Buy calls `startCheckout` (passed in by App, like `plansUrl`) with the phone's analytics id, and opens the provider's page. Coming back with `?done=` reads the token again once, 4 seconds later, with "Check again" for a slow webhook.
- Me has a Darsi Super card (status, or "See Darsi Super").
- Funnel events: `view_paywall` {from} and `begin_checkout` {plan, cur, prov}.

**The tutor** (phase 2; `tutor.ts`, `relay.ts`, the pure parts in `tutorCore.ts`):
- **Courses:** it reads the published catalogs (public, plus the learner's organization's) and unit files from Storage, cached for 30 minutes in a small LRU per kind on each instance (a search index is about 1.6 MB, and keeping every one could fill a 512 MiB instance). A lesson becomes sections (video transcript, reading with key terms, practice with explanations, project), each with an id `<lessonId>/<stepId>`.
- **Search:** BM25 over every section of the unit's track. A section whose key terms the question names gets a bonus (`TERM_BONUS`), and the learner's own course counts 1.5× (`OWN_COURSE`); "example", «مثال» and similar words don't count. Arabic is folded (alef forms, ya, ta marbuta, no diacritics, "al-" dropped). It searches the learner's language and English, since learners switch.
- **Instructions** (`instructions`): answer only from the course material (the current lesson, about 4,000 tokens) and what `search_course` finds; outside the courses, one kind sentence and back to the lesson; Egyptian Arabic or English, following the learner; two to four sentences, ending with a check-question when explaining, and another way of explaining after a wrong answer; never give, or say whether the learner is right about, the answer to a course question or the unit check (hints, the idea with another example, the part of the lesson that settles it); no personal data.
- **Graded answers stay out:** a unit check's questions and explanations (lessons `<unitId>-check`, `isUnitCheck`) are left out of the sections, so neither the lesson material nor the search has them; in a unit check the tutor reads the course's readings instead.
- **Where the models run:** Vertex AI in darsi-f0685 (`backend: "vertex"`, the default since 2026-11-03), signed in as the project's service account (`googleToken`, from the Admin SDK's application-default credential), so the cost goes on the project's Cloud billing account and GCP credits apply. Text uses the global endpoint; `gemini-3.8-live` is on Vertex only in us-central1, so voice goes there. `backend: "gemini"` (or `TUTOR_BACKEND=gemini`, which the emulator checks use) is the Gemini API with `GEMINI_API_KEY`, billed from AI Studio's prepaid balance.
- **Typing** (`tutorAsk`), two engines (`config/tutor.engine`):
  - `"direct"` (the default): `gemini-3.5-flash-lite` with the lesson plus the 4 best sections, answering JSON {inScope, answer, sources}. About $0.003 (1.5 credits at $0.002) a question.
  - `"agent"`: the tutor agent below. If it fails, the question goes the direct way, so the tutor keeps answering.
  - Either way, an answer without a known source becomes the fixed "outside your courses" reply (`finish`).
- **The tutor agent** (`agent/`, Google ADK 2.12 in Python, deployed on Vertex AI Agent Runtime in europe-west1 as `darsi-tutor` by `agent/deploy.py`; the gateway is `functions/src/agent.ts`, its pure parts `agentCore.ts`):
  - **Model:** `gemini-3.8-flash` on the global endpoint, thinking level low, for the tutor and its explainer.
  - **Tools:** `search_course` (calls the `tutorSearch` function, so the same index and ranking as the direct engine and the relay), `reply` (in scope or not, and the sources; it ends the turn) and `make_explainer` (an agent used as a tool through `AgentTool`; as a sub-agent it was also offered as a hand-off target, and the model made two explainers in 140 s).
  - **One model call per answer:** the agent writes its answer as text and calls `reply` in the same response. The gateway has already searched the courses, and says so when the search found nothing, so the agent searches again only for a different word. `make_explainer` ends the turn (`skip_summarization`), and the gateway words the chat line from the explainer's first scene: the model call after it only said "it's ready", and once took 55 s to start.
  - **Sessions:** Agent Runtime Sessions, one per tutor panel (`sessionId` goes back and forth), keyed by the uid. The gateway opens each with the state: `tutor_rules` (the same `instructions()` as the direct engine and the voice, so there's one set of rules), `material`, `lang`, `unit_id`, `org_id` and `search_url`. The model only chooses search words; what it may search comes from state. Because that state is fixed when the session opens, `tutorAsk` records the session it gave the panel on `tutorLeases/{uid}.session` {id, orgId, unitId, lessonId, lang} and passes the phone's `sessionId` on only when it is that one, for the caller's organization now and this course, lesson and language; any other id gets a new session (someone removed from an organization can't go on asking in a session opened on its private course). Each question goes with the 4 sections the gateway's own search found for it ("SECTIONS THAT MAY HELP").
  - **What comes back:** the agent writes `reply`, `explainer` and `usage` into session state, so they come back as state deltas on the `:streamQuery` events (`readTurn`). The answer is the tutor's text before `reply`; text before another tool call, and a tool's own output, don't count. `usage` is zeroed when a turn starts and added to by every model call, the explainer's included (thinking counts as output).
  - **Cost** (`turnCost`): tokens at 3.8 Flash's prices ($0.75 / $3.75 per million until 31 Dec 2026, then $1.50 / $7.50: `agentPricesAt` switches on 1 Jan 2027 by itself, and `config/tutor.agentPrices` overrides either; config prices that aren't numbers ≥ 0 are logged and ignored), plus Agent Runtime's compute for the turn's seconds (4 vCPU and 8 GiB at $0.085 and $0.009 an hour). Measured: an answer about $0.002–0.004; an explainer turn about $0.014 (`tutorStatus.explainer` shows it in credits).
  - **A turn that doesn't answer is still charged** (`AgentError`): a turn past its time limit at what it surely cost (its input once and the runtime meanwhile, `abandonedCost`), a reply-less turn or a failed repair at what was measured. `tutorAsk` charges it with the direct answer that takes its place; with no answer at all the learner isn't charged, but the cost is counted.
  - **Time limits:** an ordinary answer may take 8 s and an explainer 75 s (`ANSWER_MS`, `EXPLAINER_MS`) before the question goes the direct way (Flash-Lite, about a second). A question is text only, so it does not wait on an animation. `tutorAsk` allows 180 s and the phone's callable waits as long (`firebase.ts` `SLOW`), so an explainer, its repair turn and the direct answer all fit.
  - **The board:** an animation is made only when the learner asks to see it (Show me, or the voice tool `explain`). The phone shows the board at once with "Let me show you on the board", and the pictures start playing when they arrive. On a call the tutor says that sentence first, then talks through the scenes as they play.
  - **Speed (2026-11-03):** 3.8 Flash is what an answer waits for. With the same questions and rules it took a median of 3.4–4.1 s, with stalls of 29–35 s; Flash-Lite took 0.9 s every time. The user chose to keep 3.8 Flash. Streaming the text wouldn't help: the whole answer arrives within a few hundred milliseconds of the first word.
  - **Warm-up:** the functions and the relay scale to zero, and a cold start cost 3–7 s. When the panel opens, the phone calls `tutorAsk` with `{warm: true}` (that instance loads the course and its index, and opens the agent's session, whose id the phone keeps) and asks the relay for `/warm`. Once per lesson, for Super accounts only; nothing is charged.
  - **Quota:** Agent Runtime allows 30 queries a minute per region for the project (and 10 writes). A question is one query and a warm-up one more; past that the agent answers 429 and the question goes the direct way. More learners need a quota increase.
  - **Eval after the speed work (agent `6058503682603876352`):** 94/104 right and no wrong answers; the 10 others were errors that live goes the direct way (6 over 12 s, 3 quota refusals, 1 turn without `reply`). Median 4.0 s, 90th percentile 7.6 s, $0.40 in all.
  - **Measured (2026-11-03, agent `reasoningEngines/7612245554046697472`):**
    - The eval through the agent passed 102/104: every outside question refused, Arabic 52/52, a median of 4.4 s and a 90th percentile of 9.1 s, $0.0048 a question ($0.498 in all, against $0.146 for the direct engine). Its two misses were errors (a 90 s timeout and one model error) and answered right on a rerun; live, they'd have gone the direct way.
    - Explainers through the gateway: English 30 s and $0.017 (5 scenes, 7.9 KB), Arabic 25 s and $0.016. Both played in the light player at 375 px, the Arabic one right to left.
  - **Who may call:** Agent Runtime doesn't see Darsi's users. The gateway checks the ID token, Super and the credits before every call, and calls the agent as the compute service account. `tutorSearch` answers only a Google ID token made for its own address by `config/tutor.agentAccount` (checked with Google's tokeninfo and cached until it expires; `Bearer emulator` on the emulators).
- **Explainers** (`functions/src/explainer.ts`):
  - **What the agent writes:** flat scenes (`agent/darsi_tutor/explainer.py`): one shape for the templates `title-hook`, `steps`, `flow`, `message-mock`, `quiz` and `summary`, 4 to 6 scenes, the narration in the learner's language and the player's own icons. Free `canvas` scenes stay out until they're checked on phones.
  - **The package:** `toPackage` turns them into Lesson Studio scenes (reveal cues on words actually said, edges only between known nodes, a quiz only with its answer among the options), adds a one-language manifest (`ai` disclosure, brand `darsi-light`) and timing from `estimateTiming`. It then checks the whole package with Lesson Studio's `validatePackage`. A rejected explainer gets one repair turn with the problems, then is dropped. A typical explainer is 5–10 KB of JSON.
  - **The checker's source:** `validatePackage` and `estimateTiming` come from `functions/vendor/lessonstudio-spec.js`, built by Lesson Studio's `scripts/darsi/vendor-spec.mjs`; its types are hand-written beside it. A test checks that the Python schema's templates and icons are ones Lesson Studio knows.
  - **On the phone:** `tutor/Explainer.tsx` plays the package with the light player from memory (`LightLesson`'s `pkg`; nothing is fetched). App passes the player in (`Player`): importing `lesson/LightLesson` from the tutor chunk split startup into seven files. Typing, it waits for Watch and plays silently with captions. In a call it starts by itself.
  - **Show me:** in-scope answers have "Show me" with the explainer's cost. The voice tutor has an `explain` tool (`EXPLAIN_TOOL`): the relay asks the agent, sends `{type:"explainer", pkg}` to the phone, and gives the voice the scenes' narration to talk through. The voice can drift a second or two from the scenes' estimated timing. The relay charges explainers on top of the voice's own cost, one at a time per call. A learner who hangs up before the drawing comes back is still charged for it: the call's held credits are settled and released only after the agent's turn returns (the voice place with them), and a failed one is charged at what it cost (`AgentError`). On SIGTERM the relay waits up to 8 s for those last charges.
- **Talking** (the relay, a WebSocket server on Cloud Run, `node lib/relay.js`): the phone sends `hello` with its ID token; the relay checks Super, credits and the course (side by side where it can), then opens a Gemini Live session while the course's search index builds (`gemini-3.8-live`, voice Kore, the instructions, `search_course`, transcripts both ways, server voice detection with 700 ms of silence). Audio is 8 kHz μ-law both ways (about 64 kbit/s); the phone sends it only while the learner speaks, plus a second of the pause. It answers tool calls itself, sends captions and sources, and closes after 90 s of silence, at `maxMinutes` (14), or when credits run out. Only darsiai.org (and localhost) may connect. `GET /warm?u=<unit>&l=<lang>` answers 204 and warms the settings, Google's token and a public course's index (Cloud Run reserves `/healthz`). Measured from here: Live's first word comes about 1.3 s after the learner stops, a course search included; the relay is in europe-west1 and `gemini-3.8-live` only in us-central1.
- **Charging** (`charge`): every answer, and every spoken turn, costs the higher of Google's reported tokens (audio and text at their own prices) and, for voice, the audio's own minutes at Google's per-minute prices, turned into credits at `economics.usdPerCredit` and rounded up. A call that cost more than the balance takes what was left. `tutorUsage/{uid}/days/{day}` and `analytics/{day}.tutor` count the cost and credits.
- **Settings:** `config/tutor` {backend, textLocation, liveLocation, liveModel, textModel, voice, live, text (USD prices), maxMinutes, on} overrides the defaults in `tutor.ts`. `GEMINI_API_KEY` is a secret for `tutorAsk`, `checkCapstone` and the relay, used only with `backend: "gemini"` (`unset` closes the tutor then). It must exist for every functions deploy, but since 2026-11-03 it holds a dead key (the "Darsi tutor (Gemini)" API key was deleted), so `backend: "gemini"` needs a new key first. The relay needs `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` for Vertex.
- **On the phone:** every lesson screen has a floating button with the tutor's face at the inline end (right in English, left in Arabic), above Continue (`tutor/Fab.tsx`, rendered by `LessonTutor` in App.tsx; it and the face, `tutor/Face.tsx`, are on the startup path and import nothing else; their styles are `styles/tutor-fab.css`, global). It opens `TutorPanel` beside the lesson: a sheet up to 88vh on phones, a 420 px side panel from 900 px; Escape or Close shuts it and any call, and moving to another lesson closes it. The panel is the lazy `tutor/Tutor.tsx` chunk (6.8 KB gzipped on 2026-11-07, not precached), loaded on the first tap while the button pulses; `/tutor?unit=…&lesson=…` shows the same body as a page. App passes in `tutorAsk`, `tutorStatus` and `VITE_TUTOR_RELAY`.
- **The avatar:** Salma from the Lesson Studio cast, drawn inline (no request, works offline), not a streamed video avatar: it costs no data or credits. Its mouth opens with the tutor's voice (`Speaker` reports the loudness of every 50 ms as it is heard, `mouthFor`), a ring grows with the learner's voice while it listens (teal) and pulses with the tutor's while it speaks (orange), its brows lift and it bobs while thinking or connecting, and it blinks. The panel sets `--mouth` and `--ear` on the avatar's element directly, so the animation never re-renders React. Opening the panel spends nothing: talking starts with a tap.
- **The conversation:** People without Super see what it is and a link to the Super screen. It shows the credits and roughly the minutes of talking left, Type (the default with Data saver or 2G) and Talk, the conversation with each answer's source lessons (links) and cost. Talk uses an AudioWorklet for the microphone with echo cancellation (Hosting's `Permissions-Policy` allows `microphone=(self)`; with `microphone=()` the browser refuses without asking, and the local preview doesn't send that header, so `tests/tutor.test.tsx` guards it. The service worker answers pages from its precached index.html with the headers it was cached with, so `vite.config.ts` puts a hash of Hosting's headers into index.html's revision: a header change alone reaches installed phones), and raises its voice threshold while the tutor speaks, so the speaker isn't heard as the learner.
- **Eval:** `scripts/tutor-eval.mjs` (104 questions in `scripts/tutor-eval.json`: a key term of each of 36 teacher courses in English and Arabic, asked from the course's first lesson, and 16 questions outside the courses per language). It grades scope, the source course, the language and the length; the bar is every outside question refused and 90% right overall.
**Checked (phase 2):** functions 129 tests (`tutor.test.ts` 8: sections, search in both languages, the instructions, costs, the audio codec); 20/20 checks against the emulators with a stand-in for Gemini's REST and Live APIs and the relay run locally (status, typed answers with sources and their charge, outside answers replaced, Arabic, no Super or credits, the Live setup, search_course, audio both ways, captions, the per-turn charge, running out, other websites refused); in the browser on the emulators, "Ask the tutor" from a lesson, a typed answer with its source and cost, at 375 px. **On Vertex AI (2026-11-03):** the eval passed 104/104 (72 in scope, 32 outside refused, 52 per language) for $0.146; its first run, before the search changes, passed 90/104. A real Live session with the relay's setup: an 8 kHz spoken English question heard as "fishing" was searched as "phishing" (after the voice rule "search first" was added; before it, the tutor declined without searching) and answered from cyber-1-1-2 with a check question, first audio 0.9 s after the question ended, $0.012 for the exchange; an Arabic question got an Egyptian Arabic answer from the same lesson ($0.013). Vertex's Live `usageMetadata` has the same shape as the Gemini API's (prompt and candidate tokens per modality, plus thoughts).

**Checked (phase 1):** functions 121 tests (`payments.test.ts` 8: amounts, both signatures, Paymob's outcomes, Stripe's sessions and form encoding); 26/26 checks against the emulators with a local stand-in for both providers (the intention, closed Stripe, who may buy what, forged and replayed callbacks, a wrong amount, a licence, refunds, a Stripe subscription with a renewal, a partial and a full refund, and cancelling); in the browser on the emulators, Buy opened the stand-in Paymob page and, after the signed callback, `/upgrade?done=` showed Super. Not yet tried against Paymob's or Stripe's real test modes: that needs their test keys.

**Checked (phase 0):** functions 113 tests (`billing.test.ts` 15), web 1201; 34/34 checks against the emulators (scratch script): the margin refusal, plans published without costs, grant → claims, mirror and credits, a role change keeping Super, the credits rules, licence and seat limits, `fillSeats`, idempotent payments, revenue counts, packs, refunds, payments readable only by platform admins, and the overview.

### The tutor in production: credits, deadlines and the relay

- **Held credits always come back.** The hold is on the lease: `tutorLeases/{uid}.calls[id]` = `{kind, until, hold {fromPlan, fromPacks}}`. A lease past its `until` gives its hold back (`reclaim`), inside `startCall`'s transaction and inside `balance()`, always in one transaction with the credits, so a hold is returned once only. A call that never reached `endCall` (a function stopped at its limit, a relay instance replaced) therefore costs nothing for long: a typed question's lease lasts 200 s, a voice call's `maxMinutes + 2` minutes. `endCall` with its lease already gone charges the cost directly, never below zero.
- **`tutorAsk` keeps to one deadline and always settles.** 165 s from when the call arrives (the function stops at 180 s); the agent gets the deadline, a rejected explainer is repaired only when enough time remains, and the direct answer gets what is left. The settle is in `finally`: an answer is settled at its cost, no answer gives the hold back. A settle that fails is logged as `tutor: call not settled` and never thrown: the learner keeps the answer and the lease returns the hold.
- **`checkCapstone` asks Auth about the account first**, so a suspended account's still-valid token costs no GitHub requests and no paid review (`disabled`, `no-account`).
- **Seats and licences:** `fillSeats` loops on the server (450 people a pass under the seat lock, up to 240 s) and answers `{given, more}`; the screen calls again while `more` (at most 40 calls a press, saying how many so far; still `more` after that says to press again). "Take back" and "Remove licence" ask on the page first (`staff/InlineConfirm`). `setSeat`, `fillSeats` and a seat checkout refuse an organization that is suspended or being deleted (`org-suspended`, `org-deleting`, read from the org document the seat transaction holds), with one way left open: a platform admin can still take a seat back (`setSeat` with `on: false`) there; giving one is refused for everyone. `grantSuper` writes the credits and the subscription in one transaction.
- **The voice relay** (`functions/src/relay.ts`, Cloud Run `darsi-tutor`):
  - *Isolation:* everything a hello does (the token, the settings, the credits, the course, opening Gemini Live) runs inside one try/catch. A failure closes that socket with `tutor-gone`, releases its hold and logs one error; a promise nobody caught is logged and the relay keeps running; an exception nobody caught settles the calls and exits 1, so Cloud Run starts another instance.
  - *Shutdown:* on SIGTERM it stops accepting, tells every phone `tutor-gone`, ends every call (the last charge, then the hold released), waits up to 8 s for those writes and exits 0 (Cloud Run gives 10 s).
  - *Capacity:* `RELAY_MAX_SOCKETS` (300 by default) is how many phones one instance holds, and `RELAY_MAX_PENDING_PER_IP` (40) how many sockets from one address may be waiting to finish their hello. Only those count per address, so a class behind one address can talk; once signed in the limit is one voice call per account. Over either limit the socket is accepted, told `busy` ("The tutor is busy right now. Try again in a minute.") and closed. Keep `RELAY_MAX_SOCKETS` about 10 under the service's `--concurrency`, and the pending limit at half of it or less (docs/FIREBASE.md §9).
  - *Health and logs:* `GET /health` answers `{ok, sockets}`. Every log line is one JSON object with a severity; tokens, audio and what anyone said are never logged.

## Content, lessons and kits

- **Content is data, and the app ships none.** Every course a learner sees is published with the course editor to Cloud Storage (see [Authored content](#authored-content)); units are typed by `src/content/types.ts`. Every learner-facing string is `{ "en": …, "ar": … }` (or more languages), with English as the fallback.
- **The starting courses** are sources in `content/units/` at the repo root, with their kits in `content/kits/` and their media in `content/media/`. There are 96 (2026-11-07): AI 16, and 10 in each of the other eight built-in tracks (IoT, Cyber security, Programming, Data engineering, Data science `ds-…`, AI agents, Optimization, AI security). Per track: a teachers' course per level (`<track>-<level>-1`; the AI, Cyber and IoT Beginner ones, `ai-1-1`, `cyber-1-1` and `iot-1-2`, came from the course packs), a students' course per stage (`<track>-s<level>`, `audience: "students"`), the level-3 students' capstone (`<track>-capstone`, `kind: "capstone"`; see [Capstones](#capstones)) and a university course (`<track>-u1`, `teach: "university"`); AI also has `ai-u2`–`ai-u4` and the students' `ai-d1`–`ai-d3`. That makes 48 for students (capstones included), 12 university courses and 9 capstones (docs/CURRICULUM.md has the framework and the ids; `docs/curriculum/<track>.md` each track's plan). **What is live** is `content/catalog.json` in Storage, not this folder: 51 courses on 2026-11-07 (per track the teachers' Advanced course, the university course, the students' Intermediate and Advanced courses and the capstone, plus `ai-u2`–`ai-u4` and `ai-d1`–`ai-d3`). The primary and secondary courses were unpublished on 2026-11-05 and keep their drafts. `npm run seed -- <id>…` publishes sources through the editor's own publish code (`publishDraft`), uploading the kits and media, so they are ordinary published units that platform admins can edit. Their ids are kept, so progress, notes and certificates still match.
  - Courses quote numbers from the Labs apps they use, so after changing a lab's model, search `content/units` for its id.
- **Retired courses** are in `content/retired/`. `ai-1` ("AI basics for teachers", the first starting course) was taken off the site on 2026-10-28 with `npm run seed -- --unpublish ai-1`. The unit tests and the e2e suite still use it, and its draft stays in the editor. Its learners keep their progress. Since 2026-11-06 a certificate stays once Home or Me has recorded it (`state.certs`, and signed in `users/{uid}/certs`: "Cloud sync"); one earned before then is worked out only while its course is in the catalog.
  - **Media** (`content/media/<lesson>/`, gitignored, about 15 MB per course) comes from the Lesson Studio repository: the 240p MP4 and captions per language, and the light lesson. `scripts/import-packs.mjs` made it for the three pack courses; `scripts/import-media.mjs <unit>…` does it for every other course, filling each video step from the Lesson Studio lesson with the same id (docs/CURRICULUM.md has the whole pipeline). From a checkout that has no Lesson Studio beside it (a worktree), pass `--studio <Lesson Studio repository>` (or set `LESSON_STUDIO`); the script stops when the folder isn't one. The seed uploads each file to `content/media/<unit>/<content hash>/…` with a one-year immutable cache, so a re-seed sends only what changed. Media that isn't on the computer keeps its app path.
  - **Pictures in lesson text:** a read or setup paragraph that is only `![caption](/media/…)` is a figure (the capstones' screenshots, `content/media/capstone-shared/`). The seed uploads them like other media and rewrites each such paragraph to `![caption](<Storage URL>)` without isolates (`scripts/lib/kits.mjs`); a unit whose pictures aren't on the computer isn't published, since Hosting answers a `/media/` path with the app page. The app shows only `/media/` paths (the dev server serves `content/media` there) and this project's published course media (`content/figure.ts`), and a download saves them (`unitAssetUrls`). `sizeByLang` counts them: publishing reads each picture's size from Storage (`figureSizes` in `content.ts`, `figureUrls` in `catalog.ts`).
  - **Video languages beyond English and Arabic** (since 2026-11-15, pipeline only; no course is voiced in them yet). A Lesson Studio storyboard can have `fr.json`, `es.json`, `de.json` and `pt-BR.json` beside `ar-EG.json` (the same shape, plus `strings`: the design's on-screen words keyed by their English); the converter makes every language present. `scripts/lib/studio-media.mjs` maps Darsi's codes onto them (`EXTRA_LANGS`: `fr`, `es`, `de` as they are, `pt` → `pt-BR`) and `import-media` takes a language only once Lesson Studio has narrated and rendered it, filling `sources`, `captions`, `light.langs`/`files`/`sizeKB`, the chapters' and checkpoint's `atBy` and the transcript for it. `lightLang` then plays it on pages in that language and English elsewhere. The video's transcript is what its narration says; a course translation in `content/i18n/units` must not overwrite it.
  - **Kits** that exist only in English (the packs' own lesson plans, worksheets and answer keys) are uploaded in English only; `kitUrl` falls back to English.
  - `tests/units.test.ts` checks every starting course: no errors and no warnings (Arabic included), the content rules in docs/CONTENT.md, the published form (Storage URLs) and no "·" in Arabic.
- **Light lessons.** A video step may carry `light`: the same lesson as a Lesson Studio package (manifest, scenes, per-language timing and narration), which the phone draws itself. It's about 380 KB per language with the narration, against about 1.1 MB for the 240p MP4, and stays sharp on any screen.
  - `lesson/LightLesson.tsx` plays it with the vendored Lesson Studio player (`src/vendor/lessonstudio-player.js`, an ES module of `createPlayer`, about 26 KB gzipped, precached so downloaded lessons play offline; refresh it with `node scripts/darsi/vendor-player.mjs` in the Lesson Studio repository, which also copies its `public/lib` here). The player's `resolve` maps package paths to `light.files` URLs; `library` points at `/lib/`.
  - **Science kit** (since 2026-10-28): videos can draw equations, plots, neural networks trained for real, model architectures and tables (Lesson Studio `docs/science.md`). They come in technology packs (Lesson Studio `docs/packs.md`): science, code, data, iot, network and opt. Each is its own file, `src/vendor/lessonstudio-pack-<id>.js` (science ~14 KB gzipped), loaded only for lessons that use it. Packs aren't precached (`globIgnores`). Downloads saves the ones a course's light lessons need (`prefetchLightPacks`, using the vendored `packsUsed` and `prefetchPacks`). The vendored files are `lessonstudio-player.js`, the packs and `lessonstudio-chunk-*.js` (shared code); startup stays 4 files.
  - **Bundle:** the player is a lazy chunk that App registers with `setLightPlayer(() => import(…))`, and it imports nothing. A lazy `LightLesson` component (sharing React and `content/load` with startup code) split the startup bundle into six files, and moving its `import()` into App didn't help; only a chunk with no shared imports keeps startup at four.
  - `StepView` shows the light lesson first when it has the teacher's language (`lightLang`: `light.langs`, e.g. `ar` → `ar-EG`, else English), with "Watch as a video file (1.1 MB)" to switch to the MP4 and "Light version" to switch back. The player asks its own quick checks, so the step's checkpoint question is for the MP4 and the transcript. Each check has `explain` (shown after a right answer) and `explainWrong` (after a wrong one; the converter takes it from the storyboard's `feedback_wrong`), because the right-answer text opens with "Correct —". The step can continue once a check is answered or the lesson ends, as with the video. `StepView` gives the player its own words (`labels`): the check's button says "Keep watching" (the lesson's Continue beside it moves on and skips the rest of the video), and the end card says "Video finished" with "Quick checks right: {n} of {m}", which the player fills in the lesson language's digits (ar-EG: ١ من ٢). Its defaults ("Continue", "Lesson complete") are for a lesson that is only the video. The explanations open with their own verdict ("Correct —", «مش بالظبط:»), which the player drops under its own verdict line (`withoutVerdict` in Lesson Studio's `labels.ts`; code at the start keeps its case). Opening the transcript hides the player, paused (`LightLesson`'s `hidden`), so closing it goes on from the same moment with its checks answered (until 2026-11-07 it started again at 0:00); a step that opens on its transcript loads the player only once the transcript is closed. The Transcript buttons carry `aria-expanded`.
  - Each language is narrated separately, so chapter and checkpoint times can differ: `atBy[lang]` holds that language's own time, read with `timeIn()`.
  - A chapter starts a scene: `import-media` times it from the storyboard scene that starts at the chapter's `at`. A time that starts no scene is reported and put at the next scene's start (until 2026-11-07 it silently became 0:00, so 22 "Remember" chapters in ai-d1–d3 and ai-u2–u4 sat at 0:00). The validator warns when a chapter, in any language, isn't after the one above it.
  - Downloading a unit stores the light lesson (its JSON, the narration in Opus or, where the phone can't play Ogg Opus, AAC, and the library) instead of the MP4. The player picks the same way: the Opus files are Ogg, and Safari before 18.4 (iOS 17.4–18.3, Macs before 15.4) plays Opus only in WebM, so asking about WebM gave it a file it couldn't play and the lesson ran silently. If the Opus file still fails, the player tries the AAC file once from the same moment, and only then goes on with captions and no sound (`no-audio`).
  - **Its controls fit the lesson column** (Lesson Studio player, 2026-10-28): under 720 px the floating bar drops the volume slider and the time; under 480 px (phones) play, time and seek share one row and the other buttons a second, with "Made with AI help" as a badge on the picture. Under 320 px (a portrait video on a phone, 16rem) the row drops the next-scene and volume buttons (chapters and the phone's keys cover them) and wraps rather than overlapping. A quick check or the end screen takes the picture's place in a player under 720 px, which the lesson column always is (until 2026-11-06 only under 480 px: on laptops the paused picture repeated the question with options that did nothing, and pushed the card under the Continue bar); wider players put it under the picture. Full screen falls back to stretching over the page where the Fullscreen API on elements is missing (iPhone), and the pause icon is a filled shape (it was invisible). The player lives in a shadow root, so the document names the host as the full-screen element: the player asks its shadow root. Until 2026-11-06 it compared the document's with itself, so on Android and desktop it also stretched over the page in real full screen, never turned the screen to landscape, read the seek bar sideways, and needed a second tap to leave. The focus ring is two-tone (a dark ring around a yellow band) so it shows on the cream stage and the dark bar alike, and the chapters and settings menus move with the arrow keys, Home and End, with Escape back on their button (Tab moves on from it). The progress line tells screen readers its place as a time ("0:06 of 3:42", «٠:٠٦ من ٣:٤٢»), not raw seconds, and the "Made with AI help" badge on the picture is 8:1 (it was 3.2:1). When the learner picks "Portrait video", `LightLesson` sizes the stage itself (`fitStage`), on every screen: it measures the room between the player's top and the bottom of the screen (titles take one line or three), keeps 15.5rem for the row under the player (Transcript, the video's shape, the video file; the controls sit on the picture) and the Continue bar, and makes the portrait (9:16) stage as wide as fits, centred, but never under 16rem (a short screen scrolls a little). It measures again when the phone turns. (Until 2026-11-04 it fell back to landscape under 12rem, which is most phones once the browser's bars are counted, and left wide screens to the player, which chose landscape: the button changed nothing.) Full screen lifts the player host's `contain: content` while it stretches over the page (`data-ls-full`), which had clipped the picture away on iPhone, and measures the picture again once the page settles after leaving full screen. Checked at 360×640 to 412×915 in English and Arabic.
- **Lesson shape:** video (with a checkpoint question) → reading with key terms → mixed questions (some lessons add a `lab` step that embeds any Labs app) → classroom project → reflection → "Try it in class tomorrow".
- **Step types:** `video`, `read`, `choice`, `multi`, `truefalse`, `image`, `match`, `order`, `fill`, `type`, `hotspot`, `scenario`, `lab`, `project`, `reflect`, and the code steps `code`, `parsons`, `codefill`, `bug` and `blocks` (see "Code on the phone"). Any question may show a `snippet` of code.
