- **Add:** pick from common languages (names prefilled, direction from the code) or type a code. Each language has its own name, English name, writing direction and number locale, with a live sample.
- **Translate** (`/super/languages/:code`): one card per UI string with the English and Arabic for reference. Search; filters for "Not translated" and "To fix" (placeholders that differ from the English, which block publishing); a progress bar. Drafts are saved in `translations/{code}` with a `rev` check, by themselves as the course editor saves (`useAutosave`: 3 s after the last change, when the tab is hidden, and as the page closes, the app's own navigation included); Back saves first and asks inline only if that fails. No browser dialogs (the Tracks page asks inline too: its changes publish to phones, so they never save by themselves).
- **Spreadsheets:** download `.xlsx` or `.csv` (key, English, Arabic, the language), translate anywhere, import it back (`cms/sheets.ts`). The text column is the one headed with the code or name; empty cells are skipped, and keys the app doesn't have are reported and left out.
- **Publish** (`publishLanguage`): the page first saves the draft with only the keys the app has, trimmed; the function writes the immutable pack `content/i18n/{code}/v{n}.json` and lists the language in the settings. **Unpublish** takes it off the pickers. A draft can be deleted while unpublished.
- **What a pack translates** (`i18n/translatable.ts`, `PACK_EN`): the app's strings (`i18n/en.json`) plus the learner-facing strings lazy chunks register (the tour, Report a problem, the tutor, Upgrade): 755 in all. A pack is merged into the language's strings when it loads, so those chunks find their translations there. Staff strings (`admin/`, `cms/`) stay English and Arabic: in a pack, every learner would download them. Push wording has its own copies (`MORE` in `functions/src/messages.ts`), and the built-in track and level names carry them in `content/tracks.ts` (settings that kept a built-in name keep its translations, `mergeTracks`).
- **Phrases** (`i18n/phrases.ts`): the content written as English and Arabic in code and files (Labs, the placement check, the home page) is translated for an added language in `public/content/phrases/<lang>/<area>.json`, a map from the English text to the translation. Areas: `labs` (shelves, every app's title and summary, the kit, app files other apps import), `lab-<id>` (one app's tables), `placement` and `landing`. A screen fetches only its areas, in the page language, with a plain request (no list of files ships; a missing one is a 404 or the app page, and the text stays English); the service worker keeps them in `darsi-content`, and they are not precached. `L()` looks a LocalText's English up there when the text has no copy in the language; `useS()` translates a lab's table the same way (`translated` in `labs/kit/strings.ts`), and a function string is keyed by what it returns for `{0}`, `{1}`… (one that does more than fill in its arguments stays English). `labs/loader.tsx` loads `labs` and `lab-<id>` before the app shows, and "Save all labs" saves them too. `node apps/web/scripts/phrases.mjs` reports coverage and strings no longer used; `sources <dir>` and `missing <lang> <dir>` write what to translate. `tests/phrases.test.ts` checks markers and script rules. Still English for added languages: broadcasts (written in English and Arabic) and `lang === "ar"` branches inside some labs.
- **Staff screens** (admin, the course editor): their strings ship English and Arabic inside their lazy chunks; an added language's are `public/content/i18n-staff/<lang>/{admin,cms}.json` (keyed like `en.json`), fetched when a staff chunk registers its area (`addStaffArea` in `i18n/index.tsx`) and whenever the language changes. Not precached; learners never load them. Plans (`/super/plans`) take a name per published language (`sp.nameIn`; `validatePlans` accepts language codes, English and Arabic required).
- **Courses** (`scripts/unit-i18n.mjs`): a course's translations into an added language are `content/i18n/units/<lang>/<unitId>.json`, `{ "<path>": { en, t } }` (path = where the text sits in the unit, `en` = the English it was made from, `t` = the translation in the English's shape, fill-in `{blank}` objects in place). `apply()` adds `t` only where the unit's English is still `en`, so a rewritten sentence shows in English until it is translated again (the coverage report and `tests/unit-i18n.test.ts` list stale ones). A university course's pack is `pack-<unitId>.json` beside it. `npm run seed` applies them before saving the draft, so the editor and the published course carry every translated language; `build-kits.mjs` builds each course's kits in its translated languages (`--langs=fr,es` builds only those; the kits' own wording is `content/i18n/kits/<lang>.json`). Commands: `sources <dir> [ids]` / `missing <lang> <dir>` (what to translate), `assemble <lang> <dir>` (checks shapes and writes the files), `labels <lang> [--write]` (quoted Labs buttons → the lab's own label in that language).
- **Per-language course files:** `publishDraft` writes `v{n}.json` with English and Arabic only, and for each added language the unit's title has, `v{n}.{lang}.json` with that language and English (`inLanguages`, `extraLangs` in `functions/src/catalog.ts`); the catalog entry lists them in `langs` and `sizeByLang` counts each file. `loadUnit` fetches the page language's file (`configureContent({ lang })`), so a phone downloads one language, not seven.
- **Packs in the repository:** French, Spanish, German, Portuguese (Brazilian) and Urdu are kept as `content/i18n/<code>.json` ({code, native, english, dir, numerals, messages}). `npm run languages` (`scripts/seed-languages.mjs`) saves them as drafts in `translations/{code}` (a draft that differs, edited on the page, is left alone unless `--replace`; `--pull <code>` writes it back to the file), and `--publish` publishes through `publishLanguageDraft`, the code behind the page's Publish. `--emulator` for the emulators, `--unpublish <code>` takes one off the pickers. `tests/i18n-packs.test.ts` checks every pack (publishable, placeholders, no "·" in right-to-left packs, Urdu letters rather than Arabic ي/ك).

**At run time** (`i18n/index.tsx`, `content/settings.tsx`):
- `SettingsProvider` reads the published languages with the tracks (`content/settings.json`) and registers them (`setLanguages`). `useLanguages()` feeds every picker: Onboarding, Me, the landing page (a menu once there are more than two), the lesson preview.
- **Packs load when someone picks the language**, never for everyone: cache-first by versioned path (`loadLanguagePack` in `load.ts`, a plain `fetch`, so guests still never load the SDK). Until it arrives, App shows its loading screen instead of a flash of English, for at most 6 seconds; after that English shows and the strings switch in when they come.
- The last version used is remembered (`darsi:langPacks` in localStorage), so the next start reads the pack from the cache at once instead of waiting for the settings over the network. A language that is no longer published shows English.
- **Direction has one source:** the language's `dir`. `applyLocale(lang, theme, dir)` sets `<html dir>`; before the settings arrive, `dirFor(code)` guesses from the code.
- **Fonts:** an added Latin-script language caches the Latin fonts plus the extended-Latin headings; an Arabic-script one (`fa`, `ur`, `ps`…) the Arabic fonts; other scripts use the phone's fonts (`familiesFor` in `platform/fonts.ts`). A language's own name in a picker always uses the phone's font (`.dr-lang-native`, `.dr-other-script`), so no page downloads a script's web fonts for one word.
- **Files:** built-in kits and media exist in English and Arabic only, so `kitUrl` and `videoSrc` use the English file for any other language (Hosting would otherwise answer a missing file with the app's HTML). `captionsFor` gives the language's captions, else the English ones. Read-aloud asks the phone for a voice in the language's own code.
- **Push:** a phone registers with its real language (`lang-<code>` topic). Notifications are worded in Arabic for Arabic and in English for every other language (see [Push notifications](#push-notifications)).

**Lessons in any language:** content is `LocalText` keyed by language code already. The lesson editor shows English plus the languages the author picks ("Languages" in the outline, remembered per browser; a unit's existing languages are always added). Every text, list, fill-in sentence, video, captions and kit file then has a field per language, in that language's direction. `validateUnit(u, { langs })` warns about missing translations (`missingLang`, with the language) for the shown languages, and the checks fold them into one group per language ("Français: 206 texts not translated").

## Design system and CSS

- **`@darsi/design-system`** turns `tokens.json` into CSS variables and TypeScript, and provides the components (buttons, fields, the question types, video player, lesson complete, classroom kit, and more), icons, logos and self-hosted fonts:
  - Baloo 2 / Baloo Bhaijaan 2 for display;
  - Noto Sans / Noto Sans Arabic for body text.

  There are two themes: the default light theme and **Sunlight**, a high-contrast theme for outdoor use. There is deliberately no dark mode. Reduced motion is handled globally.
- **App styles** live in `apps/web/src/styles/`, one file per area, and every class has exactly one owning file (`OWNERS` in `scripts/check-css.mjs`):
  - loaded globally (`main.tsx`): `base` (panels, tables and their phone cards, forms, pills), `layout` (rail, bottom nav), `auth`, `lesson` and `labs` (the catalogue); `bug-fab` (the Report button) and `tutor-fab` (the tutor's button) come with their startup components;
  - loaded with a lazy chunk: `admin` (the staff screens; also the `oc-` course grid and the `site-` address cards), `analytics` (charts, the `or-` report classes), `person`, `people` (adding and importing), `tabs` (`tb-`, with `admin/Tabs.tsx`), `sso` (`sso-`, the school sign-in wizard and status card, with `admin/SsoSetup.tsx`), `editor` (`ed-`, the course editor), `landing`, `licences` (also the legal pages), `school` (`sf-`, the school finder and the sign-in screen's school parts, with `auth/SchoolFinder.tsx`), `tutor` (`tu-`, the panel), `bug` (the report sheet), `tour`, `code` (`cd-`, the code steps) and `lab-kit` (everything inside the lab apps, `lb-` classes, with the first lab).

  That is the whole of `OWNERS`, one entry per file in `src/styles/` (22). A new stylesheet gets its own entry and a new class goes into its owner's list. What the check enforces: a listed class has a rule in its owner's file and is defined in no other stylesheet. It does not notice a class that nobody listed, so listing is the author's job.
- **`npm run lint:css`** enforces:
  - logical properties only (RTL);
  - design-token colours only, and every `var(--…)` must exist;
  - hard offset shadows only, with no blur or backdrop-filter;
  - no dark mode, letter-spacing or uppercase (both break Arabic);
  - nothing Chrome 87 WebViews lack: nesting, `:has()`, container queries, `@layer`;
  - no bare element selectors.
- **Breakpoints:** 600 px and 900 px only. Mobile-first. Tap targets are at least 48 px.

## Security

A static review on 2026-11-04 (3 High, 8 Medium, 12 Low) led to these changes. The report is in the session notes (`security-fixes.md`).

- **Who is who (H1):** a profile's `email` is the account's Auth email, set once. Admins find people through `findAccount` (Auth), see whether the email is verified, and are refused when several profiles claim one email. Sign-up by email sends a verification email.
- **Lesson results (H2, M1):** every progress field is typed and bounded; the summary trigger costs a fixed few reads per write and counts only published lessons, with the unit's own competencies.
- **Analytics (H3):** `collect` keeps counts only under known ids, drops events more than 10 minutes ahead and limits per address, device and instance. The analytics writes in the tutor's charge and in `recordPayment`/`recordRefund` never fail the call or the webhook.
- **Stale admin tokens (M2):** demotions and removals revoke sessions; privileged callables re-read the claims; nobody changes their own role.
- **Payments (M3, L7):** Paymob payments are matched by the signed order id; refunds only from signed or re-fetched data; a full refund takes back what it bought.
- **Passwords (M4, I1):** org admins reset only the logins the org made; `mustChangePassword` is cleared only after a real change.
- **Tutor credits (M5):** held before a call and settled after, a few calls at a time per person, `warm` limited.
- **Relay (M6, L9):** `ws` 8.22.0, an Origin header from Darsi's own site required, revoked tokens refused, sockets per instance capped and, per address, the sockets that have not finished their hello (the address is the last `X-Forwarded-For` hop, the one Google's front end adds; the earlier ones are the caller's to make up), `/warm` limited.
- **Projects (M8, L2):** a field allow-list; attachments only from this project's Storage, in the person's own folder; no SVG or HTML; the app's file names. Review screens show only such links (`admin/evidence.ts`).
- **Smaller ones:** typed profile edits and CSV cells that can't run as formulas (L1); a transactional join limit (L4); framing, HSTS and opener headers (L5); transactional seats (L8); `tutorSearch` checks the caller first (L11); drafts can't be published or created across owners (L12).
- **2026-11 audit:** the org document holds nothing private (the request's contact and reason are in `private/request`), and org admins change only its name and logo (an unbounded `country` once pushed `stats/overview` past 1 MiB; `overview()` also cuts names and countries now). Element sizes are bounded too, not only counts: competency codes in lesson results (30 of up to 40 characters), track ids and level maps on profiles (`prefs` is gone), and `createdAt` at sign-up is the server's time. `collect` and `reportBug` key their per-address limits on the visitor's address behind Cloudflare (`cf-connecting-ip`, believed only when Hosting saw a Cloudflare edge: `clientIp.ts`), not on the edge every visitor of a city shares; `reportBug` counts toward its instance budget only reports that pass the address and account limits, so one address can't switch reports off for everyone. Username logins (`@users.darsi.app`) are only for accounts organizations make: one signed up for by hand gets no profile from the rules, `joinOrg`, `requestOrg` or the summary trigger (`handMadeUsername` in `roles.ts`); refusing the sign-up itself needs a blocking function (Identity Platform). A project is reviewed by the person's teacher and the admins of the organization the person is in now, the same people who can read it, not by the organization it was sent from.
- **CSP reports:** the report-only policy names `report-uri /api/csp`, and `cspReport` logs each report (`csp`, with the directive, what was blocked and the page's path; never a query string, the policy, a user agent or a referrer) and counts it in `stats/csp` by directive (shown on `/super` → Records → Status). POST only, at most 8 KB, 20 a minute per address and 600 a minute in all; it always answers 204. The counts are summed in memory and written at most once every 5 seconds per instance, so the last reports of a burst are in the logs before they are in the count. `connect-src` names the hosts the app uses, so a new backend host must be added there or it shows up in the counts. `index.html` has no inline script of Darsi's own (`boot.js`); the four left are the legacy plugin's, with known hashes (`e2e/legacy.spec.ts` fails if another appears). Enforcing the policy needs those hashes (the plugin's own `cspHashes` export; they change only with `@vitejs/plugin-legacy`, 8.2.3 when this was written), `data:` for the plugin's module check, and a served page for the code sandbox's frame. Wait until `stats/csp` stays quiet across a release first.
- **Staff access follows the profile** (see [Security rules](#security-rules)); audit entries carry the caller's address and a request id; course access and Google Analytics settings go through audited callables; a person the organization removed cannot rejoin with a code they still know; nobody reviews their own project.
- **Hosting headers:** every path gets HSTS. Every path except Firebase's own `/__/` pages (the sign-in helper frame and popup) gets `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'`, `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin-allow-popups` (the Google popup still reports back), and a full `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only`. That policy is only reported until it has been checked on the live site: the code sandbox's srcdoc frame inherits the page's policy, and the legacy build's inline scripts need hashes.
- **Left to do:** App Check on Firestore, Storage, the callables and `collect`; Auth's email-enumeration protection and a password policy; a dedicated service account for each server part instead of the default compute account (M7); signed certificates with a public check (M1); answer keys out of public Storage (I2); enforcing the CSP; npm advisories with no fix old enough yet (L10). Lesson results are still the phone's word for real lessons: someone can claim full marks for lessons they didn't do.

## Build, checks and release

How to run a release, undo one and watch the system is in [docs/OPERATIONS.md](docs/OPERATIONS.md). This section says how the build is put together.

### Startup: six files, and the `more` chunk

The entry script and what it imports statically are six files, 224.4 KB gzipped. Rolldown splits that bundle as soon as a module is shared between startup and a lazy chunk in a way it can't merge, so two rules hold (they are also written into `screens/more.ts`, `auth/sso.ts` and App's comments, and `tests/startup-diet.test.ts` guards them):

1. **Few lazy chunks, not one per page.** The learner pages that aren't needed at once (the account screens, Me with its panels, Kit, My tracks, Labs and the lab page) are **one** lazy chunk, `screens/more.ts` (`more-*.js`, about 14 KB gzipped, precached so it opens offline). A new lazy page that shares app code with startup goes **into that chunk**, never into a chunk of its own: one chunk per page gave 21 startup files.
2. **No `import()` inside such a chunk, nor in a module startup shares with it.** App makes the call and hands the loader over, as it does with `setOrgReader`, `setLightPlayer` and `setSchoolChunks` (the school sign-in chunks). The Firebase SDK's own `import("firebase/…")` is fine: the SDK shares nothing with startup.

Staff screens are separate lazy chunks left out of the precache: by name (`LAZY_SCREENS` in `vite.config.ts`, which now also lists `Legal` and the school sign-in chunks), or found at build time (`staffOnly()`: every module under `src/admin`, `staff`, `results`, `cms` or `org` and not loaded at startup; stylesheets don't count when it decides).

`scripts/check-bundle.mjs [build dir]` measures a built app against `scripts/bundle-budget.json` and fails on more startup files or KB than it allows, on a Firebase chunk among the startup files, and on a precached file that should load on demand. When a change has to grow startup, the budget is raised in the same commit, with the reason.

### Build configuration (`apps/web/vite.config.ts`)

- **A production build needs the project's settings.** `checkBuildEnv` stops `vite build` in production mode before any work when one of `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY`, `_AUTH_DOMAIN`, `_PROJECT_ID`, `_STORAGE_BUCKET`, `_APP_ID`, `_VAPID_KEY` or `VITE_TUTOR_RELAY` is empty, or when `VITE_USE_EMULATORS` is `true`. `apps/web/.env.example` marks the same keys REQUIRED (a test keeps the two lists equal). Other modes aren't checked, nor is a build with `DARSI_LOCAL_BUILD=1`, which is how `npm run local` says its build is meant for the emulators.
- **`.env.e2e`** (committed) is what mode `e2e` reads: the public demo values of `.env.development` with `VITE_USE_EMULATORS=false`. It outranks `.env.local`, so nothing of the live project reaches the browser suite's build, which goes to `dist-e2e` and leaves `dist` alone.
- **The build carries its name** (`__DARSI_BUILD__`, read through `src/build.ts`): `DARSI_BUILD` when the release script gives one, else `<short commit>-<yyyymmdd>` (UTC), else `dev`. Usage batches and problem reports carry it.
- **`public/boot.js`** (ES5, about 1 KB compressed, `no-cache`, precached) replaces the inline scripts `index.html` had: it sends the Hosting site's own addresses (`*.web.app`, `*.firebaseapp.com`) to darsiai.org, registers the service worker the tag names (the build turns the tag into `<script src="/boot.js" data-sw="/sw.js">`, and fails if `index.html` has lost the tag), and shows "Please update your browser" only when the app has not mounted 15 seconds after the page was parsed. The built page's remaining inline scripts are the legacy plugin's four.
- **`VITE_AUDIT_SINCE`** (optional, `YYYY-MM-DD`): the day organizations' activity logs begin, shown on the log.
- **Unit tests:** `findBy…` and `waitFor` get five seconds and a test 15 (`tests/setup.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`); a failed test runs once more on CI.

### CI and release

- **`npm run verify`** (`scripts/verify.mjs`) runs, in order and stopping at the first that fails: the design system's and the app's type-checks, the functions' build, every unit test (web, design system, functions), the CSS rules, the contrast check, and two more type-checks that cover what the builds leave out, the functions' tests (`functions/tsconfig.test.json`) and the web app's e2e suite, rules tests and tool configs (`apps/web/tsconfig.tools.json`). **All ten steps fail the run** (the last two only reported until both were clean). It needs no `.env.local`, no Java and no browser. Flags add steps: `--rules` (the rules tests), `--e2e` (the browser suite), `--signed` (the signed-in browser suite on the emulators; off unless asked for) and `--bundle <dir>` (the startup budget on a built app). `firebase.json`'s functions predeploy also runs the functions' tests, so a plain `firebase deploy` stops on a failing one.
- **CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs on every push to `main` and on every pull request: `verify`; `bundle` (the startup budget, on a build of its own); `rules` (the rules tests); `audit` (`npm audit`, which reports and never blocks); `e2e` (the browser suite, skipped when `apps/web/.env.e2e` is not committed); and `e2e-signed` (the signed-in suite on the emulators, allowed to fail for now: it has never run on a runner). It has no secrets and deploys nothing. `.github/dependabot.yml` asks for security updates only (weekly version updates are off: versions are pinned by hand), grouped, for npm (`/`, `/functions`), pip (`/agent`) and the actions; it opens nothing until "Dependabot security updates" is on in the repository's settings. The agent's Python tests (`agent/tests`) are in no gate: they need its virtual environment. Still the owner's: protect `main` with `verify` and `bundle` required, and pin the workflow's actions to commit SHAs after the first green run.
- **The bundle check does not import `vite.config.ts`** (it loads Vite and reads files as it loads): it reads `LAZY_SCREENS` from the file's text and repeats the other on-demand patterns, and `tests/check-bundle.test.ts` imports the real `ON_DEMAND` and fails if the two differ. `index.html`'s plain scripts (`/boot.js`) and the stylesheet are listed with their sizes and not budgeted. Budget numbers are in KB of 1024 bytes; Vite's own report counts 1000 and reads about 2% higher.
- **`npm run deploy -- <targets> [--yes]`** (`scripts/deploy.mjs`, `scripts/lib/release.mjs`): refuses an uncommitted tree, a missing `apps/web/.env.local` or `functions/.env.<project>`, and a Hosting site `.firebaserc` doesn't map; runs `verify` (with the rules tests when rules go out); builds Hosting with `DARSI_BUILD` set and checks the startup budget and that the build holds this project's id; then deploys in a fixed order whatever order was typed: **indexes, functions, Hosting, rules**. `hosting` means both sites: the app (target `app`) and school sign-in's callback site (target `login`). A finished release is tagged `live-YYYYMMDD-n`.

## Testing

| Suite | Where | What |
|---|---|---|
| Unit (web) | `apps/web/tests/` | Lesson engine and content integrity; the staff screens rendered with mocked data hooks and callables; cloud-sync merge and push rules (certificates included: only missing ones, the rules' shapes, refusals), plus the sync hook with Firestore mocked; the notifications panel's states and the announcement composer (`notify.test.tsx`); every course (`units.test.ts`: validator, content rules, published form, kits, isolates, code steps run in MicroPython and SQLite) and its kits (`kits.test.ts`); the routes and where each role lands (`app-routes`); two tabs sharing the store (`store-tabs`); offline (pins in `darsi-saved`, `offline-sw.js`, cached course files); data rights in Me and on person pages (`accounts`); certificates as first earned (`certs`); capstones (sending, refusals, settings: `capstone*`); project sending and the review queue (`project-send`, `review*`); the lesson player and a lesson in progress (`lesson-player`); code normalisation (`code/normalize`); pictures in readings (`figures`); the licences page and its list (`licences`); the dev catalog kept out of builds (`dev-env`); course visibility in organizations (`org-courses`); the public pages (`site`: which pages, canonical and hreflang, JSON-LD, no script, links that lead somewhere, counts that hold, sources with years, the catch-all and the service worker) |
| Unit (design system) | `packages/design-system/tests/` | Components (questions, the lesson parts, the video player and its full screen) and tokens |
| Unit (functions) | `functions/test/` | Generated passwords (character classes, no look-alikes), invite-code format, usernames from any script; `pickNudge` rules across time zones and daylight saving; every message in both languages, with no "·" in Arabic; the course editor's Storage layout, permission matrix and catalog, and that the shared validator copies match the web source; tracks (`mergeTracks`, `validateTracks`, units against a tracks list, `trackConflicts`, media kept for other drafts, catalog order); the summary and the progress trigger; data rights (`accounts`), the order `createMembers` makes people in (`members`), `projectsWaiting` (`waiting`), the visitor's address behind Cloudflare (`clientIp`); billing and payments; capstone checks and the AI review's fence; the tutor (`tutor*`, `agent*`, `relay`, `explainer`), analytics and problem reports. `fakeFirestore.ts` (an in-memory Firestore and Auth) runs `recordPayment` and `tutorAsk` whole |
| Unit (course editor) | `apps/web/tests/validate.test.ts`, `content.test.ts`, `cms.test.tsx`, `cms-rollback.test.tsx`, `cms-autosave.test.tsx`, `tracks.test.tsx` | The validator (ai-1 passes with no issues; one broken copy per error rule; track and level against the saved tracks); catalog merge, offline fallbacks, cache-first units and the sign-out purge; the editor screens (list grouping, filters and search, duplicate and delete, new unit with the track and level pickers, org import with fresh ids, publish blocked by errors, save conflict, inserting a step between two, duplicating a lesson, delete then Undo, Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y, copying a lesson to another unit, saving before leaving, autosave (and the save as the page closes, on the Languages page too), a draft equal to the live version shown and marked as published, checks naming fields and taking the cursor to them, marking the right answer on an option, one language at a time with the English, the steps as cards with previous and next, preview, en/ar string parity); the Tracks page (add with a suggested id, levels and delete guarded by units in use, refused saves explained); `mergeTracks`, `useTracks`, the design-system colours from settings, `placementNext` (the stopping rule) and the undo history; `tests/mine.test.tsx`: audience and tracks, Home's track switcher, the placement check (audience, chosen tracks, stopping), Home's order from your level and the level-up |

| Unit (Labs) | `apps/web/tests/labs/` | The kit (network gradients against numeric ones, softmax, plots); the catalogue (every folder's meta is valid, ids unique, en+ar text, ladder positions); render tests of the Labs screen and `LabEmbed`; and every app's model, in `models.test.ts` and `b1`–`b4.test.ts` (for example: the perceptron converges but never on XOR, ID3 picks the textbook split, Q-learning finds the 10-step route, TOTP matches the RFC 6238 vectors, SHA-256 and RSA round trips in English and Arabic) |
| Rules | `apps/web/tests-rules/` (`security`, `access`, `tenancy` and `sso` `.rules.test.ts`; for how many pass, see the latest run) | `npm run test:rules -w @darsi/web` runs them with `@firebase/rules-unit-testing` against their own Firestore and Storage emulators (`firebase.rules-test.json`, project `demo-darsi-rules`; JDK 21). `tenancy.rules.test.ts`: stale and frozen staff tokens (and tokens minted before a suspension, stopped by the profile's `suspended` or `frozen`), the staff screens' own list queries and a 30-member batch, course access by organization and end date, a platform admin's direct writes, self-review and suspended accounts, files after a change of organization, and one table of every server-only collection against seven kinds of caller; learner documents are built with `progressDoc` and `certDoc` from `state/sync.ts`, so a change to what the app writes is tested against the rules. `sso.rules.test.ts`: school sign-in's collections and profile fields. The others: profiles, lesson results, notes, projects, the course editor, project files and logos in Storage, problem reports, the organization's private request and what its admins change (never `courses`, which no client writes), element sizes, the username-login domain, who reviews a project, certificates, and course access for university teachers |
| End-to-end | `apps/web/e2e/` (`storage.ts` stands in for Cloud Storage and serves the retired `ai-1` from `content/retired/` as `npm run seed` publishes a unit, since the app ships none) | Desktop Chrome and Pixel 7, 16 tests each: guest onboarding, guests never loading the Firebase SDK, Me without onboarding, the sign-in screen loading Firebase on demand, the Arabic RTL switch, a full lesson through to the kit download, the video fallback, Labs (cards open apps, the playground trains then pauses, labs aren't precached and "Save all labs" works offline), offline after the first visit with its own fonts (English and Arabic), the service worker leaving `/__/` alone and leaving the public pages to the server (no app code, no Arabic fonts on English pages, no sideways scroll), no Arabic fonts on English pages, and a code step in the real sandbox (`code.spec.ts`). `legacy.spec.ts` (both Chromium projects) takes the branch an old phone takes, so the build for Chrome 61–104 loads through SystemJS and renders, and checks that the built page has no inline script but the legacy plugin's. `smoke.spec.ts` runs four tests on WebKit (iPhone 13): onboarding, a lesson step, Arabic right to left and the sign-in page; where WebKit isn't downloaded they are skipped, except on CI. 40 tests in all |
| End-to-end, signed in | `apps/web/e2e/signed/*.signed.ts`, run by `scripts/e2e-signed.mjs` (`npm run e2e:signed -w @darsi/web`; `playwright.signed.config.ts`) | 15 tests in three files (`accounts` 2, `admin` 4, `school` 9), one browser at a time in file order, against the Emulator Suite on private ports (project `demo-darsi-e2e`; Auth 9199, Firestore 8185, Functions 5101, Storage 9299, the app 4181, the stand-in school provider 4796), so `npm run local` can run beside it. The app's build knows only the Firestore port; the tests move its Auth, Functions and Storage requests from the usual ports to the run's own in the browser (`remapPorts` in `e2e/signed/fixtures.ts`). The runner builds the functions, starts the emulators, seeds a platform admin and two organizations (claims and profiles in agreement), turns school sign-in's switch on, builds and serves the app, runs Playwright with service workers blocked, and stops what it started (`--keep`, `--no-build`, `--no-tests`; anything after `--` goes to Playwright). Covered: sign-up and joining by invite link; an admin adding people, changing a role, removing someone, and another organization's admin refused; tabs in the address with Back and Forward; school sign-in from the wizard to turned on, a student's one-time test link (Darsi says what it would do and makes nothing; a used link refuses; the admin's Test step shows the result), the admin letting a removed person back in, after which the school's sign-in opens the same account, a new person signing in as a student and again into the same account, a removed person refused, connecting from Me; Arabic at 375 px without sideways scroll. It adds its own `SSO_KEYS` to `functions/.secret.local` for the run and puts the file back. `npm run verify -- --signed` and CI's `e2e-signed` job run the same |
| Screenshots (not a test) | `apps/web/e2e/signed/shots.signed.ts` | A visual pass over the staff screens' tabs and school sign-in: full-page PNGs named `<name>-<lang>-<width>.png`, in English at 1280 px and Arabic at 375 px (the wizard also in Arabic at 1280 px). Skipped unless `SHOTS=1`, so the normal signed run never takes them. Run it on a kept stack: `node scripts/e2e-signed.mjs --keep --no-tests`, then in `apps/web` `SHOTS=1 SHOTS_DIR=<folder> E2E_SIGNED_STATE=../../.emulator-tmp/e2e-signed/state.json npx playwright test -c playwright.signed.config.ts shots` (default folder `test-results/shots`). Each run makes an organization of its own and removes the one an earlier run made, so it can be repeated on the same emulators and never touches the two organizations the other signed files use |
| CSS lint | `scripts/check-css.mjs` | Brand, RTL and old-WebView rules |

The Firebase Emulator Suite needs **JDK 21+** (on this Windows machine also `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=-Djdk.net.unixdomain.tmpdir=C:\Users\Public`; see docs/FIREBASE.md). The course editor's backend was checked end to end against the emulators on 2026-10-26: 42 checks covering the rules, publish, versions, org-only reads, media upload limits, unpublish and delete. Tracks and levels add 24 more: who may save, the public settings file, stale saves, validation, a level-5 unit in an added track, refusals that would strand units, and removal once no unit uses a track. Languages add 26: draft rules, who may publish, problems refused, the public pack, settings keeping tracks and languages side by side, versions, no client uploads, unpublish and delete. Those were scratch scripts; the rules tests above are the committed, repeatable check of the rules.

## Performance budget

| Measure | Now | Target (brand book) |
|---|---|---|
| Precache on install | ~1.3 MB, 42 files, ~418 KB gzipped (measured 2026-11-07; 50 entries in Workbox's list, 8 of them listed twice). The biggest: the entry chunk (164 KB gzipped), `placement.json` (72 KB), the `i18n` chunk (46 KB), the light-lesson player (26 KB), the main CSS (14 KB) and the lab CSS (11 KB). No lab, code-step, Darsi Tiny, light-pack, staff or first-visit chunk is in it | ≤ 2 MB including fonts |
| Fonts, kept per language | ~76 KB (English) or ~207 KB (Arabic, which needs Latin too); every script was precached before (~237 KB) | |
| Startup JS | **224.4 KB gzipped in six files** (measured 2026-11-11 on a scratch production build; no Firebase SDK): the entry 172.4 KB, `i18n` 46.8 KB (React, the design system, i18n), then `dist` (`@capacitor/core`), `preload-helper`, `app-config` and `rolldown-runtime`. `scripts/check-bundle.mjs` fails a build over `scripts/bundle-budget.json` (8 files, 225.9 KB), or one whose startup files hold a Firebase chunk (see [Build, checks and release](#build-checks-and-release)) | within the budget file |
| Firebase SDK, signed-in only | ~210 KB gzipped (`firebase`), plus ~21 KB (`firebase-extra`: Functions, Storage, Messaging + Installations) for admin actions, uploads and notifications | |
| Push handlers in the service worker | ~1.4 KB gzipped (`push-sw.js`, no SDK) | |
| Course editor, staff only | ~56 KB gzipped JS (`Cms`, with its strings) + ~3.4 KB CSS, not precached | |
| First-visit chunks, not precached | Report a problem ~13.6 KB gzipped (+0.8 KB CSS), the tour ~11.4 KB (+1.5 KB CSS), the tutor's panel ~6.8 KB, `/licences` ~8.4 KB | |
| Labs | 109 apps in 111 chunks (with the shared `lab-agents` and `lab-tiny`): ~1.1 MB, ~481 KB gzipped in all, most 1–7 KB each (`tutor` 47 KB), not precached. "Save all labs" fetches ~509 KB gzipped (`labs-manifest.json`: the labs plus the sandbox and Darsi Tiny's code they share). The kit's shared chunks and the lab CSS are precached. The catalogue's metadata (titles and summaries, en and ar) is in the startup bundle | |
| Code runtimes, first lesson with code | Python 0.29 MB or SQLite 0.34 MB gzipped, once, cached in `darsi-lib` | |
| Darsi Tiny, first app/tutor in one language | ~2.75–2.77 MB gzipped model, once per job/language, cached in `darsi-lib`; the worker/page chunks are shared and not precached | ≤ 3 MB per model |
| Main CSS | ~14.2 KB gzipped (82 KB) | |
| Code steps, first lesson with code | `CodeSteps` ~9 KB and `sandbox` ~16 KB gzipped, before the runtime | |
| Light-lesson technology packs | 10–14 KB gzipped each (code, data, iot, network, opt, science), only for lessons that draw them | |
| One lesson | ~19 KB of JSON (both languages); kits and media download on demand | ≤ 300 KB |

Measure with a private build (`npx vite build --outDir <a folder outside the repo>` in `apps/web`; other sessions use `apps/web/dist`): the `precache` line gives the entries, the URLs in the generated `sw.js` the files (some are listed twice), and the entry's static imports (`import … from "./x.js"` in `index-*.js`, followed through those files) the startup files. Rebuild the design system first if its sources changed: the build reads its `dist`.

## Known gaps and roadmap

- **Videos** come from Lesson Studio (docs/CURRICULUM.md). Open there: its `convert.ts` knows only the 12 pack lessons' order, so newer videos end without a "Next: …" card; the students' videos use the narrator style written for teachers; arrow icons don't flip in Arabic; a seek past a quick check in the light player skips it (the MP4 player stops).
- **Security review:** the static review of the rules, functions and client was done on 2026-11-04 and the rules have their tests (see [Security](#security) and [Testing](#testing)). Left: a scan of the deployed site (`H:\htdocs\pentest-lab`), and the items under "Left to do" in [Security](#security).
- **School sign-in:** a personal account opened through the school can still change its password or email, or delete itself, from the client SDK; its staff alerts (a client secret about to expire, endpoints that moved, a full school) are pushed only to admins who turned staff alerts on, and one that could not be sent is not logged; nothing here was tried against a real provider or on the live project yet (docs/FIREBASE.md §10).
- **Members and staff screens:** when the audit write at the end of a `bulkMembers` call fails, the call fails although the people were changed, and for a password reset the new passwords are then lost and must be reset again. The local store's "couldn't be opened" card and the "phone is full" notice have their words in the code, in English and Arabic only, and don't show an organization's name or logo. `orgAudit` names who acted; the person an entry is about shows as an id in its details. A phone's own Back gesture is not held by the leave question.
- **The owner's to decide** (left as the code does now, after the 2026-11 audit):
  - School sign-in's limits at school and university scale: whether 300 starts per address per 10 minutes is right for a school behind one address, how many CDN nodes a school's traffic really spreads over (the hop limits assume one), and a limit in front of the functions for floods from many addresses.
  - After "a different provider": members an admin made by email who only ever used the school's sign-in must now use a password they never used (`sso-connect-first`); someone removed whose link was to their own account (`self`) is made a new account by the new provider; with pairwise subjects, a client change saved as "the same people" refuses every active school-made account (`sso-connect-first`).
  - A school-made account suspended and then deleted by its admin leaves a tombstone blocked for good (no account to restore, and `allowBack` takes only removed ones); a school-made account deleted without being removed first has no row on the removed list to let back in from. A school-made teacher with no pupils and no learning is still replaced and deleted on link, and the confirmation doesn't say so.
  - Course access: a pinned grant outlives the teacher's membership and checks no role (see [Course access for university teachers](#course-access-for-university-teachers)).
  - Project drafts: a never-sent draft from before scopes goes once to whoever opens its step first on that device; a typed draft keeps the account's whole local copy on a shared device and repeats the "kept" notice at every sign-out until it is sent or emptied (no Send first / Sign out anyway question).
  - Seats bring no tutor credits (docs/SUPER.md §1). Complimentary days given on a live paid subscription replace what is left of its allowance with a full one.
  - New learner-facing strings outside the translatable dictionary (school sign-in's, the staff kit's) show in English in added languages.
  - A platform admin's members table asks before every role change, also student ↔ teacher.
- **Operations:** backups, point-in-time recovery and a tested restore, the log-based alerts, retention periods and the legal texts are the owner's to set (docs/OPERATIONS.md). The tutor's conversations on Agent Runtime are created without an expiry (`newSession` in `agent.ts`: nobody has checked that the deployed ADK accepts one), so `scripts/purge-agent-sessions.mjs` is their only clean-up and nothing schedules it; an erasure reaches only the agent named in `config/tutor`, not an older one.
- **Content and languages:** the nine tracks, their code exercises and the students' courses fitted to school stages are in place; most primary and secondary courses are unpublished for now (51 of 96 live). Next: French, Swahili, Urdu and Hausa.
- **Certificates in the cloud:** cloud sync writes and reads `users/{uid}/certs` (see [Cloud sync](#cloud-sync)); the Firestore rules for it must be live before (or with) the app, or the reads and writes are refused (lessons still sync). Left: /super/certificates works from the summaries and the catalog, so it doesn't list a certificate whose course is unpublished; a person's page names such a course from its certificate only when it has lesson results to group (results from before `unitId` was recorded, in a course no longer offered, aren't grouped).
- **Native apps:** Android and iOS through Capacitor (see docs/NATIVE.md). The native shells don't register the web service worker, so they need native push (FCM through a Capacitor plugin) for reminders.
